- **Remarks:**

  **Three real bugs were found and fixed by actually running the code, not just reading it** — exactly the category `superpowers:verification-before-completion` and this repo's own log protocol both exist for: (1) the home-port fallback branch in `/query` was dead code (P3.1, see above); (2) `language_ingress`'s new rung-tagging code indexed a lookup table with a key (`"passthrough"`) the table didn't have, so a plain English query — the majority case — reported the ingress span as `status: "failed"` with `error_detail: "'passthrough'"` while still answering correctly (the exception boundary swallows it); caught by watching the reasoning trace in the browser, not by unit tests, which never exercised the new rung-tagging path; (3) the onboarding wizard's own profile-cache staleness (P3.4, see above). All three are fixed at the root, not patched at the call site.

  **`Persona`/`stakeholder_persona` CI guard 2 was tripped twice while writing this, both false positives from field-naming, not real leaks**, and both are worth recording so the next person doesn't spend time on a similar one: a schema field literally named `persona` in `orca/auth/schemas.py` (guard 2 scans all of `orca/auth/` for a bare `persona[:=]`) — renamed to `default_persona`, matching the DB column and `UserOut`; and, separately, the *comment explaining that renaming* itself contained the literal substring `persona:` inside backticks, which the regex has no way to distinguish from real code — reworded without spelling out the trigger sequence. `scripts/verify_ci_guards.py` is green on all 4 guards as of this entry.

  **P3.7 and P3.8's remaining gaps are the two genuinely irreducible blockers in this phase**, not scope cut for time: no code change makes an AI agent a native Kannada/Bengali/Marathi/Tamil speaker, and no code change produces a real Bhashini latency measurement without a real Bhashini credential. Both are called out explicitly rather than the log going quiet on them, per principle 1 (no fabricated values) applied to this log's own claims, not just the product's.

  **P3.4's conversational elicitation and P3.12's full-app string coverage are scope reductions, not blockers** — both are buildable, both would touch either the query-answering prompt path (P3.4) or dozens of call sites across every page (P3.12), and both were left at "the infrastructure exists and is proven on a first surface" rather than expanded further in this session. Flagged here so they don't quietly read as finished.

  **Every change was checked against the live stack, not just imported**: local Postgres (`orca-postgres-1`, migrations applied through 007) and Redis (`orca-redis-1`) were already running; the backend was started fresh at least four times over the session to pick up code changes, and the frontend's own `next dev` hot-reloaded throughout. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint app` are both clean on the whole frontend tree as of this entry. The shared `agent@orca.test` account was used throughout (never a second account — CLAUDE.md's rule); its `default_persona`/`language` were reset back to `fisherman`/`en` at the end of this session so the next agent or dev finds it in a known, sensible state, with a registered vessel, a home port and one saved location left in place as artifacts of the verification itself, not cleaned up (they are harmless and demonstrate the features working).

### [2026-09-23] P3.8 — Bhashini credentials added mid-session — LIVE-VERIFIED, 2 more bugs found and fixed

- **Implements:** orca_final §14.1, P3.8.
- **By:** Claude (Sonnet 5) — Dev added real `BHASHINI_USER_ID`/`BHASHINI_ULCA_API_KEY`/`BHASHINI_INFERENCE_API_KEY` to `.env` and asked to verify the integration works before anything else.
- **Files:** `backend/orca/agents/bhashini.py`, `backend/orca/agents/language.py`.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **Done-when test:** `python -m orca.agents.bhashini` (with `orca.db.engine` imported first so `load_dotenv` runs) → live round trip: `nmt("Is it safe to go to sea today?", "en", "ta")` returns real Tamil; that Tamil fed to `tts(..., "ta")` — actually run as `tts(<english>, "en")` then `asr(<that audio>, "en")` — returns the transcript `"Is it safe to go to sea today?"` byte-for-byte; `transliterate()` returns `"intru kadalukkuch selvathu badugapanatha?"` for the Tamil. All four assert-checked in the file's own `__main__` block, which now runs a live check instead of only the credential gate when `BHASHINI_*` is set. `python scripts/verify_ci_guards.py` → all 4 green throughout.
- **Remarks:**

  **Two bugs found only because real credentials made a live call possible, neither visible from reading the code:**

  1. **TTS returned a bare `500 Internal Server Error` with no JSON body** — the Dhruva inference endpoint requires a `"gender"` field inside the TTS task's `config` object, undocumented in anything the config-call response itself surfaces (the ASR/NMT/transliteration tasks need no such field). Added `gender: str = "female"` as a parameter to `bhashini.tts()`, defaulting to a value, not a claim about who is speaking.
  2. **`transliterate()`'s return type was wrong** — ULCA's transliteration response's `target` field is a **list** of candidate romanizations (`["kadal paathukaappaanathu"]`), unlike NMT's `target`, which is a plain string. The function's own type hint (`-> str`) was violated at runtime; every caller (P3.5's romanized-input detection, P3.11's romanized SMS variant) would have received a Python list where a string was promised the first time either ran. Fixed to index `[0]`, the service's top pick.

  **A third bug was found live, in production-shaped text, not a synthetic test string, and it is the one that mattered most:** the P3.8 masking scheme committed in the previous log entry — bracket placeholders `⟦0⟧`, flagged in its own comment as "not verified against a live NMT call" — came back from Bhashini as a bare `0` with the brackets stripped. `"GO: All Parameters Within Safe Operational Limits"` translated to `"0: பாதுகாப்பான செயல்பாட்டு..."` — the verdict word a marine safety answer leads with, silently replaced by a meaningless digit, on every single translated answer, every time. Found by running one real Tamil query end to end and reading the actual response rather than trusting the unit-level round-trip. Fixed by trying several alternative placeholder shapes directly against the live model before picking one: an all-caps, punctuation-free run (`ZKEEPZ0Z`, and a letter variant `ZKEEPZAZ`) survived byte-for-byte, including three in one sentence with Tamil's own word-reordering around them, while a bracketed or all-digit-looking form did not and a dictionary-word-shaped one got semantically translated instead of copied. `_mask_protected_terms`/`_unmask_protected_terms` in `orca/agents/language.py` now use this scheme; re-ran the identical live query afterward (bypassing the query cache with `fresh=1`, since the first confirming re-run served the pre-fix cached answer and looked like the bug persisted) and confirmed `"GO: <Tamil text>"` — the verdict intact.

  **`txt-lang-detection` (TLD) is the one Bhashini task NOT confirmed working, even now**: `POST /model/search {"task": "txt-lang-detection"}` shows a real model (Bhashini-IIITH Textual Language Detection) exists, but `getModelsPipeline` — the config call every other task goes through via this integration's default pipeline (`_PIPELINE_ID`) — rejects that `taskType` with `400 TaskType is not valid !`. The model's own `inferenceEndPoint.callbackUrl` in the search result is empty, meaning it wants a different integration path this default pipeline doesn't expose. Not chased further: `language.detect_language_with_bhashini` already treats any exception from this call as "fall back to the script-only result" (P3.5's own designed degrade path), so the failure mode is silent and correct, not a crash — documented in `detect_language`'s own docstring rather than left as a mystery for the next person.

  **Not done in this pass, and worth doing next**: the P3.8 Done-when's own latency table (p50/p95 per service per language, 20 clips) — now actually producible with real credentials, where it wasn't in the previous entry, but a measurement pass, not a correctness fix, and Dev's own instruction this turn was "check if that's working before fixing any other left out parts." `voice_routes.py`'s `/voice/transcribe`/`/voice/speak` HTTP endpoints were not separately re-exercised this pass (only the `orca.agents.bhashini`/`orca.agents.voice` functions directly) — same code path, not re-verified through FastAPI's own request/response cycle.

### [2026-09-23] P3.8 — all 10 core languages exercised, 1 sample each (Dev's own sizing, not the plan's 20)

- **Implements:** orca_final §14.1, P3.8's coverage claim.
- **By:** Claude (Sonnet 5) — Dev: "test all 10 languages we mentioned (don't use 20 audios per language, just 1 or 2 is fine)."
- **Files:** none changed by the test itself; see the fix below.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **Done-when test:** one scratch script, `bhashini.nmt("Is it safe to go to sea today?", "en", <lang>)` for all 9 non-English core languages, then `bhashini.tts(<translated text>, <lang>)` → `bhashini.asr(<that audio>, <lang>)` for each, `en` included via its own TTS→ASR round trip (10 languages total). Not committed to the repo — a one-off measurement, not a fixture or a test the suite should run against a paid external API on every CI run.
- **Remarks:**

  **NMT: 10/10 languages succeeded**, 0.9–2.2 s each. Every translation reads as a real, grammatical answer to the source sentence in that language (not spot-checked by a native speaker — same honest gap as everywhere else in this build that touches a language nobody on the team reads).

  **TTS→ASR round trip: 7/10 succeeded** (en, ta, hi, te, ml, kn, bn) at 1.5–2.7 s each, transcript matching the spoken text (Dravidian-script languages show minor ASR spelling drift on the round trip — e.g. Tamil "பாதுகாப்பானதா" heard back as "பாதுகாப்ப ஆானதாக" — an ASR artifact on synthesized speech, not a translation or masking defect, and not chased further since it doesn't affect the text pipeline these three components exist to support).

  **TTS failed for mr, gu and or — a live Bhashini-side condition, not a bug in this codebase.** All three route to the identical shared service (`ai4bharat/indic-tts-coqui-indo_aryan-gpu--t4`) that **also serves Hindi successfully** on the same call shape, so the model itself is reachable; specifically the mr/gu/or language configs on it are not responding. Confirmed not a cold-start latency issue: retried at 20 s timeout (still failed, both languages, ~20–21 s each) and retried mr again as a fresh call (failed again at the ordinary 3 s timeout) — a persistent condition on Bhashini's infrastructure as measured right now, not a transient blip this session's own load caused. ASR's *config* resolves fine for all three (`ai4bharat/conformer-multilingual-indo_aryan-gpu--t4`) — untested with real audio since there is no synthesized clip to feed it, but the config-level failure is isolated to TTS. This is the exact scenario `orca/agents/voice.py`'s existing two-rung design (Bhashini first, local MMS-TTS fallback) exists for, and it engages correctly: `text_to_speech(..., "mr")` still returns real audio, via `rung="mms_tts"`, once Bhashini's 3 s timeout is spent — confirmed live, though the very first call for `mr` paid a one-time HuggingFace model-download cost the plan's own docstring already describes as lazy-loaded on first use.

  **Corrects the plan's own `Now:` line.** P3.8's text lists "8 core languages with full voice: en, ta, hi, te, ml, kn, bn, mr" and treats only gu/or as "text-only until verified." Measured today, **mr's TTS does not work over Bhashini right now** — it is presently in the same state as gu/or (text-only via Bhashini, voiced only through the local fallback), not the 8th confirmed-voiced language the plan currently claims. Whether that is temporary (a model warm-up state on Bhashini's side that clears on its own) or persistent is unknown from one measurement; re-check before relying on it for a live demo, and do not assume `mr` TTS will be available on stage without re-testing that day.

  **Transliteration re-confirmed for a second language** (Hindi: "क्या आज समुद्र में जाना सुरक्षित है" → "kyaa aaj samudra mein jaanaa surakshit haye"), on top of Tamil from the previous entry — same shape, same result quality.

This is recorded on that confirmation, not re-verified here — per the plan's own §1 ("a point is done when its Done-when test passes, run by a human, not when the code compiles"), a native-speaker sign-off is structurally not something an agent can self-certify, so there is no code-level check to run for this point. `detect_distress_signal()` itself was already exercised against every language's phrase list in the earlier pass and is unchanged in this one.

  **P3.4 — DONE.** Traced every field the point names (home port, vessel class/length/engine, crew, departure hour, range offshore, phone + SMS consent, reading comfort, units) against the actual tree to find which ones the field rule ("name the answer that changes if we know this; if none, do not ask") currently permits asking for. Only two have a live consumer reachable from the conversational `/query` path today: `vessel_class` (risk-threshold deltas in `risk_assessment.py`) and `home_port` (position fallback, P3.1) — both already prompted by the existing `profile_prompts.py`/`ProfilePrompt.tsx` pair from the earlier session. Vessel draft (`draft_m`) was checked as a candidate third field — it does drive `voyage.py`'s UKC/shallow-water check — but that check is only reachable through the separate, unauthenticated `/api/voyage-plan` REST endpoint the `/voyage` page calls directly, never through `/query`'s `matched_intent_rows`, so there is nowhere in the conversational flow today where asking for it would change an answer; not added. The other five (vessel length/engine beyond draft, crew, departure hour, range offshore, phone + SMS consent, reading comfort, units) were checked the same way — grepped for any existing reader of `crew_size`, `length_m`, `engine_count`, a units preference, a reading-comfort flag, an SMS-consent flag — and none exists anywhere in the backend or frontend. Building prompts for them now would mean either fabricating a consumer (a control that looks like it does something and doesn't — principle 1's other direction) or quietly building phase-4/5 features (Small Vessel view, crew-aware thresholds, quiet hours) ahead of their own points, which §1's phase-gating forbids without a `PARALLEL-OK` marker neither carries. Left correctly unasked; each becomes askable once its own later point lands. **What changed this session:** both existing prompts' `question` text (and the vessel-class options' labels) now run through `translate_from_english` the same way P3.6's alerts and P3.11's SMS do, with the identical `except: fall back to English` discipline `main.py`'s language-change confirmation already uses — before this, the prompt text was English-only regardless of the asker's `users.language`, which contradicted P3.3's own "takes effect across UI chrome, answers, voice and alerts."

  **P3.12 — DONE.** Read every component the `/ask` page renders (`ChatTurn`, `Disclosures`, `ProfilePrompt`, `PersonaCorrection`, `FormattedResponse`) plus `/map` and `/login`, and keyed every remaining hardcoded UI string: agent-trace label, the backend-unreachable error card, the "following on from N messages" / "context expired" lines, the map-focus button (which also needed `lib/queryIntent.ts`'s `INTENT_LABEL` table converted from fixed strings to i18n keys — `intentLabel()` now takes the caller's `t()`), the English-translation panel, sources & provenance, follow-up chips, the LLM-off re-run control and its notice, the weather panel (title, all four readout labels, Active/Clear/None), the refusal card's three outcome headings plus "Ask about"/"Try", the profile prompt's two buttons, persona-correction's four labels and both its error strings, the language-switch label and its error string, `FormattedResponse`'s "Sector:" label, `/map`'s `sr-only` heading (reused `nav.map`), and every string on `/login` (reusing `common.signIn`/`common.createAccount` where the copy was identical rather than duplicating keys). 68 new keys (`en.json`: 115 → 183). `useT()` gained `{var}` substitution (`translate(lang, key, vars)`) for the five strings that needed a count, a map-focus label or a source's agent name — the module's own "no i18n framework" stance still holds; this is a ~10-line regex `.replace`, not a formatting engine. All 68 new keys machine-translated into the 8 non-English dictionaries plus Odia via a one-off script calling the same `translate_from_english` seam the app itself uses (612 calls, 2 transient failures, both succeeded on retry). **A real bug found translating them:** the first pass sent the `{n}`/`{label}`/`{agent}` placeholders through Bhashini NMT unmasked, and the model paraphrased or dropped the braces in most of the 45 language/key pairs that carried one — the exact failure class `language.py`'s own `_mask_protected_terms` docstring documents for IMBL/PFZ/numbers, just never applied to *these* placeholders because this script is outside that module. Fixed by masking `{var}` tokens with the same live-verified `ZKEEPZnZ` scheme before translating and unmasking after; 44/45 survived, and the one that didn't (`bn.chatTurn.viewOnMap` — Bhashini transliterated the mask token itself into Bengali script instead of copying it verbatim) falls back to the English string for that one key in that one language, same disclosed-fallback discipline as everywhere else translation can fail. `node scripts/check-i18n.mjs` → 9 dictionaries × 183 keys, all matching `en.json`. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint app` both clean. **Live-verified in the browser**, not just by the key-diff script: signed in as `agent@orca.test`, asked a real query, switched to Tamil via the "Speak to me in…" chip, and read back the whole rendered answer card — nav rail, "Ask ORCA" header, "You", "Map focused on local sea conditions" (confirming both the new key *and* the `intentLabel` refactor), "English translation:", "Sources & provenance", "Follow-up", "Re-run this without any LLM", "I'm actually a…" with Navigator/Researcher/Authority, "Speak to me in…", Weather/Wave height/Wind speed/Lightning/Cyclone alert with Clear/None — all rendered in Tamil, screenshotted. Switched back to English afterward and confirmed via reload (persisted `PUT /api/profile/language`, same as P3.13's mechanism) — the shared account's language and persona were left as found (`en`/`fisherman`). **Not covered, by explicit scope, not oversight:** `/alerts` does not exist in the tree (it is P4.11, phase 4) so the Done-when's mention of it is vacuously satisfied; `/voyage`, `/ops`, `/trends`, `/data`, `/reasoning`, `/zones`' and `/watches`' own already-translated surfaces were not re-touched; example query-suggestion text (`EXAMPLES` in `ask/page.tsx`, `FOLLOW_UPS` and the out-of-scope redirect chips in `ChatTurn.tsx`/`Disclosures.tsx`) stays English deliberately — these are query *content* a native speaker would need to phrase idiomatically, not chrome, and the plan's own list for this point ("nav labels, buttons, severity tokens, empty and error states") doesn't reach them.

Phase 4 is next; P4.0 (the demo script) gates the rest of it per its own point text.

Diagnosed and fixed 11 of the 12; the 12th is real but unrelated to any of this. **Files:** `tests/unit/test_language.py`, `tests/e2e/test_graph.py`, `tests/unit/test_channel_renderers.py`, `tests/unit/test_notifications.py`, `tests/unit/test_risk_assessment.py`, `tests/unit/test_voice.py`.

  - **6 in `test_language.py` + 1 in `test_graph.py` — genuinely Bhashini-caused.** These tests register a mock/local translation backend (`_EchoBackend`) or expect the credential-gate `RuntimeError`, and assert on that specific behaviour — but `_translate_with_rung` now tries the live Bhashini rung first (real credentials, P3.8), so the assertions saw a real Tamil/Hindi translation instead. Fixed by patching `orca.agents.bhashini.nmt` to raise for the duration of each of these tests, so they exercise the rung they're named for (registered-backend / no-backend) regardless of which rung would win outside the test. Product behaviour was never wrong; only the test's assumption about which rung answers first was stale.
  - **`test_channel_renderers.py::test_non_gsm7_vernacular_text_falls_back_to_the_encodable_composed_line` — not Bhashini at all, a stale test from before P3.11 shipped in this same repo.** It asserted the *old* pre-P3.11 behaviour (non-GSM-7 text drops to an ASCII composed line) against code whose own docstring now says "NEVER dropped... nothing dropped is the point's own Done-when." Rewritten (renamed `test_non_gsm7_vernacular_text_encodes_as_ucs2_with_nothing_dropped`) to assert the real, already-shipped P3.11 contract: UCS-2 encoding, ≤67-char parts, nothing lost on rejoin.
  - **`test_risk_assessment.py::test_generate_alert_payload_non_english_raises_not_silently_returns_english` — also not Bhashini, a stale test from the same day P3.6 widened the verified-language set.** It called with `language="ta"`, expecting the unverified-language `NotImplementedError` — but P3.6 (earlier the same day) moved `ta` into `_ALERT_VERIFIED_LANGUAGES`, so it now correctly takes the translate path instead (and then hit the live-Bhashini-vs-no-backend issue above on top). Fixed by pointing the test at `kn`, which is still outside the verified set — restores what the test was actually protecting (an unverified language raises rather than silently shipping mislabelled English).
  - **`test_voice.py::test_real_tts_then_asr_round_trip_produces_a_nonempty_transcript` — genuinely Bhashini-caused, plus a cache trap.** Named and skip-gated for the *local* rungs (faster-whisper / MMS-TTS); live Bhashini now answers both legs first. Fixed by patching `BhashiniTtsBackend.speak` / `BhashiniAsrBackend.transcribe` to raise. A second issue surfaced on the first re-run: `text_to_speech`'s disk cache (`data/tts_cache/`) had already persisted a Bhashini-rung clip for this exact (text, language) key from before the patch, and disk-cache hits are unconditionally tagged `rung="bhashini"` — so the patched backends were never even reached. Also bypassed `_tts_cache` (in-memory) and `_load_from_disk` for this test.
  - **`test_notifications.py::test_crossing_fires_once_and_a_second_identical_poll_is_silent` — not Bhashini, not even new: a pre-existing connection-pooling hazard the fixture's own comment already half-documents.** `run_poll_cycle` takes Postgres's session-scoped Sentinel advisory lock and releases it in a `finally`, but this test's own `db.commit()` (needed, per its comment, "so run_poll_cycle sees its row in the same connection") can cause the ORM `Session` to hand back a *different* pooled physical connection for the release than the one that took the lock — the unlock call then runs on the wrong connection, and the real lock-holder connection sits idle-but-locked in the pool, silently blocking `try_sentinel_lock` for every run after it (this session's own earlier isolated run of this exact test needed `pg_terminate_backend` by hand to unblock, twice, before the cause was clear). Not a code bug worth changing `sentinel_runtime.py`'s connection handling for on this pass — fixed at the test-fixture level instead: `db`'s setup now terminates any backend still holding this specific advisory lock (`SENTINEL_LOCK_CLASS`/`SENTINEL_LOCK_OBJ`) before each test, making the test self-recovering instead of dependent on a previous run's cleanup succeeding.
  - **`test_ocean_analytics.py::test_wind_anomaly_carries_its_baseline_or_names_the_gap` — real, unrelated, not fixed.** Asserts `era5_historical_<port>_30d.json` exists on disk for Gujarat/Odisha/Andaman; only Thoothukudi's is cached (`data/tier1/weather/`), and no refresh script for it exists anywhere in `scripts/` — this is a `data/` fixture-generation gap (P5.1/P5.12 territory: fetching real ERA5 reanalysis for the other ports, not a code path this session touched or that Bhashini affects). Left failing and disclosed rather than faked — synthesizing the missing baseline data to make the assertion pass would be exactly the fabricated-value violation principle 1 forbids.

  `python -m pytest tests/` now: **710 passed, 1 failed (the ERA5 gap above), 2 skipped** — up from 699/12/2, the one remaining failure named and explained rather than silently reappearing in a future log entry as a mystery. `scripts/verify_ci_guards.py` still 4/4.

### [2026-09-23] Migration 007 applied to local DB; variable-font weight arrays removed from the root layout — FIX

- **Implements:** two runtime failures Dev hit before the stale-data delete: `sentinel_subscriptions.escalation does not exist` in `sentinel_runtime.run_poll_cycle`, and `next/font/google queries have exactly one entry` returning HTTP 500 on every dev page load.
- **By:** Claude (Opus 5), Dev's report: "also before we delete, [backend traceback] and frontend error [turbopack font error] we need to solve"
- **Files:** `frontend/app/layout.tsx` (the backend half was a DB state fix, not a code change)
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted, per Dev's standing instruction)
- **Done-when test:** `list_enabled_watches(db)` → 2 enabled watches (the exact call that raised). `pytest -q tests/unit` → **700 passed, 1 skipped**. Frontend: dev server on :3000 went **500 → 200**, all 11 font variables present on `<html>`, `tsc --noEmit` exit 0, `eslint app/layout.tsx` exit 0, `next build` exit 0.
- **Remarks:**

  **Neither of these touched `data/`, so the delete register is unaffected.**

  **The backend failure was not a code defect — the database was one migration behind.** `infra/db/007_vessel_operational.sql` adds `sentinel_subscriptions.escalation`, three `vessels` operational columns, `users.active_vessel_id`/`quiet_hours` and the `saved_locations` table; `schema_migrations` showed 001–006 applied and nothing since 2026-09-20. Running `infra/db/migrate.sh` applied it; the file is idempotent (`ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` / `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`) and purely additive, and every object it declares was verified present afterwards. Worth recording because the failure *looked* like a model/schema drift bug and the fix was operational — the log entry exists so the next person who sees this traceback checks `schema_migrations` before editing a model.

  **The second traceback in that report was fallout, not a second bug, but it hides evidence.** Once the `SELECT` aborted the transaction, `release_sentinel_lock()` in the `finally` path raised `InFailedSqlTransaction`, which is what surfaced in the logs — a cleanup error masking the real cause. It resolves with the cause and was not otherwise touched, but the cleanup path running on a possibly-failed transaction is a latent evidence-destroyer and deserves its own ticket.

  **The frontend fix is the class, not the case.** The 500 named only `fraunces`, and the obvious fix is to change that one declaration. But 9 of the 11 families in `layout.tsx` are variable fonts and 8 of them carried the same misuse: `weight` as an array, which `next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/02-components/font.md` documents as being for *non*-variable families ("An array of 3 possible values for a non variable font"). Fraunces broke and the others did not purely because of axis count — Fraunces carries four axes (SOFT, WONK, opsz, wght) so a pinned weight makes Google emit several `src` entries and Turbopack accepts exactly one, while the Noto families have two (wdth, wght) and squeak through *today*. Axis counts come from font data that ships with `next` and changes on upgrade, so leaving the eight was leaving a trap. `weight` was removed from all nine; Barlow and IBM Plex Mono keep theirs because those two genuinely are static.

  **One visible consequence, stated rather than buried:** Indic text asking for weight 700 previously had only 400/500/600 loaded and snapped to the nearest instance; it now renders true 700 off the variable axis. That is a fidelity improvement but it is a rendering change, not a no-op.

  **Two hypotheses were tested and discarded before the real one, both by reproducing rather than reasoning.** `style: ["normal"]` on Fraunces was removed first — still 500. Next.js also warns that it "inferred your workspace root" because a stray repo-root `package.json`/`package-lock.json` (declaring framer-motion, with no `node_modules` beside them) outranks `frontend/package-lock.json`; that warning is real but is *not* the cause, since `next build` passed with it present throughout. Those two root files were then **deleted** at Dev's request, once they were shown to be inert: they declare `framer-motion@^13.2.0`, the identical spec already in `frontend/package.json`, with no `node_modules` beside them, and the package actually resolves from `frontend/node_modules` at 13.2.0. Nothing references them — CI pins `cache-dependency-path: frontend/package-lock.json` and runs `npm ci` with `working-directory: frontend`, and no script, Dockerfile, compose file or doc names the root pair. They entered the tree in merge `d48d4b4`, i.e. a stray `npm install` run one directory too high. Proof rather than inference, same method as the data register: both files were moved out of the tree and `next build` re-run — exit 0, and the "inferred your workspace root / multiple lockfiles" warning went from 2 occurrences to 0. They stay recoverable with `git checkout HEAD -- package.json package-lock.json`.

  **Unchanged from the previous entry:** the unit suite still exits 139 at interpreter teardown after reporting all 700 passes, in runs that load torch. Pre-existing, confirmed identical before and after this work.

### [2026-09-23] Stale-data register EXECUTED — 49 files, 20.53 GB deleted after a verified backup — OPS

- **Implements:** `docs/ORCA_Stale_Data_Cleanup.md` in full — §1 (44 files), §2 (`RSMC_hycom_20260830.nc`), §3 (`etopo_all_india_real.nc`) and the 0915 run of §5 (3 files).
- **By:** Claude (Opus 5), Dev's instruction: "i think i need you to delete the files that is listed in the stale dataset md file, please be careful. Start the purge!"
- **Files:** `docs/ORCA_Stale_Data_Cleanup.md` (status → EXECUTED, new §0.1). No code changed; this was an operations task.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted, per Dev's standing instruction)
- **Done-when test:** `data/` 22.35 GB → **1.82 GB** immediately after the delete (2.31 GB after the daily refresh re-fetched current data). Post-delete: `build_pfz_fallback.py` → 6 zones off `SST_NIO_20260921.nc`; `verify_gazetteer_at_sea.py` → `203 at sea, 0 no GEBCO coverage, 0 ON LAND`; `pytest -q tests/unit` → **700 passed, 1 skipped**; `orca.data.freshness` after the daily job → `28 sources | 0 breach(es)`, exit 0.
- **Remarks:**

  **This reverses the standing rule that agents do not delete ORCA data, on Dev's explicit instruction for this purge only.** Recorded here because the rule exists for a reason and the exception should be visible rather than inferred from the absence of files. The default is unchanged.

  **The first `go` was refused, and that was the most useful thing done here.** Dev had ejected the pendrive on my own earlier advice, so at the moment of the instruction the 49 files existed in exactly one place: `data/`, which is gitignored and therefore unrecoverable from the repo. Deleting then would have been irreversible with no backup reachable. The delete waited until the drive was physically re-attached. An explicit instruction to proceed is not the same as it being safe to proceed.

  **Three gates, all of which had to pass per file.** (1) The backup was re-read *off the pendrive* and re-hashed against `MANIFEST.txt` — 49 verified, 0 failed. Re-hashing the source would have proved only that the source is still the source; the copy is the thing that has to be good. (2) At delete time each file was SHA-256'd immediately before `unlink()` and compared to its manifest entry, so a file whose bytes had drifted since the backup would have been left on disk and reported rather than destroyed — a per-file gate, not an all-or-nothing precondition. 0 were skipped. (3) The script asserted that no path on its list contained `20260917` or `20260921` and would have refused to run otherwise. A typo in a 49-entry list is the one mistake that cannot be undone after the fact, so the keepers were named explicitly instead of being trusted to be absent.

  **The freshness gate reported 4 breaches immediately after the delete, and that was not the delete.** `incois_osf_hycom`, `incois_osf_ww3`, `mosdac_nrt_sst` and `mosdac_open_sst` all read 2.4d. Every one of those sources' newest content date was 2026-09-21 — a kept file — and every deleted file was older than its own source's newest, so no source's maximum content date moved. It was elapsed time against a DAILY threshold, because the refresh is still not registered as a scheduled task. Running `scripts/cron/refresh_daily.cmd` cleared it to 0 breaches. Worth recording as a trap: after a large delete the instinct is to read any new breach as damage, and the discriminating check is whether the source's *newest* content date changed, not whether its file count did.

  **Stormglass is no longer outstanding.** `stormglass_tides` now reports 0.0d with 10 files. The HTTP 402 quota (10 requests/day against 14 ports) spread itself across consecutive daily runs exactly as predicted, so it never needed a code change.

  **Still outstanding, and now the only thing on this track:** registering `orca-refresh-daily` and `orca-refresh-weekly` as Windows scheduled tasks (§3 of `docs/Guide/ORCA_Data_Refresh_Cron_Guide.md`). That needs an elevated PowerShell session, so it is Dev's to run. Until it is done the freshness gate will keep re-breaching on age every couple of days, which is the gate working, not failing.

Found by noticing the new `thresholds` field was missing from a live response despite the direct-Python unit check proving it worked; confirmed by querying the real server's LAN address instead. Killed with Dev's explicit confirmation; `localhost:8000` now reaches the correct process. Worth knowing for whoever verifies Phase 4 next: if a backend change doesn't show up live, check `netstat -ano | grep :8000` for more than one bound process before assuming the code is wrong.

It previously revealed a `ReadoutGrid` with "IMBL distance" and "MPA status", exactly the engineer-facing language this point exists to remove. Five steps, each a real interaction against the live backend: (1) run the persona's preset question; (2) watch the real agent strip stream (advances automatically on a streaming-true→false transition, not a timer or a turn count — a turn-count version was tried first and found to break on a page reload mid-tour, see remarks); (3) prompts opening a source citation; (4) asks a real follow-up in Tamil (`நாளை மாலை எப்படி இருக்கும்?`), routed through the live Bhashini pipeline; (5) links to `/reasoning?query_id=…` for the trace just produced, plus the persona's own next step (fisherman → `/watches`, navigator → `/voyage`, researcher → `/data`, authority → `/ops`). Trigger is a "Take the 3-minute tour →" link on `/ask`'s empty state, hidden once `completed`. **Live-verified end to end** as `agent@orca.test`: ran the real preset question, watched the agent strip stream and the tour auto-advance through steps 2–3, clicked through step 4's Tamil follow-up (confirmed `Language Ingress` / `Language Egress` spans read `Bhashini NMT` and the English translation panel showed a correct answer to "what about tomorrow evening"), and reached step 5 with a working `/reasoning?query_id=` link and `/watches` next-step link; clicked "Finish tour".

(1) **Copy**, "the one missing per-turn action on `/ask`" (re-ask, open trace and re-render already existed): `CopyControl` in `RerunControl.tsx`, a quiet ghost icon matching the existing rerun control's family, `navigator.clipboard.writeText()` with a 1.5s "Copied" confirmation, rendered beside `RerunControl` in `ChatTurn.tsx`. Live-verified present and clickable (clipboard write itself could not be confirmed from the automated browser, which denies clipboard permission by default — the click handler ran with no console error, i.e. the failure path was exercised, not the success path). (2) **`/design` removed from the production build**: `notFound()` from `next/navigation` at the top of `DesignPage()`, gated on `process.env.NODE_ENV === "production"` — the file stays for local dev work (the point's own text: "removed from the production build", not deleted) rather than being deleted outright. Live-verified the gate does NOT fire in dev (`/design` still renders normally with `npm run dev`); the production branch itself was not built and smoke-tested this session (no `next build` run) — traced instead, the same `notFound()`-in-a-Client-Component pattern Next.js documents.

- **Remarks:**

  **Phase 4 is now 17/17 `DONE`** — this closes the phase's own exit gate (four personas visibly different, every number provenance-badged, status bar honest when the backend is down — the last three already verified in earlier entries this phase).

  **P4.9's auto-advance bug, and why the fix matters for "resumable":** the first implementation tracked a `useRef` snapshot of `turns.length` at the step-2→3 transition, then compared against it at step 4. That ref resets to its initial value on every remount — so resuming the tour after a page reload (a real scenario: the point's Done-when explicitly asks for resumable) silently skipped the entire step 4 follow-up, jumping straight from clicking "Next" on step 3 to step 5, because the reset ref made the step-4 turn-count check pass immediately. Rewrote to track a streaming-true→false transition instead (`wasStreamingRef`), which cannot false-positive on a resume because it requires actually witnessing a query's streaming lifecycle in the current mount — verified by clearing `localStorage`, restarting the tour, and reaching step 4 correctly on a second run.

  **P4.10's scope cut, disclosed rather than silently narrowed:** the point's own row bundles four sub-items — (a) nothing fabricated/MISSING-labelled, (b) typographic hierarchy, (c) spacing + all four states (loading/empty/error/offline), (d) motion only where it explains something — plus the two concrete actions (Copy, `/design`) into one "1.5 d" estimate. This entry closes only the two concrete, literally-named actions; (a)-(d) are a cross-surface audit this session did not have budget for. Whoever picks this up next should treat (a)-(d) as the remaining open work under this point's own number, not as newly discovered scope.

### [2026-09-23] P5.5, P5.10, P5.19, P5.26, P5.27 — Phase 5 part 1: geofence-to-verdict wiring, the catch-statistics refusal, Sentinel "safe again", MPA precision, circuit breakers

- **Implements:** `R-INDIA-6` (P5.5), `R-INDIA-5` catch half (P5.10), orca_final §11.3 (P5.19), orca_final §6.2 (P5.26), orca_final §4.4/§4.6 (P5.27).

- **Done-when tests, per point:**

  **P5.3 — DONE.** The gauge reading and the astronomical prediction "were both already available and never compared" (the plan's own words) turned out to be exactly right: `_live_gauge_observation()` hardcoded `predicted_astronomical_m`/`sea_level_anomaly_m` to `None` with an honest `fields_unavailable` note, even for stations the SoI table actually covers. `predict_tides()` can't answer "what height was predicted at this exact instant" — it only names the next high/low extreme — so added `predicted_height_at(lat, lon, when)`: a half-cosine interpolation between the SoI table's bracketing high/low events (the standard approximation absent harmonic constituents; a real model is P5.14's pyTMD/FES2022 scope). Wired into `_live_gauge_observation` keyed to the *gauge's own* coordinates (not the caller's position — the prediction has to be for the station the reading came from), producing a real `sea_level_anomaly_m` residual for the two IOC gauges the SoI table actually covers (Chennai/CHE, Cochin/KOC); Marmagao, Port Blair and Nancowry have no SoI table and correctly stay in `fields_unavailable`, no fabricated estimate. Added `anomaly_datum_caveat`, disclosing that IOC publishes sea level against the gauge's own local reference, not necessarily the SoI table's chart datum (LAT) — so a *sustained* anomaly is the storm-surge-setup signal orca_final calls for, not necessarily a single instantaneous reading, which can carry a fixed per-station offset. Also did the plan's other named half — "divergence beyond tolerance floors the berthing-window confidence, same rule as P2.4, second variable": a disclosed `_ANOMALY_TOLERANCE_M = 0.3` degrades the gauge reading's own confidence from HIGH to MEDIUM when `|sea_level_anomaly_m|` exceeds it, and that confidence (previously computed but then always discarded — `run()` stripped `confidence` out of the dict before it reached `outputs["tide"]`) now actually joins `contributing` alongside the PFZ/persistence confidences that already feed the agent's overall tier, gated to `source_kind == "in_situ_gauge"` so altimetry's own (unrelated) confidence isn't double-counted. **`/voyage`'s berthing panel already renders `Observed`/`Predicted`/`Anomaly` in a three-column readout (`frontend/app/voyage/page.tsx:370-387`) — it was built for this and has been silently showing "—" for the last two columns until this fix; no frontend change was needed.** **Done-when test:** `python -m pytest backend/tests/unit/test_ocean_analytics.py -q` → `25 passed` (one test updated to reflect the fields now being populated for Chennai, one new test added for `predicted_height_at`'s interpolation and its outside-the-table `None`). The live-network path was actually exercised, not mocked: `test_tide_gauge_live_reading_carries_only_what_the_feed_publishes` skips only if the IOC feed itself is unreachable, and this run reached the real feed and confirmed `sea_level_anomaly_m == observed_level_m − predicted_astronomical_m` to within rounding.

  **P5.8 — DONE.** New `_worthwhileness_disclosure()` in `graph.py`, called from `risk_assessment_node` only when the verdict is a real `GO`: if `ocean_data.sector_status.is_data_gap` (cloud-suppressed or no advisory on record) and the nearest PFZ is either not found or farther than a disclosed `_PFZ_DAY_TRIP_KM = 50.0` km, appends a plain-language disclosure ("Safe to sail. But no fishing advisory for your sector today (cloud cover), and the nearest is NNN km away — not a realistic day trip."), matching the plan's own worked example. The 50 km cut is a fixed, named constant, not a per-vessel reach calculation — that already exists separately as `/zones`' cruise-speed-based `useVesselReachKm()` hook (frontend-only; cruise speed is not threaded into this backend's `ORCAState` yet, and adding that plumbing was out of this point's stated scope). **Done-when test:** `python -m pytest backend/tests/unit/test_reconciliation.py backend/tests/unit/test_phase2_reasoning.py backend/tests/unit/test_gps_fix.py backend/tests/unit/test_voyage.py backend/tests/unit/test_query_coverage.py backend/tests/unit/test_place_resolution.py -q` → `191 passed` (files chosen because they exercise `graph.py`'s risk_assessment_node path); no test yet asserts the new disclosure's text directly — the next person touching this should add one with a fixture `ocean_data` carrying `sector_status.is_data_gap=True` and a far `nearest_pfz`.

  **P5.15 — DONE, but it was done on 2026-09-19 and never logged.** Checked before building anything: `orca/agents/weather_intelligence.py`'s `get_incois_hazard_alerts()`/`_fetch_incois_hazard_bulletins()` already fetch INCOIS's public HWA/SSA/currents JSON endpoints live (`sarat.incois.gov.in/.../hwassalatestdata`, `samudra.incois.gov.in/.../currentslatestdata`), falling back to SACHET only on a transport failure; `orca/agents/ocean_analytics.py`'s `tide_gauge_observation()`/`_live_gauge_observation()` already fetch the IOC/UNESCO Sea Level Monitoring feed live, falling back to CMEMS altimetry. Both are already in `data/freshness.py`'s `FETCHED_LIVE` set, and `docs/ORCA_Data_Freshness_Contract.md` lines 88-89 and 285-286 already document both as "fetched live since 2026-09-19." **Done-when test:** `python -c "from orca.data.freshness import live_contract_violations; print(live_contract_violations())"` → `[]`. **Real finding, not acted on:** `get_incois_hazard_alerts()` and `risk_assessment.check_active_hazards()` (which would be the natural caller) are each exercised only by their own unit test — neither is called anywhere in the actual graph/API path, so the live HWA/SSA/currents bulletins this fetch pulls never reach a user-facing surface today.   **P5.9 — DONE.** Added `WORTHWHILENESS`, `TIMING`, `COUNTERFACTUAL`, `COMPARISON`, `ENDURANCE`, `FUEL_ECONOMICS` to `ROUTING_TABLE` (a `# arrive with P5.9` comment already marked exactly where these belonged) with real card actions in `intent_actions.py`: WORTHWHILENESS/TIMING call the real PFZ/forecast tools; COUNTERFACTUAL parses the query's own referenced window via the existing `resolve_temporal_expression` and compares it against now; COMPARISON is the actual bug fix named in the plan text ("today the first match silently wins") — it now resolves and checks **both** named places with real weather/verdict calls instead of silently answering for one; ENDURANCE is an honest gap (`vessels` has `cruise_speed_kn`/`fuel_burn_lph` but no fuel-capacity column, and endurance needs capacity ÷ burn rate); FUEL_ECONOMICS names what's missing to compute it for real (vessel numbers are not threaded into `intent_actions.build()`'s call signature yet). **Done-when test:** `pytest tests/unit/test_intent_actions.py tests/unit/test_query_coverage.py tests/unit/test_planning.py -q` → `116 passed`, including live network round-trips for TIMING/COUNTERFACTUAL/COMPARISON (not mocked — same test philosophy `test_ocean_analytics.py` already uses). **Found by the full-suite run after this entry, fixed the same session, in two rounds:** (1) `test_intent_embeddings.py::test_every_routing_row_with_agents_can_be_paraphrased_into` failed — the 7 new rows had no `ROW_PHRASINGS` entries in `orca/intent_embeddings.py`, so Tier 2 (embedding-similarity fallback) could never match a paraphrase of any of them, only the exact Tier-1 keyword list. Added 5 example phrasings per row, worded distinctly from the Tier-1 keywords (that is the whole point of a Tier-2 tier). (2) That fix broke three *other* tests it hadn't been run against yet (`test_conversation_context.py`'s carry-forward suite, and one existing `test_intent_embeddings.py` paraphrase) — my new phrasings shared a single salient word with the test's own fixture query in each case ("tomorrow" against a TIMING phrasing, "wind" against three of mine at once) and e5-small's narrow similarity band (documented at the top of `intent_embeddings.py`: unrelated marine sentences already sit ~0.80, threshold is 0.835) turned that one shared noun into a false match. Diagnosed by embedding each phrasing against the offending query directly (`_model.encode(...) @ query_vector`) rather than guessing which phrase was at fault, then removing every phrasing that shared a distinctive noun with an existing row's own test vocabulary ("wind", "tomorrow") in favour of synonyms already used elsewhere in this same dict ("swell", "this morning"). Re-verified with a full scan of all 30 existing `PARAPHRASES` against `classify_intent_deterministic`, not just the one query pytest happened to catch — zero breakages found on that second pass. **This is the general risk of adding rows to this file, not a one-off:** any new `ROW_PHRASINGS` entry should be scanned against the existing `PARAPHRASES`/conversation-context fixtures the same way before being trusted, since the failure mode is silent (a wrong route) rather than a crash.

- **Done-when tests, per point:**

  **P5.22 UI — DONE.** This app had **no `/profile` page at all** before this — the `ADMINISTRATIVE` routing row (P5.29) already linked `href: "/profile"` and it 404'd. Built one: account identity (read-only), home port (view + edit form, `PUT /api/profile/home-port`), vessel (list + make-active + an add-vessel form covering the full `cruise_speed_kn`/`fuel_burn_lph`/`draft_m` set `POST /api/vessels` already accepted but no screen exposed — this also partially unblocks P5.9's FUEL_ECONOMICS gap, since a vessel with real numbers can now exist), **quiet hours** (a 24-hour instrument strip — shaded window, live "now" tick recomputed every minute in the chosen timezone, correctly split into two segments when the window wraps midnight — plus the actual `PUT /api/profile/quiet-hours` form), and language/persona pickers (`PUT /api/profile/language`, `PUT /api/profile/persona`). Added a "Profile" entry to `AccountMenu`'s dropdown (where "My watches" already lived) since `/profile` is a settings surface, not one of the ten primary console destinations `NAV_ROUTES` lists. **Real defect found and fixed while building this:** `useAuth()`'s own documented three-state contract allows `{status: "signed_in", profile: null}` as a transient (the profile fetch hasn't resolved yet) — every panel here seeds its local form state from `profile` in `useState`'s initializer, so mounting against a still-null profile froze quiet hours/home port at "nothing set" forever, even after the real value arrived. Fixed by holding the loading skeleton until `profile` is non-null, and keying the editor by `JSON.stringify(profile)` so a save-triggered `invalidateProfile()` remounts every panel fresh instead of leaving stale `useState` around. Caught by literally saving quiet hours in the browser and reloading — the checkbox came back unchecked despite `SELECT quiet_hours FROM users` showing the write had landed; this is exactly the class of bug "read the API response and stop" would have missed. **Done-when test:** live browser round-trip — checked the box, set 22:00–06:00 Asia/Kolkata, saved (`PUT` → 200, confirmed via `docker exec ... psql`), hard-reloaded, confirmed the checkbox and both times came back checked/populated, confirmed the "quiet now"/"not quiet" badge tracks the live clock correctly for a same-timezone instant.

  **P5.6 — DONE for the named-band visual treatment, not the closing-time estimate.** `WatchCard` now renders the CLEAR/ADVISORY/WATCH/WARNING/CRITICAL ladder with its real nm cuts (12–6 / 6–3 / 3–1 / ≤1) for every `geofence_approach` watch — reading as a graded warning rather than a bare distance the viewer has to already know how to interpret, which is the point's whole ask — and tags each fired alert in the alert-history list with the band it fired at, sourced straight from `rendered_payload.snapshot.band` (P5.18's own data, not a new fetch). **Real defect found and fixed in passing:** the advanced watch form always submitted `wave_height_m`/`wind_kt` thresholds regardless of watch type, so a `geofence_approach`/`pfz_shift` watch got a stray, meaningless "wave_height_m 2.5" pill — neither type ever reads a threshold. Fixed: those fields are hidden (not just ignored) for these two types, replaced with an honest one-line explanation of what actually fires them. **Not done:** approach-direction "closing in ~6h" — that needs a live vessel heading/speed feed this codebase has no source for (a registered vessel has `last_position`/`last_position_at`, one fixed point, not a track); bearing-to-boundary is already shown via P5.18's own alert text, a fabricated closing-time is not. **Done-when test:** live browser — created a real `geofence_approach` watch, confirmed the four-band legend renders with the exact cuts, confirmed the stray threshold pill is gone and the honest hint text shows instead, for both `geofence_approach` and (read the code path) `pfz_shift`.

- **Remarks:**

  **The `useAuth()` transient-null bug is worth a general note for whoever next builds a profile-shaped screen in this app:** any component that seeds `useState` from `auth.profile` needs to either gate on `profile !== null` before mounting (this entry's fix) or re-sync via a `useEffect([profile])`, never trust the initializer alone — `{status: "signed_in", profile: null}` is a real, valid, non-transient-in-the-type-system state per `useAuth`'s own three-state union, so a form the type checker sees no problem with can silently freeze on stale data anyway.

  **Impeccable's `context.mjs` reported no PRODUCT.md and no DESIGN.md for this project** — offered here, not blocked on: running `/impeccable init` (or `document` against the existing app to derive DESIGN.md from what's already built) would give the next design pass a written record of the "marine instrument console" system this entry reverse-engineered from component source instead of redoing that reading each time. A newer Impeccable (v4.3.1) is also available (installed v4.1.3); update in a later session, not this one.

  **Still open on the UI side, unchanged from part 3's list:** per-watch escalation editing (the `escalation` jsonb column has no form anywhere yet), saved-voyages UI on `/voyage` (P5.20 is API-only), P5.28's map drawing tool for area watches, P5.24's voyage outputs. None of these were started this entry.

- **Remarks:**

  **The track for P6.7 needed real grid-searching, not a plausible-looking guess.** The first attempt used Rameswaram itself as the origin, which turned out to already be 2.2 nm from the nearest boundary — inside the WARNING band before the scenario even starts. The working track required probing `nearest_boundary_line` across a grid to find a run of points whose nearest segment stayed the same treaty line throughout; this is the same category of mistake P5.6's own log entry flagged for a different reason (checking real computed values rather than trusting an assumed geography), and it's worth recording again here because it cost real iteration time this session.

  **The "added_nm: 0.0" honesty question was deliberated, not glossed over.** `plan_voyage`'s offset-detour strategy computes added distance as the straight-line delta between the *shifted* origin/destination pair, which is mathematically always ~0 for a parallel shift — a real, disclosed property of this codebase's existing detour math (see P5.7's own log entry), not something this session introduced or could tune away without changing production route-planning behaviour for every other caller of `plan_voyage`. The scenario card's copy states "added distance" only where the plan text asks for it and lets `/voyage`'s own real numbers (11.9 nm total distance, the rerouted map, the reclassified segment table) carry the "detour" story instead of forcing a fabricated delta.

  **User questions raised alongside this work, answered in-conversation rather than in this log:** why the border-crossing scenario measures distance in nautical miles (nm is the unit `imbl_distance_nm`, `sentinel._GEOFENCE_BANDS`, and `WatchCard`'s existing band ladder already use throughout this codebase — nm is also the maritime-chart standard everywhere else in the world, so P6.7 reuses the existing field and existing 12/6/3/1 nm cuts rather than inventing a new unit for the demo); and whether the warning fires early enough — the ladder already escalates before the verdict itself changes (an ADVISORY chip at 12 nm and a spoken WATCH warning at 6 nm, both well ahead of the WARNING band at 3 nm where `evaluate_marine_safety` actually flips the verdict to CAUTION), and the underlying 3 nm/1 nm cutoffs themselves are Architecture §3.1's own hard-constraint thresholds (`risk_assessment.py`'s own comment: "transcribed verbatim... and must stay that way"), not a value this scenario is free to move without a threshold-provenance change tracked the way P6.1 tracks the others.

  **Follow-up in the same session: P6.7's card was upgraded from a text-only table to a live `MapView`**, after direct feedback that a single line of text is not how a border crossing should read. `IMBLCrossing.tsx` now carries a `MapView` instance (boundary-line layer on, layer panel/region switcher/legends/sounding HUD off — the same "focused, no console-of-knobs" configuration `/ask`'s embedded map already uses) fed a `routeGeoJson` built fresh each step from the travelled frames: one line-string leg per step, coloured `CLEAR`/`CAUTION`/`BLOCKED` off the real `go_no_go` verdict (`voyage_route_layer`'s own three-state contract, not a new colour scale), labelled with the band name on the line itself, plus a `MapPin` for the start and a second, recoloured pin that moves to the vessel's current position each frame. No new map code was written — `MapView`'s existing `routeGeoJson`/`pins` props and its existing camera-refit-to-route-bounds effect (already built for `/voyage`) do all of it; this is reuse, not a second map component. Live-verified in a real browser: the boundary line, a green ADVISORY/WATCH route with the moving vessel pin, and the same real P4.12 takeover firing at the end — zero console errors (one pre-existing, unrelated MapLibre `clusterMaxZoom` warning). `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint app/demo/IMBLCrossing.tsx` clean.

### [2026-09-24] P3.8 follow-up — Bhashini ASR actually serving voice, live captions, spoken-language picker, LLM timeout

- **Implements:** fix-forward on `P3.8` (Bhashini as the primary voice path) and the voice UX of `P3.7`; LLM timeout is a `P2.13` gap (a provider that stalls must degrade, not hang).
- **By:** Claude (Opus 5.5). Every Bhashini claim below was measured against the live Dhruva endpoint with this repo's real `BHASHINI_*` credentials; the planning stall was reproduced through `/ask` as `agent@orca.test`.
- **Files:** `backend/orca/agents/bhashini.py`, `backend/orca/api/voice_routes.py`, `backend/orca/llm/registry.py`, `backend/tests/unit/test_voice.py`, `frontend/app/components/VoiceInput.tsx`.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **What was wrong, measured:**

  1. **Bhashini ASR never served a browser recording.** `bhashini.asr` sent no `audioFormat`; Dhruva answers a bare `500` for WebM/Opus (MediaRecorder's output) unless it is declared, so every mic query fell through to faster-whisper `small` on CPU — 8.1–8.2 s per clip, and for Kannada speech it returned romanized mush (hint `en`) or Kannada written in Devanagari (auto-detect). The previous self-check never caught it because it round-tripped Bhashini's own WAV. With `audioFormat: "webm"` the same clip transcribes correctly in ~1.0 s.
  2. **Spoken language was the navbar language.** English UI + Kannada speech asked Bhashini for English ASR, which hallucinated English ("I am going to Chennai tomorrow to fish market").
  3. **No live transcript** — the clip was uploaded only on stop.
  4. **Planning "stuck" for 94.9 s** — not planning work: Planning's Tier-3 intent-confirmation call to `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` stalled, and `genai.Client` had no timeout. Direct re-measurement: first call 40.8 s, next two 1.2 s each. The SDK makes a single attempt by default (`_api_client.py` `retry_args`), so this was one hung request, not retries.

- **What changed:**

  - `bhashini.asr` declares `audioFormat` (RIFF → `wav`, else `webm`) and, by default, adds inline `preProcessors: [vad, denoiser]` / `postProcessors: [itn, punctuation]` (confirmed live: punctuation adds the `?` and commas; +0.5–1 s). `clean=False` skips them for live partials.
  - `POST /voice/transcribe` takes `partial=true`: Bhashini only, no processors, never the local rung (an 8 s fallback caption would land after the user stopped talking); returns an empty transcript when Bhashini is down. Both paths now run via `asyncio.to_thread`, so a slow fallback no longer blocks the event loop.
  - `VoiceInput.tsx`: `recorder.start(250)` timeslices; every 1.5 s the accumulated WebM (every prefix is decodable — confirmed live on 30%/60% truncations) is sent as a partial and rendered in the speaker's script; stop sends the full clip once, cleaned, into the existing editable confirm box (never auto-submitted, unchanged). A spoken-language `<select>` sits beside the mic, defaulting to the navbar language and reset by navbar changes.
  - `GeminiProvider` client gets `HttpOptions(timeout=15_000)`. A timeout classifies as `provider unavailable (timeout)` and Planning keeps its Tier-2 match (verified by forcing a 1 ms cap: `LLMUnavailable` raised, `_tier3_llm_fallback` returned `[]`).

- **Service coverage against the Bhashini access list**, probed on the default pipeline `64392f96daac500b55c543cd`: ASR, NMT, TTS, Transliteration work and are used; VAD, Denoiser, ITN, Punctuation work only as inline ASR processors and are now used; TLD, ALD, NER, TN are rejected by that pipeline (`400 TaskType is not valid !`) and need the account's own pipeline ID.
- **Verification:** `pytest tests/unit/test_voice.py tests/unit/test_phase2_reasoning.py tests/unit/test_intent_actions.py` — 100 passed; `python -m orca.agents.bhashini` live self-check ok; `python -m orca.llm.tiers` ok; eslint clean on `VoiceInput.tsx`. The user confirmed live that the Kannada transcript appears fast in the real browser. **Not re-verified after the timeout change:** the end-to-end `/ask` run (needs a backend restart to pick up `registry.py`).
- **Remarks:** Safari records MP4, which `_audio_format` labels `webm` and Dhruva will reject — Safari voice still falls to faster-whisper (marked `ponytail:` in code). Automatic spoken-language detection (ALD) is blocked on the pipeline ID, not on code.

### [2026-09-24] Ocean Analytics — an unscoreable PFZ-persistence history no longer grades the answer LOW_DATA

- **Implements:** bug fix in Agent 5's confidence roll-up (`R-PS` provenance/confidence honesty); no new point.
- **By:** Claude (Opus 5.5), from the user's report that "Conditions at mangrol" showed Ocean and Reporting as LOW.
- **Files:** `backend/orca/agents/ocean_analytics.py`, `backend/tests/unit/test_ocean_analytics.py`.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **Cause, measured:** Ocean's confidence is `_worst()` of its contributors. For Mangrol (21.08, 70.1) those were tide MEDIUM (Stormglass MSL fallback), nearest PFZ HIGH, SST/chlorophyll HIGH, and PFZ persistence LOW_DATA. `score_pfz_persistence` refuses to score below 5 archived days in its 7-day window (only 4: 16, 18, 22, 23 Sep), but `run()` let it contribute from `days_on_record >= 2` — so every query with 2-4 archived days was graded LOW_DATA, contradicting the comment directly above that gate ("without degrading live operational safety"). Reporting then inherited LOW_DATA via "worst of 4 contributing agents".
- **Fix:** persistence contributes only when it produced a real score (`label != "INDICATIVE"`); the INDICATIVE result still ships on `outputs.pfz_persistence`.
- **Verification:** new `test_an_indicative_persistence_does_not_grade_the_agent` fails on the old gate (checked by stashing the fix) and passes on the new one; `tests/unit/test_ocean_analytics.py` 25 passed. **Live-verified**: re-ran "Conditions at mangrol" through `/query` on a fresh backend — `ocean_analytics` and both `reporting` spans now MEDIUM (74), previously LOW_DATA (39).
- **Remarks:** the underlying data gap — the daily PFZ archive is not accumulating every day — is left for the planned cron setup; until it runs daily, persistence will stay INDICATIVE (reported, no longer penalising).

- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)

The history archive was keyed by scrape day (the evening *before* each advisory's `valid_for`), and two scrapes of the 23 Sep advisory counted as two days.
- **What changed:**
  - `freshness.RECENCY_BANDS` / `recency()` — per-dataset age bands (PFZ: fresh ≤ 3 d, hint ≤ 7 d, else history) plus `expired`, on the IST day boundary.
  - `analytics_loaders.pfz_advisories_by_date()` groups every snapshot by `valid_for` and de-duplicates; `load_pfz_latest()` keeps each sector's newest advisory with its band; `load_pfz_live_geojson()` serves all of them, each feature tagged (replaces the drop-older-days guard). `load_pfz_master` / `available_pfz_history_dates` / `_newest_day_only` removed — no remaining callers.
  - `nearest_pfz` searches latest-per-sector within `PFZ_MAX_REACH_KM = 150` and returns `valid_for/age_days/band/expired/max_km`; Ocean's PFZ confidence follows the band (fresh HIGH, hint MEDIUM, history LOW_DATA). `sector_status` gains `latest_advisory`. Persistence counts advisory dates, not folders.
  - Critic facts, trace summary, and a new Reporting rule 9 (generic: dated data is given with its date and age, never presented as current, never omitted for being old).
  - Scraper names new history folders by `valid_for`. Existing folders left as-is (never delete data); the loader ignores folder names.
  - Map: fresh = normal fish, hint = faded + "N d" label, history = grey fish; clusters take their freshest member's style (`clusterProperties.freshest`). Card: Valid Until badge reads **Active** or **Expired · N d ago**. `/zones`: a cloud-covered sector shows its latest advisory and age; an old nearest zone carries an "Older advisory" line. Answer-card PFZ tile appends the age.
- **Verification:** full backend unit suite 703 passed, 1 skipped (run after every backend change and both new tests); `test_ocean_analytics.py` + `test_analytics_routes.py` + `test_critic.py` 42 passed, including two new tests (archive grouping/latest-per-sector labelling; reach cap + age). `tsc` and eslint clean on the three frontend files. **Live-verified** as `agent@orca.test` on a fresh backend: `/ask` "pfzs near rameswaram" answered "Chinnapalam … 33.7 km southwest … latest available data from September 19. Today's sector data is affected by cloud cover, so use this previous advisory as a pointer"; `/map` shows zones along Karnataka and the whole east coast; Gulf of Mannar shows 83 faded "5 d" zones; clicking Mandapam (North) shows "Valid until 2026-09-19 · EXPIRED · 5 D AGO"; `/zones` shows "Latest advisory sector: 2026-09-19, 99 zones — 5 days old".
- **Remarks:** Postgres/Redis were down this session (Docker Desktop not running) — login 500'd until `docker compose up -d`. A console 404 on `PUT /api/chats/<id>/turns/<id>` appeared while saving the chat turn; unrelated to PFZ and not investigated. The daily PFZ scrape still needs the cron job; until then "fresh" zones will age into hint/history on their own.

### [2026-09-24] Nearest-PFZ answers name the place the distance is measured from

- **Implements:** fix on top of the stale-data PFZ entry above; location honesty (Reporting rule 4's intent).
- **By:** Claude (Opus 5.5), from the user's report that "pfzs near mangalore" answered "…from Kunzhathur … 32.1 km west-southwest" and never mentioned Mangalore.
- **Files:** `backend/orca/agents/ocean_analytics.py`, `backend/orca/agents/critic.py`, `backend/orca/agents/reporting.py`, `backend/tests/unit/test_ocean_analytics.py`.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **Cause:** Mangalore resolved correctly (12.85, 74.65); 32.1 km WSW is ORCA's distance *from Mangalore*. But the zone dict's only place name was `landing_center: Kunzhathur` — INCOIS's landmark, whose own distance to the zone is 52-57 km NW — so the narrator paired ORCA's distance with INCOIS's place ("32 km WSW of Kunzhathur", true of neither). Separately, Ocean's `nearest_pfz` output dict omitted the `age_days`/`band`/`expired` fields added in the previous entry, so agents only saw `valid_for`.
- **Fix:** `nearest_pfz` output now carries `measured_from` (the resolved place name, else coordinates) beside ORCA's distance/compass, and `incois_reference` ("52-57 km NW of Kunzhathur") beside the landmark; plus the missing age fields. Critic facts carry both origins; Reporting rule 10: a distance is stated with its own origin, and never paired with the other origin's place name.
- **Verification:** new `test_nearest_pfz_keeps_orcas_distance_and_incois_landmark_apart`; full backend unit suite 704 passed, 1 skipped. **Live-verified** via `/query` on a restarted backend: "The nearest Potential Fishing Zone is located 32 km WSW of Mangalore, in waters 65 to 70 meters deep … latest available advisory from September 19 (5 days old)…"; Rameswaram: "…33.7 km southwest of Rameswaram…".

### [2026-09-24] Stale-data policy, MOSDAC SST / chlorophyll / CMEMS

- **Implements:** second row of `docs/ORCA_Stale_Data_Policy.md` §4's rollout tracker, per the user's "let's do MOSDAC SST next".
- **By:** Claude (Sonnet 5).
- **Files:** `backend/orca/data/freshness.py`, `backend/orca/data/satellite_loaders.py`, `backend/orca/agents/ocean_analytics.py`, `backend/tests/unit/test_ocean_analytics.py`, `frontend/app/lib/recency.ts` (new), `frontend/app/trends/page.tsx`, `frontend/app/zones/page.tsx`, `docs/ORCA_Stale_Data_Policy.md`.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **Scope check before writing code:** unlike PFZ, the gridded SST/chlorophyll product (`satellite_loaders.py`) does not currently feed the map (no SST tile layer is ever built — `tiles.py`'s cmocean ramp is reserved for a future D3 step) or any agent's narrative (`critic.py`/`reporting.py` carry no SST facts; `correlate_sst_chlorophyll` only ever fed `/trends`). So there was no map/narrative surface to label — building one would be a new feature, not a stale-data fix, and out of scope for this pass.
- **What changed:**
  - `freshness.RECENCY_BANDS` gains `mosdac_open_sst` (DAILY, 3/7 d — same as PFZ) and `mosdac_open_chl`/`copernicus_cmems` (WEEKLY, 7/14 d — double PFZ's ratio to match the class).
  - `satellite_loaders.py`'s four grid loaders (`load_insat_sst`, `load_eos06_chl`, `load_cmems_sst`, `load_cmems_chl`) each tag their own `provenance` with `age_days`/`band`/`expired` at load time via a new `_recency()` helper, the same "banded at the loader" shape PFZ rows already carry.
  - `correlate_sst_chlorophyll`'s confidence is now the worst of its existing n/lag check and a new `_worst_band()` over the two granules' bands — closes a real hole: two old-but-simultaneous granules (no `acquisition_gap` between them, both from the same stale week) previously read HIGH.
  - `/trends`: `sst_provenance`/`chl_provenance` already carry the new fields through the route unchanged; a new `GranuleAge` component shows each granule's own age caveat once it leaves the fresh band. Extracted `frontend/app/lib/recency.ts` (`Recency` type + `ageLabel`) out of `zones/page.tsx`, which used to define its own copy, so both pages grade "how old" identically.
- **Verification:** two new tests, `test_correlation_degrades_when_a_granule_is_history_band` (isolates the band check from the n<20 and lag rules, asserts LOW_DATA) and `test_correlation_stays_high_when_both_granules_are_fresh`; `pytest tests/unit/test_ocean_analytics.py tests/unit/test_analytics_routes.py` 37 passed; `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean on the three frontend files. `python -m orca.data.freshness` and `python -m orca.data.satellite_loaders` self-checks pass (one pre-existing, unrelated assertion in the latter — `cchl` fresher than `chl` — fails identically on unmodified `main`, confirmed by `git stash`; a fact about which files happen to be on disk, not this change). **Live-verified** as `agent@orca.test`: `/trends` `SST × chlorophyll correlation` panel renders unchanged with today's fresh (2-day-old) grids and no spurious caution text; `GET /api/trends` confirmed `sst_provenance.band`/`chl_provenance.band` = `"fresh"` with `age_days: 2`.
- **Remarks:** MOSDAC SST and chlorophyll were done together, and both tracker rows marked done, because one function (`correlate_sst_chlorophyll`) and one panel serve both — touching the file twice under an artificial split would have been the more expensive path, not the lazier one. `copernicus_cmems` is one shared source id for SST, chlorophyll and sea-level fallback in `SOURCE_CLASS`; `load_cmems_ssh` (sea-level) was left untagged — it isn't part of this correlation panel and isn't in the tracker's SST/chl rows.

### [2026-09-24] Stale-data policy — INCOIS OSF WW3/HYCOM banded, Stormglass tides fixed, remaining `SOURCE_CLASS` rows closed out as exempt

- **Implements:** the last three rows of `docs/ORCA_Stale_Data_Policy.md` §4's rollout tracker, per the user's "yes lets move onto the last 3, if you think doing all them at once is too much you can do it batch wise, its your decision" — done as one batch, since the three rows turned out to be one small code change, one small code change, and one documentation-only closeout, not three separate investigations worth re-paying the setup cost for.
- **By:** Claude (Sonnet 5).
- **Files:** `backend/orca/data/freshness.py`, `backend/orca/data/analytics_loaders.py`, `backend/orca/agents/ocean_analytics.py`, `backend/tests/unit/test_ocean_analytics.py`, `frontend/app/voyage/page.tsx`, `docs/ORCA_Stale_Data_Policy.md`.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **Scope check before writing code:** for OSF WW3/HYCOM, the only surface that reaches a downstream consumer is `nearest_osf_point_forecast`'s per-product `forecast_time` (the Ocean Analytics span on the `/query` stream, shown on the Reasoning page — *corrected 2026-09-24: it does not reach the written narrative, whose inputs omit `osf_point_forecast`; see `docs/ORCA_Chatbot_Response_Plan.md` F1*) — the grid-CSV fallback has no per-cell date, the raster tile pyramid's provenance is built empty with no frontend reader, and the current/wind map-layer routes already have their own slider-sync grey-out mechanism. For Stormglass, the cached tide extremes are a forecast, not an observation: an old cached pull's predicted high/low tide stays correct until its stated window runs out (already handled by pre-existing LOW_DATA-on-exhaustion logic), so banding it fresh/hint/history the way PFZ is banded would be answering the wrong question — the real gap was that the fallback reported its acquisition timestamp as the query time instead of the cache's own vintage. For the remaining `SOURCE_CLASS` rows, checking each loader's actual return shape against `discovery.py`'s `SOURCE_REGISTRY` found no dated local copy to band under any of them (see below) — building bands for data that doesn't exist locally would be fabricating a surface, not implementing the policy.
- **What changed:**
  - `freshness.RECENCY_BANDS` gains `incois_osf_ww3`/`incois_osf_hycom` (DAILY, 3/7 d — same as PFZ).
  - `ocean_analytics.nearest_osf_point_forecast` tags each product (`wave`/`ocean`) with `recency()` against its own `forecast_time`, so WW3 and HYCOM carry independent ages rather than one shared stamp.
  - `analytics_loaders.load_stormglass_cache_date` reads a cached Stormglass file's real `meta.start`; `ocean_analytics.predict_tides` now reports that date as `source_provenance.acquisition_timestamp` when it falls back to the Stormglass rung, instead of the query time. The SOI-table path is untouched — it is computed fresh every call, so "now" was already honest there.
  - `frontend/app/voyage/page.tsx`'s Berthing window panel shows the existing `SourceChip` component (already used on the same page for route-layer provenance) when `tide.fell_back` is true, carrying the Stormglass dataset label and its real acquisition date.
  - `docs/ORCA_Stale_Data_Policy.md`: §4's tracker rows marked done; new §7 (OSF WW3/HYCOM), §8 (why Stormglass/SOI are fixed rather than banded), §9 (why `mosdac_nrt_sst/_chl/_wind`, `nasa_ocean_color`, `gfw_ais`, `bhuvan_wms` need no code — `mosdac_nrt_sst/_chl` are the identical files under a second registry id, `nasa_ocean_color`/`bhuvan_wms`/`gfw_ais` all return `held_locally: False` catalog entries, and `mosdac_nrt_wind`'s one real consumer is the already-covered wind map layer).
- **Verification:** `pytest tests/unit/test_ocean_analytics.py tests/unit/test_analytics_routes.py` 37 passed (existing tests extended with band/acquisition-timestamp assertions, no new test functions needed); full backend suite `pytest -q` 718 passed, 2 skipped; `eslint app/voyage/page.tsx` clean. **Live-verified** on a freshly restarted backend: `nearest_osf_point_forecast(8.80, 78.14)` returned `wave`/`ocean` each with `band: "fresh"`, `age_days: 1` off their own `forecast_time`; `GET /api/tides?lat=22.98&lon=70.22` (Kandla, a station with no SOI table but a real Stormglass cache) returned `fell_back: true` with `acquisition_timestamp: "2026-09-23T00:00:00Z"` — the cache's real date, not the query day (2026-09-24).
- **Remarks:** the frontend `SourceChip` on `/voyage` was reused as-is rather than checked with a Playwright click-through of the live map — the map view in this build has no pannable/searchable UI to reach Kandla precisely by coordinate, and the user asked to verify that surface themselves this session rather than have it automated. The `/api/tides` and backend-agent verification above went through the real route with real query parameters, not hand-picked internals.

- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **What changed:**
  - **C0.1:** `/ask` sends the access token on both `/query` EventSource URLs. `get_optional_user` reads it as a fallback when there is no header. `_claim_chat` adopts a `sessions` row that has no owner, and a row owned by someone else still returns 404. The frontend stops retrying a 404 and says "Couldn't save this chat" once.
  - **C0.2:** every tier is an ordered chain of providers with one time budget: the configured model, then Groq if any key is set, then `gemini-flash-lite-latest`, then (for the mid tier only) the local model, if this machine has one. The facts paragraph replaces the bare verdict line as the last resort, and the UI no longer hides the Response. Out-of-scope, needs-place and out-of-range replies are written by a model, and greetings get a greeting. Answers written without a model are never cached.
