Written up in the plan's F4 with the class fix.
    - On `HEAD`: ruff 14, mypy 10 and pyrefly 8 errors, plus `check:i18n` failing on the merge's untranslated `profile.*` and `watches.*` keys.
  - **Not done:** the reset and "speak to me in <language>" confirmations are still fixed text. They are command acknowledgements rather than answers, and are not in C0's list. The distress path stays deterministic on purpose.

### [2026-09-25] Open defects found during the stale-data and chatbot C0 sessions — not yet resolved

- **What this is:** every error or bug found during the 2026-09-24 sessions (stale-data policy rollout, chatbot plan, Phase C0) that is still unresolved, per the user's request to list them. Each item was observed or measured, not assumed. Nothing here has been fixed yet. Items are ordered by severity.
- **By:** Claude (Opus 5.5).
- **Commit:** — (list only; no code changed by this entry)
- **Open defects:**

  1. **False NO_GO on every question that names no place, and false breach/caution near the coast (safety-relevant; highest priority).** Since the Phase 5 merge, `geospatial.nearest_boundary_line()` (P5.5) measures to the nearest of all 32 features in `india_maritime_boundary_lines.geojson`. That includes 6 "Straight baseline" and 7 "Connection line" features, which are India's own coastal baselines with no neighbouring state (`eez2` empty).
     - The pilot default position `DEFAULT_LAT, DEFAULT_LON = 8.80, 78.30` (`orca/data/loaders.py:107`) reads **0.733 nm, `DANGER`**. So every question that names no place is answered NO_GO. Before the merge, the same position read 47.6 nm.
     - Kochi reads 2.5 nm, `CAUTION`.
     - A plain tide question at Thoothukudi raised the full-screen "Imminent Boundary or MPA Breach" takeover.
     - The same function feeds `graph.py` (the verdict), `sentinel.py` (watches), `voyage.py` (routing) and `geospatial_routes.py`.
     - Class fix: only consider lines that separate India from another state.

  2. **A named inland place is answered at the pilot default.** `sea conditions near Delhi` is treated as "this question names no place" and answered in the Gulf of Mannar; only the model's wording (when it runs) says Delhi is inland. A place that is named but not in the coastal gazetteer should be a `NEEDS_PLACE` guard. With defect 1, that answer is also a false NO_GO. Tracked as F4 in `docs/ORCA_Chatbot_Response_Plan.md`.

  3. **The OSF forecast age bands never reach the written answer.** `graph.py`'s reporting node passes Ocean Analytics only `tide`, `nearest_pfz`, `sector_status`, `pfz_persistence` and `productivity_diagnosis`, so `osf_point_forecast` (with its `age_days`/`band`/`expired`) is visible on the Reasoning page only. Wave height and wind now do reach the prompt, through `risk_assessment.readings` (added in C0). Tracked as F1.

  4. **Two fixed-text replies still bypass the model**, against the chatbot plan's rule that every prompt gets a model-written reply:
     - the "reset" confirmation (`session_memory.RESET_CONFIRMATION`);
     - the "speak to me in <language>" confirmation (`api/main.py`).

  5. **CI is red on `main` from the Phase 5 merge** (present on `HEAD`, none in files C0 changed):
     - ruff: 14 errors, including 2 `ISC004` in `graph.py`.
     - mypy: 10 errors in 7 files (`ops_routes.py`, `voyages_routes.py`, `sentinel_runtime.py`, `ocean_analytics.py`, `intent_actions.py`, `dispatcher.py`, `voyages_repo.py`).
     - pyrefly: 8 errors.
     - `npm run check:i18n`: fails, because the 9 non-English dictionaries lack the merge's `profile.*` and `watches.*` keys.

  6. **Teammates' databases cannot apply migration `008` with `migrate.sh`** until they pull `007a_notification_status_held.sql` (008 adds an enum value and uses it in the same transaction). Until it is applied, registration and sign-in fail with `users.typical_departure_hour does not exist`. Fixed in the tree and applied to this machine's database; each teammate must still run `infra/db/migrate.sh`.

  7. **Leftover rows and ids from the chat-save bug.**
     - 11 `sessions` rows created ownerless by `/query` since `721d07d` hold no turns. Those answers were never stored and cannot be recovered. The rows are left in place for the user to decide (agents do not delete data).
     - A browser that still holds an unsaved "current chat" id gets one `GET /api/chats/{id}` 404 on load. The frontend should drop an unknown current-chat id quietly.

  8. **The Critic shows "degraded, Low data" during a Gemini outage.** The reasoning tier's chain has no local rung by design, so with Gemini (and no Groq key) unavailable, the Critic span reads degraded even when the answer itself was written by the local model. Not a wrong answer, but a misleading span.

  9. **Frontend warnings:**
     - `tsc --noEmit` reports stale `.next/types/validator.ts` errors (`Route` vs `LayoutRoutes`; a missing `app/safety/layout.js`).
     - `/ask` logs `clusterMaxZoom` "Integer expected".

  10. **Translation quality of the two new i18n keys.** Bhashini turned the em dash in `chatTurn.writtenWithoutModel` into an unspaced hyphen in every language (e.g. Hindi "…लिखा गया-किसी…"). A native reader should review.

- **Open checks (not defects):**
  - The Groq default model `llama-3.3-70b-versatile` is unverified for 2026-09; check Groq's model list once keys are in `.env`.
  - The user is still to test the stale-data rollout on `/ask` and `/voyage` themselves.

### [2026-09-25] Fix 1/10 — false NO_GO / breach alert from India's own coastal baselines

- **By:** Claude (Sonnet 5).

- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **What changed:** `nearest_boundary_line` now excludes the 8 of 32 features in `india_maritime_boundary_lines.geojson` that are not a line between India and another state — 6 "Straight baseline" + 2 "200 NM" features, `territory2: null` in the source data. `load_boundary_lines()` is untouched and still returns all 32; the map layer (`geospatial_routes.map_layers`) still draws every line, baselines included, for context. Only the nearest-line lookup that feeds `risk_assessment`'s verdict, `sentinel.py`'s watches and `voyage.py`'s routing excludes them.
- **Verification:** confirmed the exact failure first — `nearest_boundary_line(8.80, 78.30)` (the pilot default) returned `India Straight Baseline`, 0.733 nm, before the fix. After: `Sri Lanka - India`, 45.1 nm, `CLEAR`; Kochi now resolves to `Maldives - India`, 148.7 nm, `CLEAR` (was `India Straight Baseline`, 2.5 nm). `pytest tests/unit/test_geospatial.py tests/unit/test_sentinel.py` → 48 passed, including the new regression test. `python -m orca.agents.geospatial` (module self-check) fails at an unrelated, pre-existing assertion (`wind["acquisition_date"] < "2026-09-03"`, a hardcoded date past today) — confirmed present on `HEAD` via `git stash` before this fix, so not a regression from this change. ruff and mypy clean on both changed files.
- **Remarks:** the 47.6 nm figure recorded for the pilot default in the 2026-09-24 chatbot-plan entry (before the merge introduced this bug) does not exactly match the 45.1 nm this fix measures — both are plausible for "the real international boundary distance", and the small difference is not chased further; the class of bug (measuring to India's own baseline) is what mattered and is gone. Defect 1 of 10 from the previous entry is now resolved; 9 remain.

### [2026-09-25] Fix 2/10 — a named inland place is refused, not silently answered at the pilot default

- **Implements:** defect 2 of the 2026-09-25 open-defects list, per the user's "fix them one by one".
- **By:** Claude (Sonnet 5).
- **Files:** `backend/orca/data/loaders.py` (`inland_place_name`, `_inland_place_names`, `_COASTAL_STATES`, `_GENERIC_DIRECTIONS`), `backend/orca/place_resolution.py` (wired into `resolve_or_ask`; self-check), `backend/tests/unit/test_place_resolution.py`, `backend/tests/unit/test_query_coverage.py`.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **What changed:** `resolve_or_ask` now checks, after the coastal gazetteer/tide/port tables and the typo-correction pass have already found nothing, whether the text names a real Indian place that is simply not on the coast. The check is offline and needs no new data: it reads the Census 2011 district shapefile already on disk for `geospatial.district_at_point` (641 districts, 34 states), keeps the districts and states of the 21 landlocked states/UTs (a fixed geographic fact, not a per-place rule), and excludes district names that are bare compass directions ("East", "West", "North" — several of NCT of Delhi's own districts are named this way, and a whole-word match on "west" would otherwise fire on ordinary marine wording like "wind from the west"). A match returns the existing `unresolvable` outcome with a disclosure naming the place, so it takes the same `NEEDS_PLACE` refusal path `graph.py`'s guard already gives "I don't know where that is" — no new outcome type, no new frontend case.
- **Verification:**
  - Unit: found and fixed a case-sensitivity bug during development (district names were stored title-cased but matched against lowercased query text, so nothing matched at all) before it reached a test. `pytest tests/unit/test_place_resolution.py tests/unit/test_query_coverage.py tests/unit/test_planning.py tests/unit/test_conversation_context.py tests/unit/test_sentinel.py` → 154 passed, including 3 new tests (Delhi is refused correctly; a real coastal match is never shadowed; "wind from the west"/"swell direction east"/"veering south" are never mistaken for a district) and 4 new corpus entries in `test_query_coverage.py`'s `CANNOT_PLACE` bucket (Delhi, Jaipur, Lucknow, Chandigarh). `python -m orca.place_resolution` self-check extended and passing. ruff and mypy clean on both changed files.
  - End-to-end through the real graph (`build_graph().invoke(...)`, not just the guard function): `sea conditions near Delhi` → `query_outcome: NEEDS_PLACE`, response "Delhi is inland — I only cover conditions at sea off India's coast. Name a coastal port or landing centre, or send your position, and I will answer for it." (written through C0's guard-reply model, faithful to the guard's own text).
  - **Live, through `/ask` in the browser, signed in as `agent@orca.test`:** `sea conditions near Delhi` and `is it safe to go out tomorrow near Jaipur` both rendered the "WHICH PLACE DO YOU MEAN?" refusal card with the correct place name and no marine numbers anywhere on the card. Ordinary coastal queries (Kanyakumari, Kollam, Kozhikode, Ratnagiri, Bay of Bengal, Kerala) confirmed unaffected, both directly against `inland_place_name` and through the existing 60-query coverage corpus.
- **Remarks:** deliberately scoped to whole landlocked states/UTs and their districts, not "how far is any given district from the coast" — an inland district of a *coastal* state (Coimbatore in Tamil Nadu) is not caught, and is out of scope for this fix; flagged as a possible follow-up, not attempted here (no ready offline distance-to-coast data). Defect 2 of 10 from the 2026-09-25 open-defects entry is now resolved; 8 remain.

### [2026-09-25] Fix 4/10 — the reset and language-switch confirmations are worded by a model

- **Implements:** defect 4 of the 2026-09-25 open-defects list (previously noted as deliberately out of C0's scope; the user asked to fix it too), per "fix them one by one".
- **By:** Claude (Sonnet 5).
- **Files:** `backend/orca/agents/reporting.py` (`write_confirmation_reply`), `backend/orca/api/main.py` (`_reset_stream`, `_language_change_stream`), `backend/tests/unit/test_response_guarantee.py`, `backend/tests/unit/test_administrative_confirmations.py` (new).
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **What changed:** both of the two remaining fixed-text replies now go through a model, same fallback discipline as every other guard reply (chatbot plan C0.2d): if no provider answers, or the model's reply drops a figure that wasn't in the required text, the caller gets the exact original sentence, unchanged from before this fix.
  - `_reset_stream`: `session_memory.RESET_CONFIRMATION` is now rephrased by `reporting.write_confirmation_reply` rather than sent verbatim every time.
  - `_language_change_stream`'s confirmation-only branch (no earlier turn in the chat to re-render): the English acknowledgment is rephrased the same way, **before** the existing translation step, not instead of it — the target is the language the user asked to switch to, not the language they typed the command in, so this could not reuse `write_guard_reply`'s "reply in the message's own language" framing; a new, narrower `write_confirmation_reply` (no user message, no small-talk branching, no refusal-shaped system prompt) was added instead of forcing a mismatched fit. The branch that re-renders a real earlier answer into the new language was already model-driven before this and is untouched.
  - Both SSE frames now carry `response_engine`, so the frontend's existing "written without a model" label applies here too if every provider is down.
- **Verification:**
  - Unit: 3 new tests for `write_confirmation_reply` (rephrased and not verbatim; a reply that adds a figure is discarded; falls back to the required text with no model reachable) — same pattern as the existing `write_guard_reply` tests.
  - New file `test_administrative_confirmations.py`, 4 tests, over the **real `/query` route** (`TestClient(app)`, not a hand-fed function call): a reset both clears the session and is worded by a mocked model; a reset with every provider down still confirms with the original sentence; a language switch with no earlier turn is worded by a model and still translated for delivery; the same switch with no model reachable still confirms correctly. `pytest tests/unit/test_administrative_confirmations.py tests/unit/test_response_guarantee.py` → 38 passed. `pytest tests/unit/test_conversation_context.py tests/unit/test_phase2_reasoning.py` (existing reset/session coverage) → 104 passed, unaffected. ruff clean; mypy reports zero new errors in either changed file (same pre-existing findings elsewhere, unchanged).
  - **Live, through `/ask` in the browser, signed in as `agent@orca.test`** (after the Postgres port collision below was resolved): "forget everything" produced "Done, I've cleared our conversation so your next question starts fresh, just name the place and time again if they matter." — paraphrased, not the literal `RESET_CONFIRMATION` string. A new chat's first message "speak to me in tamil" produced "புரிந்தது-இனிமேல் நான் இந்த மொழியைப் பயன்படுத்துவேன்." ("Understood — I'll use this language from now on"), confirming the English base sentence was rephrased *before* translation (a literal translation of the original fixed sentence would open with "முடிந்தது"/"Done", not "புரிந்தது"/"Understood").
- **Unrelated environment issue found and fixed along the way — a port collision, not a code defect:** signing in fresh through the browser initially failed at `/api/login` with `password authentication failed for user "orca"`, reproduced independently with a direct `psql` connection using the exact `.env` `DATABASE_URL`. Cause: two unrelated Docker Compose projects both publish host port 5433 — ORCA's own `orca-postgres-1` (`orca`/`orca`/`orca`, `postgis/postgis:16-3.4-alpine`) and a different project's `backend-postgres-1` (`plant`/`plant`/`plant_warehouse`, `timescale/timescaledb`). `orca-postgres-1` had exited; with it down, the other project's container answered on 5433 and correctly rejected ORCA's credentials, which were never meant for it. Fixed at the user's direction: `docker stop backend-postgres-1`, then `docker compose up -d postgres` to recreate `orca-postgres-1` (a prior plain `docker start` left it with no port published at all — recreating via compose was needed to restore the `5433:5432` mapping; the named volume `orca_pgdata` was untouched, so no data was lost). Confirmed fixed: direct `psql` connects, and the full backend suite returned to exactly the 3 known pre-existing failures (835 passed, up from 828 — the new tests from this defect and defect 2/3 — vs. 36 failed + 25 errors while the wrong container was serving the port).
- **Remarks:** deliberately did not touch the "re-render the last answer in the new language" branch of `_language_change_stream` — it already calls a real model to re-translate/re-render the previous answer and was never the fixed-text case defect 4 named. The reset flow's agent-trace panel showed all 12 agents as "done" in the browser despite `_reset_stream` sending zero `agent_span` events — looks like a pre-existing frontend display quirk (not something this fix touches or introduces; not chased further). Defect 4 of 10 is now resolved; 6 remain.

### [2026-09-26] Fix — "current time" / "current location" no longer misfire the marine pipeline

- **Implements:** a new finding from the user's own live testing (not one of the 10 numbered defects): asking "do you know whats current time is", then "do you know atleast current location", got "I don't have a clock" and then a narrative claiming the pilot default position off Thoothukudi as if it might be the caller's — even though the app's own header already shows an IST clock.
- **By:** Claude (Sonnet 5).
- **Files:** `backend/orca/agents/planning.py` (`_CURRENT_SELF_SENSE`, `is_self_context_question`), `backend/orca/agents/reporting.py` (`write_self_context_reply`), `backend/orca/graph/graph.py` (`out_of_scope_node`, `_self_context_facts`), `backend/tests/unit/test_planning.py`, `backend/tests/unit/test_phase2_reasoning.py`, `backend/tests/unit/test_response_guarantee.py`, `backend/tests/unit/test_self_context_questions.py` (new).
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **Root cause:** `planning._MARINE_VOCAB` lists bare "current" as marine vocabulary (an ocean current). "current time" and "current location" both contain that word, so `is_out_of_scope` misread them as marine questions and ran the full agent pipeline instead of refusing them — `resolve_or_ask` then found no place named and fell to the pilot default, and the model narrated that default as if it might be relevant to "your location", which it is not.
- **What changed:**
  - `is_out_of_scope` strips the bigram "current time/date/day/location/position" before the vocabulary test — only that sense; "current speed near Kochi" and "ocean current near Chennai" still match normally (verified both directions).
  - A new, narrow `is_self_context_question` recognises questions about ORCA's own operating context (the clock, the caller's own known position) as a distinct class from both a marine question and ordinary out-of-scope junk.
  - `out_of_scope_node` answers a self-context question from real facts instead of the generic refusal: the live server clock in IST, and — only when the browser sent a real GPS fix on **this** turn (`place_source == "gps_fix"`) — that position, explicitly labelled as information only, never a place to compute sea conditions at unless the caller names one or asks about it. A carried-over place or the regional default is never reported as "your location" — reporting either would be the exact "a wrong-but-confident position is the most dangerous output this system can produce" failure `place_resolution`'s own module docstring warns against.
  - The reply is written by a model (`reporting.write_self_context_reply`, same fallback-to-facts-verbatim and no-invented-figure discipline as every other guard reply), and rendered without the "Not something ORCA can answer" heading (`small_talk` forced `True` at the call site, not left to the model's own judgment, since this is never the "can't help" case that heading is for).
- **Verification:**
  - Unit: `is_out_of_scope`/`is_self_context_question` on both directions (self-context text refused for marine purposes; genuine "current speed"/"ocean current" queries unaffected). `write_self_context_reply`: rephrased and not verbatim; a reply that adds a figure discarded; falls back to the real facts with no model reachable. `graph._self_context_facts`: reports the clock and "no position shared" by default; reports a real GPS fix's coordinates only when `place_source == "gps_fix"`; never reports the regional default's coordinates as a position. `graph.out_of_scope_node`: answers a self-context question from real facts with `small_talk=True`; still refuses a genuine out-of-scope question (`tell me a joke`) with `small_talk=False`. New route-level file `test_self_context_questions.py` (5 tests) over the real `/query` route: current-time question dispatches 0 agents; current-location with no GPS fix never mentions the regional default's coordinates; current-location with a real (wet) GPS fix reports it, labelled as information only; a genuine ocean-current question still runs the marine pipeline; the no-model fallback still carries the real facts. `pytest tests/unit/test_self_context_questions.py tests/unit/test_administrative_confirmations.py tests/unit/test_planning.py tests/unit/test_phase2_reasoning.py tests/unit/test_response_guarantee.py tests/unit/test_query_coverage.py` → 221 passed. `pytest tests/e2e/test_graph.py` → 11 passed, 1 skipped, 1 failed — the same pre-existing `test_all_sources_down_still_returns_a_forced_low_data_verdict`, unaffected. ruff clean on every changed file (one pre-existing, unrelated `I001` finding in `test_phase2_reasoning.py`, confirmed present on `HEAD` via `git stash` at a different line number before this entry's insertions shifted it); mypy reports zero new errors.
  - **Live, through `/ask` in the browser, signed in as `agent@orca.test`:** "do you know whats current time is" → 0 agents dispatched, "The current time is 20:51 IST on 26 Sep 2026. No position has been shared for this question." "okay fine, do you know atleast current location? i wanted to ask some questions about it" → "I do not know your current location because no position has been shared with this question" — no Thoothukudi default, no false claim.
- **Remarks:** the browser does not send a GPS fix on every `/ask` turn today (only `/query`'s optional `fix_lat`/`fix_lon` params, populated when the frontend's own geolocation call succeeds) — most sessions, including this one's live check, have no fix to report, which is the honest "No position has been shared" answer rather than a defect.

### [2026-09-26] Fix — Gemini and Groq now actually use all 5 configured keys each

- **Implements:** the user's own question, asked mid-session — "don't we have 5 APIs each for Gemini and Groq? How are we mitigating this issue" — which turned up two real bugs in the C0.2/defect-fixing work itself, not a hypothetical concern.
- **By:** Claude (Sonnet 5).
- **Files:** `backend/orca/llm/registry.py` (`_numbered_keys`, `gemini_keys`, `GeminiProvider` rewritten to rotate), `backend/orca/llm/tiers.py` (`_GROQ_MODEL`), `.env.example`, `backend/tests/unit/test_response_guarantee.py`.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **What was actually happening, checked against the real `.env` (5 keys each, confirmed present):**
  - **Groq: zero keys were ever read.** `groq_keys()` matched only `GROQ_API_KEY` or `GROQ_API_KEY_N` (underscore required); `.env` has `GROQ_API_KEY1`..`GROQ_API_KEY5` (no underscore). `groq_keys()` returned `[]`, so every chain that was supposed to have Groq as its fast second rung silently had none — confirmed with a direct call before the fix.
  - **Gemini: only 1 of 5 keys was ever read.** `GeminiProvider` read a single `GEMINI_API_KEY`/`GOOGLE_API_KEY` and built one client; the other four sat completely unused. There was no per-key rotation at all, unlike Groq's (broken) attempt at one.
  - **The Groq default model (`llama-3.3-70b-versatile`) no longer exists** — a live call 404'd once real keys were available to check with (it was flagged as "unverified" in the 2026-09-25 open-defects list, since there were no working keys to test it with before now).
- **What changed:**
  - `_numbered_keys(prefix)` accepts both `KEYN` and `KEY_N`, deduplicates by value, and backs both `groq_keys()` and the new `gemini_keys()`.
  - `GeminiProvider` now builds one client per configured key and rotates to the next on a 429/`RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`/quota error — but deliberately **not** on a 503/"unavailable", since that is the model overloaded for everyone, not this key's quota, and no key swap fixes it; `tiers.py`'s own chain (a second model, then Groq, then local) is what a genuine outage needs.
  - Groq's default model is now `openai/gpt-oss-120b`, chosen by measuring the real Reporting prompt against every general-purpose model Groq's `/models` endpoint actually lists: `openai/gpt-oss-120b` 1.7 s, `openai/gpt-oss-20b` 1.4 s, `qwen/qwen3.8-27b` 0.3 s — `gpt-oss-120b` picked as the largest of the three, still far faster than every Gemini model measured the same day (best case 4.1 s).
- **Verification:**
  - Confirmed the bug directly against the real `.env` before fixing: `groq_keys()` → `[]` (5 keys present), `GeminiProvider().​_clients` → length 1 (5 keys present). After: `gemini_keys()` and `groq_keys()` both return all 5.
  - A real (non-mocked) Groq call against each of the three candidate models, timed on the actual Reporting prompt, all three keeping the verdict header intact.

### [2026-09-27] Fix — every LLM call gets the conversation and the caller's device position

- **Implements:** the user's direct request: "let our agents which are making LLM api calls behave like one, and not fed each prompt with 0 context window", and "pass my current location to the context … it should never be mistaken for a port unless explicitly mentioned in the prompt". Builds on the uncommitted 2026-09-26 work (`raw_fix` → `user_location.fix_lat/fix_lon`, the sessionStorage-cached geolocation hook), at the user's direction.
- **By:** Claude (Opus 5.5).
- **Files:** `backend/orca/agents/reporting.py` (`device_position_line`, `conversation_context`, `_context_block`; `write_guard_reply`, `write_self_context_reply`, `synthesize_narrative` take it), `backend/orca/agents/critic.py` (`_judge_prompt`, `run_critic_pass`, `run`), `backend/orca/agents/planning.py` (`_tier3_llm_fallback`), `backend/orca/graph/graph.py` (`_conversation_context`, `_guard_reply`, `out_of_scope_node`; two pre-existing ruff `ISC004` findings parenthesised), `backend/orca/api/main.py` (cache key), `backend/tests/unit/test_self_context_questions.py`.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **Root cause:** of the six model calls on the answer path, only `synthesize_narrative` saw the chat's recent turns. Guard replies (greetings, small talk, out-of-scope, needs-place, out-of-range) and the "where am I / what time is it" reply were written from the current message alone, so ORCA forgot what had been said one turn earlier. The Critic judged the narrative without the context it was written with, and Tier-3 intent classification saw only the single previous question. Separately, an inland device fix was dropped by `_usable_fix` for marine purposes and never reached any prompt, so the model did not know where the caller was.
- **What changed:**
  - One shared `reporting.conversation_context(session_history, user_location)`: the session's last `MAX_TURNS` (5) turns, plus, when the browser sent a fix this turn (land or sea), a "CALLER'S CURRENT POSITION" line stating it is NOT a port or a place the caller named and must not be named as one unless the caller's own message does.
  - Passed to every writer: narrative, guard replies, self-context reply, and the Critic's judge prompt (so references to the conversation or position are not flagged as unsourced). Tier-3 classification now sees every held question, oldest first, not only the last.
  - The no-invented-figure guards on guard/self-context replies now also accept figures present in that context (a coordinate quoted from the device position is not invented).
  - Place resolution is untouched: a fix is still only a marine answer position when it is at sea and the text names nowhere.
  - Cache/coalescing keys include the device fix, because an answer may now quote it and one caller's position must never be served to another.
  - Not changed: `write_confirmation_reply` (reset / language-switch acknowledgements; it rephrases one fixed sentence and has no user message to answer). Per-answer trimming (`ANSWER_CHARS`) deferred by the user.
- **Verification:** new route-level test `test_every_model_prompt_gets_the_conversation_and_the_device_position`: a prior Kochi turn plus an inland Bengaluru fix, then "hi". The prompt the model received carries the earlier question, `12.9716, 77.5946`, and the "NOT a port" label, and the outcome stays a guard reply. `tests/unit/test_self_context_questions.py` 7 passed. `tests/e2e/test_graph.py::test_all_sources_down_still_returns_a_forced_low_data_verdict` fails, and fails identically on a clean `HEAD` worktree (pre-existing, unrelated). ruff clean on every touched file.
- **Follow-up, same day — "what is the nearest port from my current location":** the user's live test from inland Bengaluru got the pilot default's port (Thoothukudi) as "the nearest port". New `reporting.nearest_port(lat, lon)` picks the closest entry in `loaders.port_coordinates()`, skipping regions (`is_region_name`) and open water (gulf/strait/sea/ocean), with the straight-line distance from the existing `distress._km_between`. `device_position_line` states it as a distance, never as where the caller is. Narrative rule 4 (location honesty) gains one exception: this nearest port may be named, first, when the user asks what is near them. Caught while checking live: an earlier wording made the model say the device was "near Thalassery" (253 km away) and "though not a port"; both fixed by rewording. Live on `/ask` (inland fix 12.9402, 77.4971): "The nearest port on record [is] Thalassery, about 253 km from your current device location in [a] straight line", with the readings labelled as the pilot default's. Ceiling: straight-line km over ORCA's cached-port list, not a road or sea route.

### [2026-09-27] Fix — a sector with no advisory is never narrated as "no fishing zones" (regression of the 2026-09-24 Gujarat fix)

- **Implements:** the user's report that the Mangrol answer said "no designated fishing zone (PFZ) within 150 km … outside any regulated zone", a Gujarat gap they remembered as already fixed. They asked that it never happen for any region.
- **By:** Claude (Opus 5.5).
- **Files:** `backend/orca/agents/ocean_analytics.py` (`NearestPFZ.beyond_reach`, `nearest_pfz`, `run`), `backend/orca/agents/reporting.py` (deterministic PFZ line, narrative rule 9), `backend/orca/agents/critic.py` (facts), `frontend/app/zones/page.tsx`, `frontend/app/components/PersonaAnswerMatrix.tsx`, `backend/tests/unit/test_ocean_analytics.py`, `backend/tests/unit/test_place_chip.py`.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **Why it came back:** the earlier Gujarat fix (see the PFZ entry: `/api/zones` at Veraval returning the nearest real advisory 189 km ESE, out of sector) relied on `nearest_pfz` searching without a distance limit. Later the same day, the stale-data entry added `PFZ_MAX_REACH_KM = 150` to stop the 906 km Betul answer for Rameswaram. Gujarat has no advisory of its own, and its nearest (now 268 km) fell past the cap. So `nearest_pfz` returned `found: false` for every Gujarat and Andaman position, on `/ask` and on `/zones` alike. The Gujarat fix was only ever verified live, and the cap's test used a coast with zones nearby, so nothing failed. Narrative rule 9 only told the model to give the cloud-cover reason when the sector had a `latest_advisory`, and Gujarat has none. The model therefore turned "not found" into "no zones" and invented "regulated zone".
- **What changed:**
  - `nearest_pfz` always returns the nearest advisory ORCA holds. Past `max_km` it is flagged `beyond_reach` rather than dropped. `found: false` now means only that no advisory is held anywhere. The Rameswaram/Betul case stays fixed because the far zone is labelled out of reach, never presented as nearby.
  - The deterministic answer line says "nothing is held within 150 km, so this is beyond a day trip".
  - Rule 9 has three parts. A data-gap sector always gets its reason, whether or not it has a latest advisory. A gap is never "no fishing zones". A PFZ is an INCOIS advisory, never a regulated, designated or restricted area.
  - The Critic's facts carry `nearest_fishing_zone_beyond_reach_km` and `todays_sector_advisory_gap`.
  - `/zones` shows "No advisory within 150 km — this is the nearest ORCA holds", and the answer card's PFZ tile appends "none within 150 km".
- **Verification:**
  - Unit tests. `test_nearest_pfz_is_capped_at_reach…` now expects a far zone to come back flagged, and an empty store to be `found: false`. New `test_a_data_gap_anywhere_is_never_narrated_as_no_fishing_zones` covers six coasts: Mangrol, Rameswaram, Kochi, Puri, Sundarbans and Port Blair.
  - Route-level test. New `test_a_cloud_covered_sector_with_nothing_in_reach_still_answers_honestly` hits `/api/zones` and `/query` with the model off; the answer must contain "cloud cover" and "beyond a day trip".
  - Sweep. All 248 gazetteer places return a zone (0 not found). 42 are beyond reach, all in Gujarat, Maharashtra-north, the Andamans and open ocean.
  - Live. The Gujarat question, then the Mangrol chip, on `/ask` answered: "The nearest potential fishing zone on record is located 268 km ESE of Mangrol, near Gholvad, though that advisory is a few days old…". The card disclosure reads "no fishing advisory for your sector today (cloud cover), and the nearest is 268 km away (ESE) — not a realistic day trip."
  - ruff and ESLint are clean.
- **Remarks:** the model's narrative in that live run left the cloud-cover reason to the disclosure line above it rather than repeating it. The fact is on the card either way.

### [2026-09-27] Bhashini — every service checked against the docs; TLD fixed, ALD + TN wired, mic language picker removed

- **Implements:** follow-up to `P3` voice/language (Bhashini ingress and egress), at the user's request to wire the three granted keys properly.
- **By:** Claude (Opus 5.5).
- **Files:** `backend/orca/agents/bhashini.py`, `backend/orca/agents/voice.py`, `backend/orca/api/voice_routes.py`, `backend/tests/unit/test_voice.py`, `frontend/app/components/VoiceInput.tsx`, `.env.example`.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **Checked live, already correct, unchanged:** the ULCA config → Dhruva compute flow (config returns an inference key equal to `BHASHINI_INFERENCE_API_KEY`); ASR with inline `vad` + `denoiser` pre-processors and `itn` + `punctuation` post-processors; NMT; TTS; transliteration.
- **Fixed — TLD:** text language detection was requested through the pipeline config, which rejects it ("TaskType is not valid": pipeline `64392f96daac500b55c543cd` only offers asr, translation, transliteration, tts), so it always failed and fell back to script detection. It now goes straight to compute with the documented service ID `bhashini/indic-lang-detection-all`. Romanized Tamil "naalai kadalukku pogalama" now detects as `ta`.
- **Added — ALD:** `bhashini.detect_spoken_language()` using `bhashini/iitmandi/audio-lang-detection/gpu` (right on ta/kn/hi/bn/en in probes; `bhashini/ald` said ml→ta at 0.999 and was rejected). Browser WebM needs `audioFormat: "webm"` in the config or Dhruva returns a bare 500 — not in the docs. `voice.spoken_language()` takes ALD's answer at score ≥ 0.9 when it is a language ORCA serves, else the navbar language; ALD failure logs and uses the navbar language. Both the final transcript and the live captions use it, so a Kannada speaker with an English UI is captioned in Kannada instead of in invented English (it was "Tomorrow morning, I will go to the sea." before).
- **Added — TN:** TTS sends `preProcessors: ["text-normalization"]`; measured better on English numbers/units, neutral on ta/hi. All 10 languages synthesise.
- **Frontend:** the spoken-language dropdown beside the mic is gone. The navbar language is sent as the hint; the language Bhashini reports only picks the transcript's script font.
- **Not wired, deliberately:** NER (no caller needs it); standalone ITN and TN (no documented service ID answers — both work inline as processors above); the Udyat key (`ulcaApiKey does not exist` on ULCA, 401/403 on Dhruva); translation `glossary` post-processor (Udyat-only).
- **Verification:** `python -m orca.agents.bhashini` live self-check passes (TLD romanized Tamil → ta, ALD on Tamil TTS → ta at 1.000). `pytest tests/unit/test_voice.py tests/unit/test_language.py`: 33 passed, 1 skipped (new parametrized ALD-vs-hint test). ruff and mypy clean. Through `POST /voice/transcribe` with browser-recorded WebM and `language_hint=en`: kn, ta, hi final transcripts in their own scripts with the matching `detected_language`; partials the same, in 2.0–3.1 s (ALD adds a round trip per caption). `npx tsc --noEmit` shows only the 3 pre-existing stale `.next/types` errors; `npm run lint` clean.
- **Known limit:** ML ALD is weakest on Malayalam (IIT Mandi said te at 0.75 — below the threshold, so the navbar language wins). A Malayalam speaker with a non-Malayalam UI may still need to switch the navbar language.

### [2026-09-27] Fix — a place named in a non-English question is resolved (Kannada "ಕೊಚ್ಚಿ" was answered at the pilot default)

- **Implements:** bug fix, P3 language ingress × P1.2 place resolution.
- **By:** Claude (Opus 5.5).
- **Files:** `backend/orca/api/main.py`, `backend/orca/agents/language.py`, `backend/orca/state.py`, `backend/orca/graph/graph.py`, `backend/orca/data/loaders.py`, `backend/tests/unit/test_vernacular_place.py` (new).
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **Symptom:** "ಕೊಚ್ಚಿ ಹತ್ತಿರ ಇರುವ ಮೀನುಗಾರಿಕೆ ವಲಯಗಳು ಯಾವುವು?" was answered "I do not have data for Kochi; … the default pilot region (8.8, 78.3)", while the English question got the Kochi PFZ answer.
- **Root cause:** `/query` resolves the place (and the vessel, the reset phrase, the "speak to me in…" command, the priority lane) *before* the graph, on the raw text, with English-only parsers. Translation only happened later, in `language_ingress`. The gazetteer holds Tamil-script names, which is why Tamil worked and Kannada/Hindi/every other script did not. The time guard and self-context check in the graph run before ingress too, on raw text.
- **Fix (the class, not Kochi):** `/query` now detects and translates once, up front (`language.query_language` + `language.english_query`, the same functions ingress uses). Place resolution tries the raw text first and the English translation when the raw text names nothing; vessel, reset, language-command and priority-lane checks see the English as well. The result rides in `state["pretranslated"]`, so ingress reuses it rather than paying for a second NMT call, and it seeds `normalized_english_query` so the pre-ingress guards (time, self-context) read English.
- **Also:** Bhashini renders ರಾಮೇಶ್ವರಂ as "Rameshwaram", which the resolver took for a typo and answered with "did you mean Rameswaram?". Added a Latin-variant row (`_LATIN_VARIANTS` in `loaders.py`).
- **Verification:** own backend on :8001, real `/query`: the Kannada Kochi question → place `kochi`, `PFZ_NEAREST`, answer "41.5 km WNW of Kochi … Kuzhuppilly … 19 September" (same facts as English); Hindi "कोच्चि के पास…" → `kochi`, same facts; Kannada "ರಾಮೇಶ್ವರಂನಲ್ಲಿ ಇಂದು ಹವಾಮಾನ ಹೇಗಿದೆ?" → Rameswaram coordinates, `CONDITIONS`. New `test_vernacular_place.py` (translated place resolves; no place in either text still falls to the default). Backend suite: 871 passed; the only failures are the same timing-dependent `test_notifications` / GDACS-breaker ones as the previous entry, which pass in isolation. ruff and mypy clean.
- **Cost:** one NMT call before the graph for a non-English question; ingress no longer makes its own, so it is moved, not added.

(every fisherman answer carried it, whatever was asked) and "DATA LIMITED — VERIFY LOCALLY" with no reason, which read as doubt about the safety check.
- **Boundary:** the hardcoded fisherman/unresolved sentences are removed; the IMBL readouts on the navigator/researcher/unresolved cards render only when the routing rows include `ZONES_TO_AVOID`. Backend-side, `reporting_run` hands the narrator (LLM and facts paragraph) the boundary distance only on `ZONES_TO_AVOID` or a non-GO verdict (a boundary CAUTION must still be explained), so it is said by the narrative — in the user's language — or not at all. The critical-condition banner for DANGER/INSIDE is untouched (life-safety).
- **Data limited:** the label was honest — `ocean_analytics` is LOW_DATA because the newest Kerala PFZ advisory is 8 days old and today's sector is cloud-covered. `reporting_run` now emits `confidence_reason`: the rationale of each LOW_DATA contributor (minus the trace's `[score …]` suffix). `language_egress` translates it with the answer; the card shows it under the label. `ocean_analytics._worst` now names only the inputs that set the grade (it listed every input, so the reason could not say which one), and the PFZ rationale says "N days old, not today's".
- **Verification:** own backend on :8001, real `/ask` page as agent@orca.test: the Malayalam Kochi PFZ question shows no boundary line and, under the label, "41.5 km (WNW)-ലെ INCOIS PFZ ഉപദേശം, 2026-09-19 (8 … ഇന്നത്തെതല്ല)". `/query`: Kannada "ಕೊಚ್ಚಿಯಿಂದ ಸಮುದ್ರ ಗಡಿ ಎಷ್ಟು ದೂರ? …" → `ZONES_TO_AVOID`, Kannada answer states 146.6 nautical miles; English Rameswaram boundary question → 13 nm. New tests (4). ruff, mypy, tsc (only stale `.next/types`), eslint clean. Full suite with the Gemini keys blanked (CI conditions): 876 passed; the 2 failures are `test_notifications` reading 19 rows from the local Postgres (CI's DB is empty) and the flaky GDACS breaker.

### [2026-09-28] Diagnosis only — prompt routing lets word lists refuse or misread prompts; revamp planned

- **By:** Claude (Opus 5.5).
- **Files:** `docs/ORCA_Prompt_Routing_Revamp.md` (new). No code changed.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **Symptoms:**
  - Slow and inconsistent "which place?" replies (16–23 s).
  - The Karnataka reply lost the "whole coastline" intro.
  - "pfzs near ktaka" was refused as out of scope.
  - "wats time now" was refused.
- **Root causes:**
  - Gemini `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` is returning 503/504. `_ChainClient` retries it and re-tries it on every call, with no cooldown.
  - `write_guard_reply`'s prompt lets the model drop the reason sentence.
  - Exact-match lists (`_MARINE_VOCAB`, `_SELF_CONTEXT_PHRASES`, `_TEMPORAL_PATTERNS`, …) decide scope, intent and time before any model reads the prompt. Probing also found missed distress paraphrases, and relative dates answered silently for the wrong day.
- **Priority: CRITICAL.** The user set this on 2026-09-28: the chatbot must read and answer prompts the way a general LLM assistant does (typos, context changes, message history, conversational replies). This work blocks all other feature fixes and new features. See section 0 of the doc.
- **Decided:** a model reads every prompt, and code validates what it extracts. Lists become the fast path and the offline fallback only.
- **Open work, in the order agreed:**
  1. Reassign the Groq and Gemini keys properly and add a per-rung cooldown.
  2. The Understand call and its validation.
  3. System prompt changes: the Understand prompt, the guard-reply prompt, and a review of the rest.
  4. A distress model check that can only escalate.
  5. Delete the synonym and word-overlap fallback.
  6. A messy-prompt test file.
- **Details:** the evidence, the list inventory and the plan are in `docs/ORCA_Prompt_Routing_Revamp.md`.

### [2026-09-29] LLM chain — Groq/Gemini reassigned per agent; failing model skipped for 2 min; "LLM calls" counts answers

- **Implements:** revamp item 1 and the section 8 counter fix in `docs/ORCA_Prompt_Routing_Revamp.md`.
- **By:** Claude (Opus 5.5).
- **Files:** `.env` (the three `ORCA_LLM_*_CHAIN` lines; no key values touched), `.env.example`, `backend/orca/llm/tiers.py`, `backend/orca/api/main.py`, `frontend/app/ask/ReasoningEvidence.tsx`, `frontend/app/ask/ChatTurn.tsx`, `frontend/app/ask/useAskThread.ts`, `backend/tests/unit/test_response_guarantee.py`, `backend/tests/unit/test_phase2_reasoning.py`.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **Assignment:**
  - Planning (cheap): `groq gpt-oss-20b` → `gemini flash-lite-latest` → `groq gpt-oss-120b`.
  - Reporting and all guard replies (mid): `groq gpt-oss-120b` → `gemini flash-lite-latest` → `gemini 3.5-flash-lite` → local `gemma4:e4b`.
  - Critic (reasoning): `gemini flash-lite-latest` → `groq gpt-oss-20b`, so a different company's model reviews Groq's answer.
  - Probe that day: Groq 0.4–0.7 s, Gemini flash-lite-latest 0.7–1.2 s, 3.5-flash-lite 0.8–6.1 s after a day of 503/504s.
- **Cooldown:** a hosted rung that fails is skipped by every call for 120 s (`_COOLDOWN_S`). It goes to the back of the order rather than out of it, so it is still the last resort. A success clears it. A 503 is retried on the same rung only when no healthy rung comes after it. The local rung is never cooled.
- **Counter:** `reset_llm_call_count()` is now `[answered, failed]`. The payload adds `llm_failed_attempts`, and the routing line reads "N LLM calls · M failed attempts". The "12 LLM calls" turn had made about four calls.
- **Verification:** own backend on :8001, real `/query`:
  - "wheres the nearest fishing zone near kochi": 13.8 s, 4 calls, 0 failed. Reporting on Groq in 1.4–1.6 s, Critic on Gemini in 2.1 s. The rest of the time is the data agents (weather 4.1 s, ocean 3.2 s).
  - "is it safe to go to sea tomorrow near rameswaram": 12.6 s, 2 calls, 0 failed.
  - "hi": 4.0 s.
  - Checks:
    - 4 new or updated chain tests.
    - Full suite with keys and chains blanked (CI conditions): 879 passed. The 2 failures are the known `test_notifications` local-DB one and the flaky GDACS breaker.
    - ruff, mypy, eslint and the tiers self-check are clean. tsc shows only the stale `.next/types` errors.
- **Noticed, not fixed:** the backend log shows `intent embeddings unavailable (ImportError: … is_torch_npu_available …)`. Tier 2 has been silently running on word overlap on this machine. This is relevant to revamp item 5.

### [2026-09-30] `ORCA_PRELOAD_LOCAL_MODELS=0` switch; Render free (512 MB) measured, does not fit

- **By:** Claude (Opus 5.5).
- **Files:** `backend/orca/api/main.py`, `.env.example`, `docs/ORCA_Deployment.md`.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **Change:** `_lifespan` wraps every startup warm-up in `if ORCA_PRELOAD_LOCAL_MODELS != "0"`. The warm-ups cover IndicTrans2, faster-whisper, MMS-TTS, the e5 intent model and the Ollama model. The default (blank or `1`) is unchanged. With `0`, each model still loads lazily on first use.
- **Verification:** real `backend/Dockerfile` image, `docker run --memory=512m --memory-swap=512m`.
  - Default: OOM-killed (`OOMKilled=true`, exit 137) about 15 s after startup. Uncapped it measured 824 MB after warm-up and 864 MB after two queries.
  - Switch at `0`: startup survives at about 175 MB. The first query ("hi") was then OOM-killed, because the e5 intent model loads lazily on the first query (165 → 677 MB uncapped).
  - Switch at `0` with e5 also unloadable: 5 queries survived, most of them served from Redis cache. An uncached romanised-Hindi safety query near Rameswaram was OOM-killed while the data agents were fetching and processing, with no model loading. Uncapped with `fresh=true`, one safety query used 633 MB and memory settled around 880 MB after 4 queries.
  - `ruff check` passes on `main.py`.
- **Conclusion:** 512 MB cannot run the normal query path even with every local model skipped. At least 2 GB is needed. The switch is still useful on a 2 GB host, to leave headroom.
- **Noticed, not fixed:** the slim image lacks `libexpat1`, so xarray's rasterio engine fails to load in the container.

### [2026-09-30] `ORCA_LOCAL_MODELS` switch, image import fixes, query-path memory cut to fit Render free (512 MB)

- **By:** Claude (Opus 5.5).
- **Files:**
  - Switch: `backend/orca/local_models.py` (new), `backend/orca/agents/language.py`, `backend/orca/agents/voice.py`, `backend/orca/intent_embeddings.py`, `backend/orca/llm/tiers.py`, `backend/orca/api/main.py`, `.env.example`.
  - Memory: `backend/orca/agents/geospatial.py`, `backend/orca/data/satellite_loaders.py`.
  - Image: `backend/requirements.txt`, `backend/Dockerfile`, `infra/render/Dockerfile` (new), `infra/render/Dockerfile.dockerignore` (new).
  - Docs: `docs/ORCA_Deployment.md` §2.
- **Commit:** — (uncommitted)
- **Switch:**
  - `ORCA_LOCAL_MODELS=0` replaces the earlier `ORCA_PRELOAD_LOCAL_MODELS` (same day, never committed). It is now enforced at every loader through `local_models.loading()`, so a model is never loaded, whether at startup or on first use. That covers IndicTrans2 (both models and the processor), faster-whisper, MMS-TTS, e5 and the Ollama rung, which `tiers.chain_for` drops.
  - When off, a loader raises `RuntimeError`, which every existing fallback chain already treats as "rung unavailable". Bhashini handles translation and speech, word overlap handles routing, and the hosted LLMs handle answers.
  - Blank or `1` leaves behaviour unchanged.
- **Import fix:**
  - `ImportError: cannot import name 'is_torch_npu_available'` in e5 was a race: two warm-up threads imported `transformers` for the first time at once. It was reproduced (parallel fails, sequential loads).
  - `loading()` holds one process-wide RLock, so loads never overlap. In the image, e5 now logs `intent embeddings ready`.
- **Install fixes:**
  - `IndicTransToolkit>=1.1; sys_platform != "win32"` added. The IndicTrans2 processor used to fail in the image. transformers stays at 4.46.3.
  - `libexpat1` added. xarray's rasterio engine now registers.
  - The CMD listens on `${PORT:-8000}`.
  - `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2` set.
- **Memory:** traced with tracemalloc on the real image.
  - `bathymetry_heatmap_points` called `.values` before striding, so it read the whole national GEBCO grid (~190 MB) per call.
  - `_insat_grid` cached the full disc in float64, 4 slots, ~190 MB per granule. It is now float32, 1 slot, cropped to `INDIA_BBOX` ± 5° (~8% of the disc).
  - Both return value-identical results: the GEBCO stride equals the old output, INSAT lat has 0 mismatches at 3 dp, and the SST max difference is 0.0. The loader self-check passes after the crop.
- **Verification:**
  - Switch on, no memory cap: every model warmed without error. "hi" and a Tamil safety query both answered, with memory at 1.3–1.8 GB. `IndicTrans2Backend` translated en→ta and ta→en in the container.
  - Switch off, `--memory=512m`, uncached queries in a row: startup 174 MB, then Rameswaram safety 279, Tamil safety 310, `kal kochi ke paas samundar kaisa rahega` 326, `pfzs near ktaka` 337, `hi` 337. There was no OOM. Before the memory fix, the first safety query peaked at 889 MB and was killed.
  - `ruff check orca` clean. `python -m orca.local_models` self-check ok.
  - Unit suite: 1186 passed and 3 failed. The failures are `test_notifications.py` (2), which counts leftover rows in the local DB (22 vs 1), and `test_gdacs_tracks_circuit_breaker…`, which passes alone and is order-dependent. None of them touch these files.
- **Noticed, not fixed:** `pfzs near ktaka` is still refused as out of scope. This is the chatbot read defect in `docs/ORCA_Prompt_Routing_Revamp.md`.
- **Not done:** pushing the image, and creating the Neon, Key Value and Render services. These need the user's accounts; the steps are in `docs/ORCA_Deployment.md` §2.

### [2026-10-01] Cloudflare Worker proxy for Open-Meteo API (solves Render 429 rate limit)

- **Implements:** fixes `api.open-meteo.com` returning `429 Too Many Requests` on Render free tier due to shared outbound IP quotas.
- **Files:** `infra/cloudflare/open-meteo-proxy.js` (new), `backend/orca/agents/weather_intelligence.py` (`_fetch_open_meteo`), `docs/ORCA_Deployment.md` §2.9.

- **What changed:**
  - `infra/cloudflare/open-meteo-proxy.js`: lightweight Cloudflare Worker forwarding `/v1/forecast` and `/v1/marine` with `X-Orca-Key` authentication check.
  - `weather_intelligence.py`: when `OPEN_METEO_PROXY_URL` and `OPEN_METEO_PROXY_KEY` are configured, routes upstream calls through the worker. Open-Meteo counts requests by `CF-Worker` header rather than Render's shared IP, providing a dedicated 10,000 requests/day quota.

- **Verification:** tested live via curl: 200 on forecast, 200 on marine, 403 when key missing.

### [2026-10-04] PC1.1 — Extract validate step as one pure function — DONE

- **What was built:** `validate_reading(places, when, user_location, now) -> ValidationOutcome | None` — a pure function that composes the existing `resolve_or_ask` / horizon / `position_guard` logic. Returns `ValidationOutcome("NEEDS_PLACE", body)` or `ValidationOutcome("OUT_OF_RANGE", body)` on a hard stop, `None` on success. Soft land disclosures (non-explicit source) are left for the caller (`query_guard_node`, PC1.2) — validate_reading returns `None` for them. No behaviour change anywhere in the graph.

All Done-when cases covered: unknown place → `NEEDS_PLACE`; past date → `OUT_OF_RANGE`; beyond horizon → `OUT_OF_RANGE`; explicit outside-extent position → `OUT_OF_RANGE`; valid inputs → `None`. Existing guard tests all pass untouched.
- **Not in scope / next:** PC1.2 (rewire graph edges + call `validate_reading` from `query_guard_node`).

### [2026-10-05] FIX-D10 — Follow-up and conversational messages are classified, and answered by the model (not the canned capability text) — DONE

- **Implements:** the two `/ask` defects the user found on 2026-10-05 ("i need more detailed description" after "what all can you do"; "so you cant give me any land based forcast?" after a Bengaluru answer), both answered with the same fixed capability sentence under a "Not something Sagar Sarathi can answer" heading.
- **Root causes:** (1) the classifier's history block showed only the user's earlier questions, never what ORCA replied, and it had no kind for conversation about the last reply, so such messages fell into `what_can_orca_do`; (2) `out_of_scope_node` answered that kind through the guard writer, which orders the model to keep every sentence of a fixed text and discards a reply that drops the "reason" sentence, so only a near copy survived; the fallback was not tagged small-talk, which is what put the refusal heading on it; (3) planning let the follow-up flag attach a carried sea intent to a non-sea kind, and let a word list (`is_out_of_scope`) decide whether a non-sea kind was out of scope, so any chat message containing "weather" or "forecast" would have run the sea agents.
- **Files:** `backend/orca/agents/understand.py` (new kind `chat_followup`; `NON_SEA_KINDS`, the one list of kinds answered without sea agents; the history block now shows ORCA's replies; prompt rule 14), `backend/orca/agents/planning.py` (the three copies of the non-sea kind list now read `NON_SEA_KINDS`; no carried intent for a non-sea kind; the model's kind decides out-of-scope for the kinds it owns, `distress` and `off_topic` excepted), `backend/orca/agents/reporting.py` (`write_chat_reply` + `CAPABILITY_FACTS`), `backend/orca/graph/graph.py` (`out_of_scope_node`: `what_can_orca_do` and `chat_followup` use it and are tagged small-talk), `backend/tests/unit/test_fix_d10_chat_followup.py` (new, 16 tests). `temp/follow_up_class_matrix.py` is a re-runnable live check, not part of the suite.
- **A regression I introduced and fixed:** the first version of rule 14 made the model call "what about tomorrow evening?" after a safety answer a `chat_followup` in 2 of 3 runs. Rule 14 now says a short follow-up after a sea answer is a sea question and `chat_followup` is only for talk about ORCA or its last reply.
- **Verification:**
  - New tests: 16 passed. With D-9's tests and the PC0-PC3, planning and conversation tests: 413 passed, 13 xfailed, 5 failed. Each of the five was run alone on this tree and on HEAD with identical results: `test_follow_up_that_matches_on_its_own_is_not_overridden`, `test_a_greeting_gets_a_model_written_reply_not_a_refusal` and `test_run_degrades_to_medium_confidence_on_no_match` fail on both; the other two pass alone and fail only after `test_pc2_2_merged_planning.py`, which sets `ORCA_LLM_ENABLED=0` for the whole process. None is caused by this change; none was fixed.
  - Live classification matrix against the real model, 21 prompts x 2 runs across four conversation contexts (after a PFZ answer, a safety answer, a capability answer, a land-weather refusal): 20 of 21 as expected. The one difference, "ok thanks" after a PFZ answer, came back once as `chat_followup` and once as `greeting_or_small_talk`; both are answered conversationally.
  - Live conversations through `/query` on a backend restarted without `--reload`: "what all can you do" then "i need more detailed description" gives a fuller model-written description as chat (no refusal heading); "what is the current weather in bengaluru" then "so you cant give me any land based forcast?" gives "I only provide sea-condition forecasts ... give me a coastal location"; "pfzs near mangrol" then "i need more detailed description" stays a sea question, carries Mangrol and reruns the agents; "is it safe to go to sea near kochi" then "what about tomorrow evening?" carries Kochi.
  - `ruff`: no new errors in the changed source files (counts equal to HEAD); the new test file is clean.
- **A test-harness mistake worth knowing:** `fresh=true` makes a turn bypass the conversation window, so a multi-turn check run with it silently tests single turns (`context_turns` stays 0). My first replay did exactly that.
- **Not done / still open:**
  1. `off_topic` was deliberately left on the old rule: "what is the current weather in bengaluru" is classed `off_topic` but the word list sends it to the sea agents, which answer at the pilot default position with "CAUTION: Insufficient data ... Proceed with caution" (seen live). The kind should decide, and the reply should be conversational like `chat_followup`.
  2. A model-classified `distress` that reaches planning is still not escalated.
  3. A follow-up PFZ detail reply once opened with "**VERDICT: REVISED**" and called an expired advisory "fresh": a reporting/critic issue, not routing.
  4. The "which place?" chip loop (Veraval) and the verdict-header wording ("GO -" on a non-safety answer) from the same review are separate and untouched.
  5. `greeting_or_small_talk`, `reset_or_language_switch` and `off_topic` replies still go through the old guard writer.

### [2026-10-05] FIX-D13 — An inland place is recognised by planning and answered directly, without running the sea agents — DONE

- **Implements:** defect D-13 (`temp/PC_Verification_Guide.md` section 7): "hows the weather in bengaluru" ran Discovery, Risk and Reporting at the pilot default position and showed "This question names no place... answered at the pilot default position" plus a stale-verdict banner.
- **Root cause:** the model read the message as `off_topic`, then `planning.run` let the old word list `is_out_of_scope` ("weather" is marine vocabulary) override that and run the sea agents; with no coastal place, the sea pipeline fell back to the default position. There was no way for the model to say "this is a weather question about an inland place".
- **Files:** `backend/orca/agents/understand.py` (new kind `inland_place`, in `NON_SEA_KINDS` automatically; prompt rule 15), `backend/orca/agents/planning.py` (if the model says `inland_place` but a place in the message resolves in the gazetteer or is a whole coastline, the gazetteer overrules it and the question stays a sea question), `backend/orca/graph/graph.py` (`out_of_scope_node` answers `inland_place` through `write_chat_reply` with facts saying the place is inland and ORCA has no data for it; fixed fallback text with no figure), `backend/tests/unit/test_fix_d13_inland_place.py` (new, 20 tests).
- **Design note:** the model decides the kind, code validates it against data (the project's rule). An inland call can only ever lead to a no-data reply, so the check guards the other direction: a real coastal question must never be refused. Mumbai, Chennai, Kochi, Kolkata, Mangalore, Visakhapatnam, Goa, Gujarat and Kerala are all overruled back to sea questions in the tests.
- **Verification:**
  - New tests 20 passed; wide regression set (D-9, D-10, D-13, PC0-PC3, planning, conversation, response, place resolution, distress) 476 passed, 13 xfailed, 5 failed: the same five failures recorded under FIX-D10, none caused by this change.
  - Live against the real model on a backend restarted without `--reload`, 12 prompts: "hows the weather in bengaluru", "weather in delhi today", "hyderabad weather tomorrow", "how is the weather in pune", "bangalore weather" all gave `kind=inland_place`, 0 agents run, outcome OUT_OF_SCOPE and a model-written reply saying the place is inland and ORCA covers the sea; "hows the weather in chennai", "weather in kochi tomorrow", "how is the sea near mumbai", "weather in kolkata" and "wave height at pamban" stayed sea questions and ran all agents; "hows the weather in goa" stayed a sea question and asked which place.
  - `ruff`: no new errors (per-file counts equal to HEAD); new test file clean.
- **Stale cache, not a bug in the fix:** "what is the current weather in bengaluru" first looked like a miss in the live run: it returned the OLD answer ("CAUTION: Insufficient data...") with 0 agent spans. That is the 30-minute Redis query cache (`orca/query_cache.py`, TTL 1800 s) replaying an answer stored before this fix; the same prompt with `fresh=true` gives the new inland reply. It expires by itself. Nothing was deleted.
- **Not done / still open:**
  1. Offline fallback: with no LLM, `_fallback_understand` has no inland concept and still sends "weather in bengaluru" to the sea agents (the old behaviour). A word list for inland cities was deliberately not added.
  2. A place that is neither in the gazetteer nor obviously inland (a small inland town the model does not recognise) is still the model's call; if it says `sea_question` the pipeline asks "which place?" as before.
  3. `off_topic`, `greeting_or_small_talk` and `reset_or_language_switch` still use the old guard writer, and `off_topic` still lets the word list override it.
  4. The Veraval chip loop (D-12) and the "GO -" wording on non-safety answers (D-11) are untouched.

Both are prompt-driven narration defects; the user asked that they be fixed so they cannot reappear.

### [2026-10-05] FIX-D11/D14 — A non-safety answer never opens with a verdict header; a follow-up answer never says "VERDICT: REVISED" or calls an expired advisory fresh — DONE

- **Implements:** D-11 ("GO -" on a PFZ answer) and D-14 (the "odd reply" to "i need more detailed description" after "pfzs near mangrol": "**VERDICT: REVISED**", an expired advisory called "fresh").
- **Root causes (all reproduced live before the change):**
  1. **D-11:** `lead_with_verdict` was correctly false, but the only control on the model was one sentence in the prompt. Nothing checked what it wrote, and the critic's own header regex (`^(GO|CAUTION|NO_GO):`) did not even recognise "GO -", so such an answer looked header-less.
  2. **D-14a:** `critic._revise_prompt` was called with `verdict_header or ""`. An answer with no verdict header (every non-safety answer) was told it "MUST still begin with the exact verdict header ''". The model obeyed by inventing one ("VERDICT: REVISED", "**EXPIRED / OUTDATED**", "**PFZ detail ...:**"). Reproduced 2 of 5 runs, exactly when the critic flagged an issue (5 LLM calls instead of 4).
  3. **D-14b:** the reviser was never shown the measured facts, only the text and the issue list, so it "fixed" problems by guessing.
  4. **D-14c ("fresh"):** the data itself says a 3-day-old PFZ advisory is `band: "fresh"` AND `expired: True` (`orca/data/freshness.py`: "fresh" means inside the source's normal cadence, not current; the module's own self-check asserts a 1-day-old advisory is fresh and expired). The narrator was handed that raw label, and the reviser built "classed as a 'fresh' zone due to updated conditions" on it. The critic's judge facts never contained the band, so the judge could not catch it.
- **Files:** `backend/orca/agents/reporting.py` (`strip_unrequested_verdict` applied when `lead_with_verdict` is false, falling back to the deterministic paragraph if nothing is left; a stronger no-header rule in the prompt; `narration_view`, which gives the narrator `recency` (`current` / `latest_held_not_today` / `pointer_only_not_current`) instead of `band`; prompt rule 9 rewritten to match and to forbid calling an expired item fresh or current), `backend/orca/agents/critic.py` (`_revise_prompt` takes the facts, has a separate no-header instruction and a "never add a claim not in MEASURED FACTS, delete it" rule; `_revision_is_safe` refuses a revision that invents a heading or a figure not in the text or the facts; wired into `run_critic_pass`), `backend/tests/unit/test_fix_d11_d14_narration_guards.py` (new, 46 tests).
- **Safety carve-out kept and tested:** only a `GO` header is ever stripped. A CAUTION or NO_GO always leads whatever was asked (`should_lead_with_verdict`, unchanged), and the structured verdict, the card's "Conditions checked" line and every figure are untouched. The critic's existing "verdict header must survive" rule is unchanged for safety answers.
- **Verification:**
  - New tests 46 passed. Wider run (critic, reporting, verdict presentation, response guarantee, conversation, planning, PC0 baseline, ocean analytics, analytics routes, D-9, D-10, D-13): 372 passed, 13 xfailed, 4 failed. ### [2026-10-06] FIX-PYREFLY — Last pyrefly error cleared (`LLMUnavailable` unbound) — DONE

- **Implements:** CI's pyrefly step, reported failing by the user after FIX-MYPY (`ERROR LLMUnavailable is uninitialized [unbound-name]`, `orca/agents/distress.py:362`).
- **Cause:** `detect_distress_model_check` imported `LLMUnavailable` and `llm` inside the `try`, then named `LLMUnavailable` in the `except`; if the import itself failed the name would be unbound. `LLMUnavailable` subclasses `RuntimeError`, so `except (LLMUnavailable, Exception)` already meant `except Exception`.
- **Change:** `distress.py` only: import just `llm`, catch `Exception`. Behaviour is identical (no model means the phrase-list result stands).
- **Verification:** `pyrefly check` reports 0 errors (was 1); distress, distress-queue and PC0 tests 70 passed, 13 xfailed.
- **Note, not changed:** `.impeccable/config.json` is tracked in git (committed 2026-09-05, before `.gitignore` line 55 was added), which is why it shows on GitHub despite being ignored; ignore rules never apply to tracked files. `git ls-files -ci --exclude-standard` shows the other tracked-but-ignored files: `skills-lock.json`, and the source folders `backend/orca/data/` and `frontend/app/data/` (caught by the `data/` pattern; those must stay tracked, but a NEW file added there is silently ignored unless added with `git add -f`).

### [2026-10-06] FIX-CI-GREEN — Every backend CI step passes, by fixing causes; the Veraval chip loop (D-12) fixed — DONE

- **Implements:** the user's request to get CI green "the right way, not hacked green", plus D-12 (the "which place do you mean?" chip loop), which the full unit suite surfaced as a failing test.
- **What was red and why (each cause fixed, none suppressed):**
  1. **CI guard 4** (`verify_ci_guards.py`): `distress.py` imported `orca.llm` for the Revamp 7.4 escalate-only model check, against the rule that safety-path files never gain the means to call a model. The check moved to a new module, `orca/agents/distress_escalation.py` (`escalate_with_model`, body unchanged); `distress.run(state, escalate=...)` now takes it as an argument and has no model import; the graph's distress node supplies it (`_distress_with_escalation`). The guard now also forbids a safety-path file importing the new module. Behaviour is unchanged: the phrase list still decides first, the model can only raise an alarm, and any model failure leaves the phrase-list result standing. `tests/unit/test_distress_escalation.py` (new) pins escalate-only, every failure mode, that `distress.py` is model-free, and the graph node end to end.
  2. **`test_a_greeting_gets_a_model_written_reply_not_a_refusal`** (red since `35eb311`): the guard prompt told the model both to answer a greeting warmly (rule 2) and to keep the refusal's "reason sentence" (rule 6), and `write_guard_reply` then discarded a small-talk reply that lacked it. The reason rule now applies to everything except a reply the model tags `[SMALL_TALK]`, and the check skips tagged replies (the invented-figure check still applies to them). Four new tests in `test_response_guarantee.py` pin both sides.
  3. **`tests/test_messy_prompts.py`** did not exist although `ci.yml` runs it. Written as a real gate: 83 cases over the model-off fallback (well-formed plan for 29 messy messages, horizon refusal, scope, conversation vs sea question, offline follow-up, distress phrase list, places never silently defaulted) plus 8 STRICT xfails for known gaps, each with its reason, so a gap that gets fixed turns the run red until the marker is removed. A mutation check proved the gate bites: removing the offline-follow-up fix fails `test_a_follow_up_carries_the_previous_sea_intent_offline`; restored, it passes.
  4. **The offline follow-up rule** that gate exposed: since the planning/understand merge, "and tomorrow?" after a sea turn was refused as off-topic whenever no model was reachable (`_fallback_understand` had lost the `carry_intent` + `is_continuation` exception planning used to apply). Restored in `understand.py`.
  5. **Process-wide environment writes in tests:** five test files wrote `ORCA_LLM_ENABLED=0` into `os.environ` at import or inside tests, never undone, so other tests' outcomes depended on collection order and on whether a machine had API keys (two conversation tests failed only with keys present). Replaced by a per-test autouse `monkeypatch.setenv` fixture; the two conversation tests also get a `raw_user_query`, as the real API always supplies one. The affected files pass forward and in reverse order.
  6. **Order-dependent tests the full unit suite exposed:** `test_gdacs_tracks_circuit_breaker...` (the circuit breaker is process-global and counts failures over five minutes; failures left by an earlier test made it trip early) now has an autouse fixture clearing the four breaker ids the weather module uses, the convention `test_satellite_loaders.py` already follows; `test_pre_dawn_briefing_fires_once...` asserted `sent == 1` over a shared dev database that this test, like others, commits into and never cleans, so the count grew by one each run (it read 33, then 34): its assertions are now about its own user.
  7. **D-12, the chip loop:** `validate_reading` re-checked the words of "pfzs near gujarat (at veraval)" against the gazetteer even though the chip had sent Veraval's coordinates, so it asked the same question again. A position the caller chose (`place_source` `explicit` or `coordinates`) now settles the place; a GPS fix, home port, carried or default position still do not. The date horizon and the data-extent checks still apply. Five new tests in `test_validate_reading.py`; `test_place_chip.py` (previously failing) passes. Verified live on a backend restarted without `--reload`: "pfzs near gujarat (at veraval)" with Veraval's position answers at Veraval; the same text with a date beyond the horizon is refused; "pfzs near gujarat" alone and with only a GPS fix still asks which port.
  8. **mypy and pyrefly** (earlier today, FIX-MYPY / FIX-PYREFLY): 0 errors.
- **Verification (clean checkout, the way CI sees it):** a temporary git worktree of HEAD with these changes applied, no `.env`, no `data/`, every API-key and database variable unset; the seven backend steps of `ci.yml` in order: see the result lines appended below. Frontend: `npm run check:i18n`, `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` all pass in the main tree.

The run before it (`ca7c44b`) failed at pyrefly, since fixed. None of the changes above were in the pushed commits, so the pushed code still fails CI guard 4.
  2. A message the planning model reads as `distress` is not escalated by planning (it still gets a sea plan). Recorded as a strict xfail in `test_messy_prompts.py`; needs a distress handoff after planning, not a word list.
  3. The distress phrase list misses "engine failed near pamban" and romanized Hindi/Kannada distress (strict xfails; unchanged).

Before: "pfzs near rameshwaram" was detected as Malayalam and answered in Malayalam. Three native-script prompts (Tamil, Hindi, Malayalam) still gave `ta`, `hi`, `ml`, replies in their own script, and a translated English query.

### [2026-10-06] UI-DECLUTTER — Remove secondary controls and notes from the answer card — DONE

- **Implements:** the user's request, from three screenshots of `/ask` (the "Marine Conditions Summary" card, the "Sea Safety Assessment" card and the "Re-run this without any LLM" button): keep the card title, remove the extra things around it, remove the re-run button.
- **Removed from the card:** the "N Agents Verified" badge; the quiet "Conditions checked — no hazard threshold crossed" line (a GO on a non-safety answer); the fisherman "Why this answer?" button and its three-sentence expander (and the `fishermanWhy` helper only it used); the "Following on from N earlier message(s) in this chat" note; the "View on map" / "Map focused on ..." link; the "Re-run this without any LLM" button. Props that only fed them (`agentsVerified`, `isMapFocus`, `onViewOnMap`) are gone from `PersonaAnswerMatrix`, `ChatTurn` and `ask/page.tsx`; five i18n strings no one uses now (`chatTurn.followingOnOne`, `followingOnMany`, `mapFocusedOn`, `viewOnMap`, `rerunNoLlm`) were removed from all ten dictionaries.
- **Kept on purpose:** the card title; the **Go / Caution / No go chip with its reason line** (the screenshot's second item included it; it is the safety verdict, and a CAUTION or NO GO must never be hidden: the backend forces it to lead whatever was asked, and the chip is how the card shows it); the "Data limited — verify locally" notes; the context warning when a follow-up lost its context (only the routine "Following on" line went); the "Carried over" chips; the answer text; "Sources & provenance"; the cross-source check; the confidence meter and its own "Why?"; the follow-up chips; the "Try again" control in the card header; and the note on an answer that WAS produced with the model off.

### [2026-10-07] FIX-D16 — A native-script answer body disappears from the card — DONE

- **Root cause (measured, not guessed):** the backend sent the full Kannada answer (210 characters, starting "GO: ..."); `FormattedResponse` strips a leading "GO: reason" so the card does not repeat the verdict the chip already shows, and decided where the header ends with `/^(GO|CAUTION|NO_GO):\s*([^\n*]+?)(?:\.\s+(?=[A-Z])|$)/`: a period followed by a CAPITAL LATIN letter, or the end of the text. Indic scripts have no capitals, and translation joins the header and the body into one line, so the header ran to the end and all 210 characters were hidden. The play button reads the raw text, which is why it still spoke. **Every native-script safety answer was affected:** run on the saved answers from the nine-language probe, the old rule left an empty body in Hindi, Tamil, Malayalam, Kannada, Telugu, Bengali, Marathi, Gujarati and Odia. English was not affected (its header ends at a line break).
- **Fix:** the split moved into one pure function, `frontend/app/lib/verdictHeader.ts` (`splitVerdictHeader`), used by `FormattedResponse.tsx`. It keeps the old English behaviour, and only takes the split when a body is left; otherwise it removes only the `GO:` label and shows the rest. An answer can no longer render blank because of the split. (`FormattedResponse`'s parsed header is not displayed anywhere, so only the body changed.)
- **Check:** `frontend/scripts/check-verdict-header.mjs` (8 checks; the Kannada, Tamil and Hindi cases are the real backend answers), run as `npm run check:verdict-header`. Mutation check: removing the new guard makes it fail; restored, it passes. Added to the frontend CI job, together with `npm run check:i18n`, which the README's "Quality gates" already listed but `ci.yml` never ran.
- **Verification:** `npm run lint` clean, `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `check:i18n` OK (227 keys), `check:verdict-header` 8 passed. Not checked in a browser (user's instruction).
- **Not done:** the backend still loses the line break between "GO: reason" and the body when it translates (Bhashini returns one line). The front-end fix makes that harmless for display; translating the header and the body separately in `language_egress` would keep the structure, and is a backend change for later. The Kannada answer now shows the translated reason at its start as well as the English reason under the chip.

### [2026-10-07] PC5.8 — An explicit "answer in <language>" request is honoured; English stays the default for Latin text — CLAIMED

- **Implements:** user's decision 2026-10-07 after tracing `answer this in kannda: pfzs near mangalore` (answered in English: ingress sets the language from the script alone, planning noticed the request in its English reading but had nowhere to put it, and narrative rule 12 forces English). **Rule:** when the user explicitly asks for a language, the response is in that language; otherwise English is the default for Latin text and the script's language for native-script text (unchanged). Overrides D11 only when a language is explicitly requested. Serves `PS-C2`.
- **By:** Claude (user-prompted 2026-10-07). **Files expected:** `understand.py`, `planning.py`, `state.py`, `graph.py`, `language.py`, `api/main.py` only if the response payload needs it, tests. No browser check (user's instruction).
- **Status:** DONE 2026-10-07 (code + tests; the user verifies the UI himself). Planning returns `reply_language` (validated against the ten codes in `understand._clean_reply_language`; prompt rule 17), stored in state, egress translates to it (it wins over the script's language), and the payload's `detected_language` reports the answer's language. Two follow-on fixes found live: (1) the narrative model obeyed or translated "say it in Kannada:" (wrote Kannada into the English field, or answered with the question), so planning's `english_reading` now excludes the request and the narrative is given that reading when a language was requested; (2) `reporting.is_english_text` discards a narrative that is >=60% non-Latin for the English facts paragraph (threshold measured: wholesale Kannada/Tamil 93-94%, English quoting a Tamil question 43%). Live (port 8001, fresh backend): "answer this in kannda: pfzs near mangalore", Kannada-script request, explicit Tamil/Hindi/English all answer in the asked language; no-request Latin stays English and native script keeps its language. Kannada-script request x6: English narrative a full answer each time after fix (was 4/6 fallback). Tests: 49 in `test_pc5_8_reply_language.py`; full unit suite 1257 passed. Open: Hindi output still leaks `ZKEEPZ` placeholders (known), and the Kannada-script prompt is still model-variable on tone.

A first live run showed the planner treating "what should i do if my engine fails at sea" as an emergency; `understand.py` rule 1 now names hypothetical, how-to and MRCC-number questions as `sea_question`, and the re-run was clean. Not re-verified: other phrasings.
