### [2026-10-08] FIX-COLD — The first query and the first Play Verdict no longer pay one-off setup costs — CLAIMED

- **Defect (user, 2026-10-08):** the very first query after a start took very long, and so did the first Play Verdict. **Measured, cold backend (port 8001):** healthy at 6.9 s, but the warm-ups (Whisper, MMS-TTS, intent embeddings, local model) keep loading until about 35 s; first query 24.6 s vs 16.1 s and 7.6 s for the next two (`distress_check` 9.4 s the first time, 3.2 s after: the first Gemini client import/TLS); first `/voice/speak` 5.4 s vs 3.4 s (Bhashini pipeline-config lookup + synthesis). **Change:** a fire-and-forget `_prime_first_request` at startup makes one tiny cheap-tier model call and one short Bhashini/local TTS synthesis, so the first user pays neither. **By:** Claude. **Files expected:** `api/main.py`, a test. **Not covered:** the model-load contention in the first ~30 s, and the steady-state cost of the distress model check (3.2 s per query on Gemini, was 0.9 s on groq).
- **Status (FIX-COLD):** DONE in code 2026-10-08. Two parts. (1) `_prime_first_request` (one tiny cheap-tier call + one short synthesis) at startup. (2) Measured that the 9.5 s first distress check was contention with the model warm-ups (45 s wait: 3.6 s), so `lifespan` now awaits the warm-ups (translation, Whisper, MMS-TTS, intent embeddings, the priming; capped at 90 s; the local LLM is not waited for) via `_wait_until_warm`. Result, cold backend: healthy at 34 s ("ORCA ready after 29.1s of warm-up"), first query 17.6 s (was 26.0) and equal to the later ones, first speak equal to the later ones (was +2 s). Trade-off: the server takes about 30 s to start accepting requests (was 7 s). **Found on the way:** a Python client calling `localhost` on this Windows machine pays about 2 s per request (it tries IPv6 `::1` first; uvicorn is on 127.0.0.1): a cached speak is 0.01 s via 127.0.0.1 and 2.05 s via localhost. Browsers are expected to fall back quickly, not verified. **Still slow, unchanged:** a full sea question is 7-17 s warm (weather fetch ~4.6 s, distress model check 3 s, planning 1.5-4 s), an uncached speak 3-5 s (Bhashini network). Tests: `test_fix_cold_priming.py` (3).

### [2026-10-08] CFG-NO-LOCAL-MODELS — ORCA no longer loads or uses Ollama or IndicTrans2 — CLAIMED

- **Decision (user, 2026-10-08):** "dont wake up the ollama model, indictrans2 models, better dont even use them, it doesnt exist in many of my friends systems." Supersedes the plan's "IndicTrans2 as fallback" for translation (the sentence the user kept on 2026-10-0x) and the "local model is the lowest-priority fallback" rung for answers. **Why it also fixes startup:** both loaded models into memory during the 22 s startup wait (Ollama generate call at 3 s; the translator loaded two models and then failed at 22 s because `IndicTransToolkit` is not installed here). **Changes:** `main.py` no longer registers, warms or waits for `IndicTrans2Backend` or `warm_local_models`; `tiers.chain_for` no longer appends an Ollama rung by default (an explicit `ollama:` entry in a chain env var still works, as an opt-in); `.env` and `.env.example` drop `ollama:gemma4:e4b` from the MID chain; translation is Bhashini only, and when it fails ingress passes the raw text through and egress shows the English answer (both already did, marked degraded); the span labels no longer claim IndicTrans2 ran (`engines.INDICTRANS2` -> `NO_TRANSLATION`, passthrough provenance "No translation (passthrough)", the wording `test_pc5_language_ingress.py` PC5.7 specifies). **Not removed:** the `IndicTrans2Backend` class and the `OllamaProvider` (still imported by their own tests, unreachable from the running app), Whisper, MMS-TTS and the e5 intent model (not asked). **By:** Claude. **Files:** `api/main.py`, `llm/tiers.py`, `agents/language.py`, `engines.py`, `.env*`, tests.
- **Status (CFG-NO-LOCAL-MODELS):** DONE in code 2026-10-08. Cold backend log: 0 lines mentioning Ollama (`11434`) or IndicTrans2; "ORCA ready after 18.0s of warm-up" (was 21.9 s), healthy at 22.0 s; first query 13.3 s, first speak 1.1 s. Live: Tamil question answered in Tamil, `answer this in kannda` answered in Kannada, Latin stays English, all through Bhashini; the English language spans now read "No translation (passthrough)". Tests: `test_fix_cold_priming.py` (+3: startup source has no IndicTrans2/Ollama references, spans never claim a translator on English, a failed translation degrades with no local fallback); `test_response_guarantee` default-chain test rewritten for the new rule; `test_pc5_3...` engine assertion corrected. The friend's `test_pc5_language_ingress.py` now fails 2 (was 4): PC5.5 (reading in the reporting output) and the "translation returned input unchanged -> degraded" check. **The remaining 18 s** is Whisper, MMS-TTS and the e5 embeddings loading one after another (the `local_models.loading` lock serialises them: MMS-TTS 7.2 s, then embeddings 5.5 s). Not asked about, left loaded. **Docs not edited:** `docs/plans/ORCA_Pipeline_Consolidation_Plan.md` and the README still describe IndicTrans2 as the translation fallback.
- **Cache deleted at the user's explicit request (2026-10-08):** `~/.cache/huggingface/hub/models--ai4bharat--indictrans2-en-indic-dist-200M` (4.0 GB) and `...-indic-en-dist-200M` (3.5 GB), 7.5 GB freed, nothing else touched (the other model caches, including Whisper, MMS-TTS and e5, remain). The weights had never been usable here (IndicTransToolkit not installed). **Docs deliberately left as they are:** the plan and README keep describing IndicTrans2 as the translation fallback, by the user's decision: it is a design-level fallback that can be re-added in a small piece of work.

### [2026-10-08] PLAN-VOICE-1 — EXPANDED after investigation: everything found wrong with the spoken answer — LOGGED ONLY, NO CODE CHANGED

The user reported, for the greeting "Hello! I'm ORCA — I help with sea conditions off India's coast: safety to go out, waves, wind, tides, fishing zones, ...": a doubled voice on the word "out", the em dash read as "E", "zones" read as "zvones"/"ones", and asked for every other voice problem to be found before any change. **Method:** the text was sent to Bhashini TTS exactly as the app sends it (`bhashini.tts`, English voice, `text-normalization` on), the audio was played back into Whisper (`faster-whisper small`, word timestamps) and compared with the text, then variants of the text were tried one change at a time. Probe scripts and the WAVs are in `%TEMP%` (`tts_probe.py`, `tts_v2.py` to `tts_v6.py`, folder `tts/`). **Limits, stated plainly:** Whisper is a proxy for an ear, not an ear; it can mishear, so each item says how sure it is, and the user's ear is the final check. Bhashini's output was identical on four repeats of the same text, so these are deterministic faults in text handling, not random glitches.

**A. Confirmed in the audio (reproduced, deterministic).**
1. **"!" is read as "factorial".** "Hello! I'm ..." is heard as "Hello Factorial"; same for "Hey! (emoji)". "Hello," / "Hello." / no mark are fine. Every friendly chat reply uses a "!".
2. **An all-caps word is spelled letter by letter, and the em dash after it adds a letter.** "I'm ORCA — I" is heard as "O R C E E" (and a 1.6 s gap); "I'm Orca." is read as a word, and so are "ORCA," and "ORCA.". So what the user heard as the dash being "E" is the acronym reading plus the dash. The name is no longer all caps ("Sagar Sarathi"), so the greeting no longer triggers it; an em dash in the middle of a sentence ("conditions — safety") is read as a normal pause (checked). Any other all-caps word can still do this (see 6).
3. **"zone(s)" loses its "z".** "fishing zone(s)" is heard as "fishing one(s)", "vans", "wands"; "The zone lies" as "The one lie is". It depends on context (it works after "nearest"), so it cannot be predicted, and it fails in the greeting. In the real greeting sentence "fishing zoanz" and "fishing zohnz" were heard as "zones", and a comma pause ("fishing, zones") also worked; "zownz" was heard "sound", and capital "Zones", "potential fishing zones", "the fishing zones" and plain "zones" still failed. (Candidate: respell in the spoken text only, with a test over the real sentence.)
4. **A non-breaking hyphen (U+2011) in dates and ranges makes the voice read wrong numbers.** The narrative model writes "2026‑10‑02" and "30‑35 m" with U+2011. Read back: "valid for **2000 and 2062**" and "depth is **3035** a.m.". With a normal "-" the date is read right ("2 October 2026") but the range is still "3035m" ("30-35 m"). A wrong date and a wrong depth spoken to a fisherman are accuracy faults, not cosmetics. Fix: normalise the dashes first, then speak a range as "30 to 35" (verified: "Depth is 30 to 35 m").
5. **"km/h" is read without "per hour"; "m/s" is read "Rims"; a lone "m" is read as the letter M.** "Wind is 12 km/h" is heard as "12 kilometers"; "3.4 m/s" as "3.4 Rims"; "1.5 m" as "1.5 M". With the units written out ("12 kilometres per hour", "3.4 metres per second", "30 to 35 metres") every one is read right. A wind speed with half its unit missing is a safety-relevant misread.
6. **The acronym IMBL is read "AMBL" or "emerald"** (the safety boundary term). "I M B L" is still heard "AAMBL"; a spoken form has to be chosen by ear. PFZ, MRCC, NNW and VHF are read correctly.
7. **"/" and "&" are dropped:** "Wave/wind data & tide data" is heard "Wavewind data, Tite data". Needs "and" or a comma.
8. **Times:** "08:24 IST" is heard "8, 24 AST" (IST misread); "8:24 in the morning Indian Standard Time" is fine.
9. **The emergency phone number is read as grouped numbers, not digits.** "+91-44-2539-5018" is heard "plus 9144, 2539, 5018" (ninety-one forty-four ...). Spelled as digits ("plus nine one, four four, two five three nine, five zero one eight") it is read right. A number someone has to dial must be read digit by digit. This is on the distress path.
10. **The product name:** "Sagar Sarathi" is heard "Cigar/Sigur/Segar Sarathi" in the English voice. "Saagur Sarathi", "Saa gar Sarathi" and "Sagar Saarthi" were each heard as "Sagar ...". (The user's ear decides which sounds right.) Place names also come out in an English accent: "Thoothukudi" is heard "Tututudi".
11. **The Hindi placeholder leak is spoken aloud.** A Hindi answer that contains the translation-protection token `ZKEEPZ0Z` is read "जेड के ए ए पी जेड 0 जेड" (letter by letter). The cause is in egress (the token comes back not unmasked) and is already an open defect; speech makes it worse. A guard before speaking should refuse to read a placeholder.
12. **Markdown and emoji:** `**`, `*` and bullets are ignored (fine); an emoji is silently dropped (fine), but see 1 for the "!" next to it.

**B. Checked and fine.** "GO:", "CAUTION:" and "NO_GO:" are read as words; PFZ, MRCC, NNW, VHF; degrees ("343°" is read "343 degree"); "40 %"; "16 km"; "0.6"; and the Tamil, Kannada and Hindi voices read their sentences back correctly through Bhashini ASR (including "GO:" as "கோ" and "16 km NNW" as letters), apart from the placeholder in A11.

**C. Not reproduced: the doubled "out".** The greeting WAV has no repeated or spliced stretch (no near-copy of any earlier stretch at lags of 50 ms to 1.2 s) and Whisper hears "out" once. "out" does run about 0.5 s with a flat energy plateau, longer than in other sentences, which could sound like a second voice on that word, but that is a guess. **There is a second, real cause on the playback side** (`AnswerSpeaker.tsx`): `speak()` sets "playing" at once and then waits for the audio (1 to 5 s on the first play). Pressing the button in that gap calls `stop()`, which pauses nothing (no audio exists yet) and flips the icon back to "play", while the pending `speak()` carries on and starts playing. Pressing play again then starts a second `Audio` and the first is never paused (`audioRef` is overwritten), so two copies of the sentence play over each other, offset by however long the user waited. The slow first play (the cold start the user reported) is exactly when people press twice. This would double the whole sentence rather than one word, so the user should say whether the doubling is on one word only, on a fresh click, with a single press.

**D. Silent change of voice ("voice feels off" in places).** `bhashini.TIMEOUT_S = 3.0` applies to the TTS call as well, there is no retry, and a failure falls to the local MMS-TTS voice (a different, robotic voice; for a long answer it is chunked at 200 characters), and the result is cached under the text, so the same answer keeps the wrong voice. A long sentence taking Bhashini over 3 s did time out in the probe. The `X-TTS-Rung` response header already says which voice was used; nothing shows it. (Candidate: TTS gets its own timeout of 10 to 15 s and one retry before falling back.)

**E. The text sent to the speaker is the raw answer.** It includes the "GO: reason" header, line breaks, Markdown, U+202F and U+2011 characters, "!" and units exactly as the model wrote them. There is no speakable form of the text.

**Proposed shape of the fix (not started; the user has to say go).** One speakable-text step in `voice.text_to_speech` (so prefetch and play both get it; applied to what is spoken, never to what is shown; the cache key is the spoken text), language-neutral rules first: normalise dashes (U+2010 to U+2015, U+2212) to "-"; "!" to "."; em and en dashes and " - " to ", "; "/" and "&" to words; emoji and Markdown removed; ranges "N-N unit" to "N to N unit"; units, compass and time words spelled out ("km/h" to "kilometres per hour", "m/s" to "metres per second", "m" after a number to "metres", "IST" to "Indian Standard Time"); phone numbers digit by digit; refuse to read a "ZKEEPZ" placeholder. Then English-voice-only respellings (zone to "zoanz", the product name, IMBL) kept in one small table with the evidence beside each entry. Plus the playback fix (one `Audio` at a time, `stop()` cancels a pending request, `speak()` pauses any audio first, a second press while one is starting is ignored), plus the TTS timeout and retry and a visible "local voice" marker. Tests: every item above as a case (text in, spoken text out), a Whisper-free unit test for the rewriting, and a rerun of the round-trip probe as a manual check. **Decisions needed from the user:** which spoken form for IMBL and for the name (by ear), whether the Indic voices get the same neutral rules (they already read correctly, so only the neutral ones are proposed), and the TTS timeout.

### [2026-10-08] PLAN-VOICE-1 (addendum) and PLAN-CHART-1 — findings after the user's listening test — LOGGED ONLY, NO CODE CHANGED

The user listened and **reproduced every fault listed above**, and added: (a) "don't" is read "don, a pause of about a second, tee"; (b) the double voice does happen "sometimes", subtle but noticeable, and once on the word "wind" in the greeting; (c) the product name is सागर सारथी: long "aa" in the first syllable of both words (Saa-gar Saa-ra-thi), which the English voice does not say; (d) the weather chart on the answer card "is almost dancing": with the map open its lines draw in and out repeatedly, and they are drawn properly only after the map is closed with the button above.

**Voice addendum.**
- **A13 Apostrophes.** The answer text is written with the typographic apostrophe (U+2019) by the models; the speech engine mishandles it. Measured with Whisper: with U+2019 "it's" lasts 0.76 s (0.32 s with a straight "'") and "I'm" is heard as "aim"; "don't" with a straight apostrophe is read cleanly. This matches the user's "don, pause, tee". Contractions written out ("do not", "it is", "cannot") read cleanly in every test. Fix: normalise U+2018, U+2019, U+02BC, U+00B4 and the backtick to "'"; if the user still hears the pause with a straight one, expand the contractions in the spoken text only.
- **A14 The name.** Target pronunciation सागर सारथी. Latin respellings cannot be judged by Whisper (it does not hear vowel length), so they are left to the user's ear. What was measured: the Hindi voice reads "सागर सारथी" exactly (Bhashini ASR round trip returns the Devanagari); the English voice reading Devanagari inline is heard "Sagar Sarthi" (drops a syllable); "Saa-gar Saa-rathi" is heard "Sagar Sarathi". Candidate WAVs: `%TEMP%\tts\listen\10_`..`14_` (current, "Saa-gar Saa-rathi", Devanagari inline in the English voice, the Hindi voice spliced into the English sentence, a whole Hindi sentence). Likely best: speak the name with the Hindi voice and the rest in English (a splice, a short gap between the parts); the cost is two different voices in one sentence, which the user has to judge.
- **C. The double voice, further.** The doubling on "wind" was not found in the waveform either (a per-word autocorrelation and a double-peak search were run on the greeting WAV; both just flag ordinary voiced speech and cannot tell an echo from a vowel, so they prove nothing either way). So there are three candidates, and the listening files separate them: (1) the sound is in the audio Bhashini returns (then `01_` has it too, whatever the browser does); (2) it is in the long single call and goes away when the text is cleaned, cut into sentences, or voiced by the male voice (`02_` one call, `04_` sentence by sentence, `03_` male voice); (3) it is the app's playback (two `Audio` objects, the bug described under C above), which a file played outside the browser cannot have. **Asked of the user:** play `01_` in a normal player; if it is doubled there too it is Bhashini's, if not it is the browser/app.
- **Files for the user's ear:** `%TEMP%\tts\listen\` (16 WAVs): `01_` the greeting exactly as the app sends it, `02_` cleaned one call, `03_` cleaned male voice, `04_` cleaned sentence by sentence, `10_`-`14_` the name, `20_`-`22_` apostrophes (curly, straight, expanded), `30_`/`31_` units and dates as written and as spoken, `32_`/`33_` the phone number as written and digit by digit.

**PLAN-CHART-1 — the weather chart on the answer card.** (Found by reading `charts.tsx` and a live answer; not watched in a browser, per the user's rule.)
1. **It dances (the user's report).** Probable causes, all in code: `TimeSeriesW` leaves Recharts' line animation on (no `isAnimationActive={false}`), so every re-render that changes the data identity or the container size replays the draw-in; the chart sits inside `ChatTurn`'s root `motion.div layout`, which animates a layout change with scale transforms, and `ResponsiveContainer` re-measures through that; with the map open, the thread column resizes and moves as the map panel and its toggle change, so the chart is re-measured and redrawn again and again; closing the map stops the resizing, and the lines settle, exactly as the user saw. **Fix:** `isAnimationActive={false}` on the lines, drop `layout` from the card root (the entrance fade stays), keep the chart's container size stable; and removing the map from /ask (PLAN-ASK-1) removes the trigger.
2. **Colours are for the old dark theme.** `charts.tsx`/`chartSpec.ts` hard-code `#7fd4e8` and `#9aa8d8` (pale lines), `#17384c` (a dark navy gridline) and `#64879a` (axis); on the cream light theme the lines are close to invisible and the dotted grid is heavy. They should read the theme variables (`--color-ocean-cyan`, `--color-hairline`, `--color-ink-dim`), as the rest of the card does.
3. **Wave height (m) and wind speed (m/s) share one y axis.** The wind danger line is 55 km/h = 15.3 m/s, so the axis runs to 16 and a 0.64 m wave and a 0 to 3.4 m/s wind are squeezed into the bottom fifth of the plot (live data: 72 hourly points, wave 0.64 to 0.8 m, wind 0.1 to 3.4 m/s). Two series in two units need two axes, or two small charts. The card above the chart speaks wind in km/h while this chart uses m/s: one unit for wind everywhere (km/h, which fishermen use).
4. **The x axis prints raw ISO timestamps** ("2026-10-08T01:00:00Z") at 6 positions for a 72 hour range: unreadable. Should be local time and day ("Thu 6 am").
5. **The threshold labels collide** with the y-axis numbers ("Wind speed danger" over the 16, "Wave height danger" over the 4) and with each other. They should sit at the right end of their lines, or in the legend.
6. **Wind unit in the header** reads "m | m/s": ambiguous.

### [2026-10-08] FIX-NAME-1 — The product name is written correctly in each language, not by the translator's guess — CLAIMED

- **Defect (user, 2026-10-08):** in a Kannada answer the name reads "ಸಾಗರ ಸರಥಿ" (short "ra"); it must be **ಸಾಗರ ಸಾರಥಿ**. **Measured:** Bhashini translates "Sagar Sarathi" by sound, so the spelling is the model's guess and differs by language and by sentence (en to kn gave "ಸಾಗರ್ ಸಾರಥಿ", with a final virama; hi to kn transliteration gave "ಸಾಗರ್ ಸಾರ್ಥಿ"; ta "சாகர் சாரதி", te "సాగర్ సారథి", ml "സാഗർ സാരഥി", bn "সাগর সারথি", gu "સાગર સારથી", or "ସାଗର ସାରଥି"). **Fix (class, not case):** the name is protected like IMBL/PFZ/numbers already are (`_mask_protected_terms`): hidden from the translator and replaced after by a fixed spelling per target language. **Confirmed by the user:** Devanagari (hi, mr) सागर सारथी, Kannada ಸಾಗರ ಸಾರಥಿ. **Not confirmed, taken from Bhashini where both of its routes agree:** bn সাগর সারথি, gu સાગર સારથી, or ସାଗର ସାରଥି. **Left to Bhashini, awaiting the user:** ta, te, ml (its spelling ends in a virama, as the Kannada one did, which the user rejected). **By:** Claude. **Files:** `agents/language.py`, test.
- **Status (FIX-NAME-1): DONE in code 2026-10-08, generalised on the user's instruction ("dont just hardcode kannada, it should be there for all languages and other scenarios").** **Mechanism:** `language.PRODUCT_NAME_NATIVE` holds the name for all nine languages (the one place; nothing else keeps a copy); `localize_product_name(text, language)` writes it wherever it appears, in Latin letters or spelt by ear in the language's own script (a script-free consonant skeleton: vowel signs, a final virama, a ya-phala after a virama, tha/ta and Tamil's ca/sa and ka/ga all drop out, so ಸಾಗರ್ ಸಾರಥಿ, ಸಾಗರ ಸರಥಿ, ಸಾರथि and Bengali স্যারথি are corrected while a different word pair such as ಸಾಗರ ಸಾಗತಿ is left alone). **Where it applies:** (1) every translation into a language (`_translate_with_rung`): the name is hidden from the translator as a placeholder and put back, with the function as a safety net after; (2) every chat reply a model writes in the user's own language (`graph._in_name_spelling`, at the four reply sites): the reply's OWN script decides the language, not a state field, so "answer in kannada: ..." works and an English fallback sentence is never given a native name; (3) the three reply prompts tell the model to write the name in Latin letters; (4) the answer-writer prompt now says "You are Sagar Sarathi ... never give or invent another [name]" (live runs had it invent "OceanWatch Advisor" and "SafeGuide"); (5) the planner prompt makes "who are you / what is your name" a capability question in any language, never a sea question (it was answered as one in Telugu, Malayalam, Marathi and Odia). **Spellings:** confirmed by the user: Devanagari (hi, mr) सागर सारथी, Kannada ಸಾಗರ ಸಾರಥಿ. **Not yet confirmed by a native reader:** ta சாகர சாரதி, te సాగర సారథి, ml സാഗര സാരഥി, bn সাগর সারথি, gu સાગર સારથી, or ସାଗର ସାରଥି. These are Bhashini's transliteration of the Latin "Saagara Saarathi", the spelling that reproduces the user's Kannada and Devanagari exactly (the other Latin spellings tried did not); bn, gu and or are the same under every route tried. **Live (fresh backend, "who are you / what is your name" in all nine languages plus an explicit Kannada request):** kn, hi, bn, ta, te, ml, gu, mr and the explicit-Kannada reply all carry the name in the table's spelling; Odia described its role without stating the name (planner/model variance, not a spelling fault). **Tests:** `test_fix_name_native.py` (the table covers every language; the Latin name and by-ear variants are corrected in each of the nine; real misspellings seen in answers; punctuation kept; look-alike words left alone; translation placeholder round trip and the safety net in each language; the graph hook and the English-reply guard; the identity prompts). **Not done / found on the way (new defects, not fixed):** (a) **a model writing Kannada also inserted a Korean word ("나는") and a nonsense phrase ("ಭಾರತ ಕೀರ್ತಿನ")**, twice: a reply in a native script can contain letters of other scripts. A guard that rejects a chat reply containing a script other than its own and Latin, and falls back to the English sentence translated by Bhashini, is proposed, not built; (b) the UI dictionaries (`frontend/app/i18n/*.json`) and the SMS/CAP advisory text keep the name in Latin letters inside native sentences; whether the Kannada UI should read ಸಾಗರ ಸಾರಥಿ is a design choice for the user; (c) Marathi is still detected as Hindi (so a Marathi chat reply is in Hindi script).

Bhashini's own Kannada for these is "30ರಿಂದ 35 ಮೀಟರ್", "16 ಕಿಲೋಮೀಟರ್", "ಗಂಟೆಗೆ 12 ಕಿಲೋಮೀಟರ್", "2026ರ ಅಕ್ಟೋಬರ್ 2", "343 ಡಿಗ್ರಿ", "40 ಪ್ರತಿಶತ", "5 ನಾಟಿಕಲ್ ಮೈಲಿ"; "30 ರಿಂದ 35 ಮೀಟರ್" reads "ಮೂವತ್ತುರಿಂದ 35 ಮೀ", so a native range word fixes the range. **Step 1 (now):** the "!" rule becomes language-neutral. **Step 2 (proposed, awaiting the user):** a per-language lexicon (to, metres, kilometres per hour, degrees, percent, nautical miles, month names, year spoken) for all nine languages from Bhashini's own translations, applied to digits and Latin units inside native text. **By:** Claude.

- **PLAN-VOICE-5 (double voice): INVESTIGATED, cause NOT found; nothing implemented, as asked.** The user's description: on one press, one or two words in a response sound doubled "like a robot or when we speak near a table fan"; it does not happen on the cleaned files. **Established (measured 2026-10-09):** (1) Bhashini returns bit-identical audio for identical text (same SHA-1 over six runs, two texts), so a doubled word is a fixed property of that text's audio, not luck; (2) the audio contains no second voice, no period doubling (cycle-amplitude alternation is positive on every word, none negative), no spliced or repeated stretch, no discontinuity; narrow-band and wide-band spectrograms of the reported word ("wind", raw greeting) show one clean harmonic ladder; (3) the WAV container is ordinary (float32, 22050 Hz, mono, sizes correct, extra `fact` chunk, no trailing bytes); (4) **the audio is peak-normalised to exactly full scale** (peak 1.0000 in every file); after resampling to 44.1/48 kHz (what a browser does) the raw greeting goes over full scale on ONE word only: "wind," (2 samples), the word the user reported; the cleaned greeting's overs are on a different word ("What") and the cleaned response has none at 48 kHz. Two samples are not audible by themselves; the significance is that the loudest word or two sit at 0 dBFS, which is where a limiter, a "loudness" enhancement or a volume boost in the sound chain acts, and that would hit one or two words. **No measure** (loudness spike, cycle alternation, cepstral roughness, 18-70 Hz envelope flutter, pitch flatness) separates the file the user called doubled from the ones he called clean, so none is a detector. **Not ruled out:** the neural vocoder's own roughness on a word (a property of the model); the browser or the OS audio path. **Decisive experiment prepared** (`Desktop\voice-tests\double-voice\`): D0 the cleaned response exactly as the app speaks it, D1 the raw text with no cleaning, D2 the female voice, D3 one call per sentence, D4 only dashes and brackets fixed, D5 cleaned but the four words with their original spelling (are the respellings the cause?), **D6 the same audio 6 dB quieter, D7 12 dB quieter, D8 the same level in 16-bit format**. If D6/D7 are clean and D0/D8 double, the cause is the full-scale level and the fix is headroom (scale the audio to a peak of about 0.7 before it is returned): a one-line change. If D0, D6 and D7 all double on the same words in a desktop player, the cause is in Bhashini's audio for that text and the options are re-synthesising by sentence or changing the words. If they are clean in a player and doubled only in the app, it is the browser path.
- **PLAN-VOICE-5 result of the user's listening test (2026-10-09), nothing implemented:** D0 (the cleaned male response exactly as the app speaks it) is the best of the nine files; it, D7 (12 dB quieter) and D8 (16-bit) all still have a SUBTLE double voice on a word or two, and the user hears height, roughly, relied and the other words right in them. D1 (raw text), D2 (female voice: "really bad, multiple places"), D3 (one call per sentence), D4 (only dashes fixed) and D5 (original spellings) are all worse. **Conclusions:** (1) the full-scale-level lead is REFUTED (12 dB quieter and a different file format double just the same); (2) the audio is identical on every request, so it is a property of Bhashini's English voice for that text; (3) the user hears no doubling in the other languages, so it is specific to the English model; (4) Bhashini's pipeline config lists exactly ONE English text-to-speech service (`ai4bharat/indic-tts-coqui-misc-gpu--t4`, voices male and female), so there is no other English voice on Bhashini to switch to; (5) the current implementation (male, cleaned, one call) stays. **Open options, none started:** (a) the user names the words that double and each gets a respelling test like "zones" (the audio is deterministic, so a per-word fix holds); (b) a different provider's English voice, to be tested before it is trusted (for example Gemini's speech output if the key allows it; unverified); (c) accept it as a residual. The checklist item VOICE-5 stays open until the user decides.
- **Status (VOICE-5 follow-up and FIX-VOICE-6 tools), 2026-10-09:** the user chose D0 (the current setup; no code change) and option 1 (per-word respellings). **Blocked on data:** the words that double are not known; nothing measured can tell (loudness, period doubling, roughness and flutter do not separate the file the user called doubled from the clean ones, and the doubling is not in the level or the format: D7 and D8 double). So two tools were added, both manual: `scripts/voice_check.py --timeline` prints each word with the second it is spoken at, and `scripts/voice_respell.py WORD --carrier ...` scores spellings of one word in real sentences (the sweep used for height, roughly, relied, gauge, zones), and the word-by-second sheet for the D0 audio was written to `Desktop\voice-tests\double-voice\D0_word_times.txt` so the user can name the doubled words by their time. Live check of `--timeline`: one sentence with the reported words read back with 0 of 28 words different. Tests: `test_voice_check_script.py` 11 (timeline format, spelling scoring, candidates). ### [2026-10-09] FIX-VOICE-7 — a mid-sentence pause in long answers, coordinate letters after a degree sign, "≈", abbreviated months — CLAIMED

- **Reported by the user (2026-10-09), same response as before plus the fisherman's answer:** (1) a pause after "You" in "You can head out now." with no punctuation there; (2) the fisherman answer "...at roughly 9.915° N, 76.074° E and 28-33 m depth. The latest advisory ... issued on 2 Oct 2026 ... wave ≈ 0.66 m, wind ≈ 0.78 m/s" was spoken with "N" and "E" as letters; (3) month names must be complete ("Oct", "Sept" are mispronounced); (4) "meters" vs "metres" pronunciation (direction unclear, asked back); (5) the answer wording per persona needs auditing (a checklist item, not code).
- **Cause of (1), measured:** the English voice cuts its output at about 25 s and restarts after a 0.5-0.8 s gap, wherever it is in the text. The response is 394 characters (26.7 s); the gap is at 24.9 s, inside "You can | head out now". Proof: with the last sentence changed to "Head out now.", "You may head out now.", "You can go out now." the gap stays at 24.8-24.9 s; with only the first two sentences (372 chars, 24.9 s) there is no gap; a text with a short sentence added in front or behind keeps the gap at 24.9-25.0 s; longer texts get gaps at about 26 s and 52 s (the two longest corpus answers: 26.8 s/750 ms; two answers joined: 26.4 s, 52.5 s; three joined: 26.4, 52.5, 62.1, ...). So it is the service's maximum length per call, not the words. **Fix:** `BhashiniTtsBackend` splits text longer than 260 characters (about 18 s) at sentence ends (and, for one long sentence, at a comma or "and") before sending, and joins the pieces with a 0.2 s gap, the length of the voice's own sentence gap (170-320 ms). Answers up to 260 characters (most) are one call, unchanged. **Not known:** whether the double voice also grows near the cut; the user judged D3 (a call per sentence) worse than D0, so the splitting is only for long text and the user is asked to compare.
- **Cause of (2):** the compass-letter rule needs a digit directly before the letter, and the degree sign sits between ("9.915° N"); after "°" became "degrees" the letter was no longer after a digit. Same in the Indian-language branch, and the translator was shown "° N". **(2b)** "≈" is read "approximately equal" (seen in the corpus: "is approximately equal"); now "about". **(3)** abbreviated months next to a day or year are written in full in the speech text ("2 Oct 2026" -> "2 October 2026"), English only (the Indian-language branch already maps Latin months to native names); the ISO-date form now also uses digits for the year ("2 October 2026"), the form the user approved by ear in file 31, instead of the "twenty twenty-six" words I had chosen from a recogniser's output.
- **By:** Claude. **Files:** `agents/voice.py`, `agents/language.py` (the protected term), tests.
- **Status (FIX-VOICE-7): DONE in code 2026-10-09; awaiting the user's ear.** (1) **Pause fixed in the audio, measured:** the Mangalore response through the new path is 394 characters -> 2 pieces (cut at the full stop after "breach."), 27.0 s, no silence over 400 ms (it had 695 ms at 24.9 s); the fisherman answer (442 characters, 3 pieces, 41.3 s) has none. `voice.split_for_bhashini` (limit 260 characters, sentences first, then a comma or "and" for one very long sentence, a word run is never cut) and `_synthesize` (pieces joined with a 0.2 s gap, the male voice, the name splice still in the Hindi voice, other languages split too with their default voice); short answers stay one call. (2) **Coordinates:** "9.915° N, 76.074° E" -> "9.915 degrees north, 76.074 degrees east" in English and in the Indian-language branch; the translator now keeps "9.915° N" whole (`_PROTECTED_TERM`). (3) **Found while testing:** the protection regex did not treat the non-breaking hyphen U+2011 (the character the models actually write, "28‑33 m") as a range separator, so real ranges were still split into two placeholders; it now accepts U+2010-U+2015 and U+2212. (4) **Months:** abbreviations next to a day number are written in full in the speech text (never "Mark", "decide"); a trailing dot is not consumed ("on 3 Sep." keeps its full stop); the ISO-date year is spoken in digits again ("2 October 2026"), the form the user approved by ear in file 31, replacing the "twenty twenty-six" words I had chosen from a recogniser's output. (5) "≈" and "~" before a number -> "about"; "±" -> "plus or minus". (6) `voice_check.py` also reports any silence over 0.45 s. **Tests:** `test_fix_voice_pauses_months.py` (new, 25), the voice, name, placeholder and language suites: 427 pass; the two old year expectations updated. **Not done, needs the user:** "meters" vs "metres" (direction unclear; four listening files in `Desktop\voice-tests\fix-voice-7\`); the double voice (word list). **Checklist:** PERSONA-AUDIT added (A2) with the fisherman answer as evidence; VOICE-7/8/9/10 added; nothing ticked.

### [2026-10-09] FIX-COMPASS-1 — Kannada and Tamil compass points (VOICE-4): one protected token per phrase, "N-N-W" normalised, a sentence-by-sentence fallback — CLAIMED

- **Reported by the user (2026-10-09):** VOICE-4 works in English and Hindi, Marathi, Telugu, Malayalam, Bengali, Gujarati and Odia but not in Kannada and Tamil, where the English translation box shows "N-N-W". **Reproduced live (fresh backend, current code) and measured — and it is a defect I introduced:** when VOICE-4 stopped the translator from touching compass points, "16 km NNW" became TWO adjacent placeholders ("801 802"). The translator treats an adjacent pair of numbers as one number and drops or moves them: Hindi lost the distance AND the direction ("...सूरतकल पॉइंट के पास 803 गहरे पानी में"), Tamil put "14.9 km WSW" into the date ("अक्टोबर 801 802"), Kannada dropped the direction ("801 kilometres 802 of Kochi" -> no direction). The completeness check then retranslated the whole answer WITHOUT protection, which is where the letters ("டபிள்யூ. எஸ். டபிள்யூ") and "N-N-W" come from. So a safety direction was being lost or garbled in a translated answer. **Measured the fix** (`%TEMP%\tok_probe*.py`, nine languages x nine sentences): current masking 265/297 tokens (89%), 45/63 sentences fully intact (71%); one token per phrase 216/225 (96%), 56/63 (89%); plus coordinates pair, time and date merged and compass spellings normalised: **276/279 tokens (98.9%), 78/81 sentences (96%)**; the three failures are one sentence with a parenthesised coordinate pair. **Change (`agents/language.py`):** (1) `normalise_for_translation`: "N-N-W"/"N.N.W." and "west south-west"/"north-north-west" (three words) become the abbreviation, spelled units after a number become symbols ("14.9 kilometres" -> "14.9 km", "28 to 33 meters" -> "28-33 m") so a phrase is one token and the translator does not have native words dropped between tokens; (2) `_PROTECTED_TERM`: a number, its unit and a following compass point is ONE token ("16 km NNW"), a coordinate pair ("9.915° N, 76.074° E") is one token, so are a time ("08:24") and a written date ("2 Oct 2026", "October 2, 2026"); (3) the fallback when a token is lost: translate sentence by sentence (a lost token costs one sentence, not the whole answer). **voice.py:** the letter forms ("N-N-W") are also read as the compass point in English. **By:** Claude. **Files:** `agents/language.py`, `agents/voice.py`, tests.
- **Status (FIX-COMPASS-1): DONE in code 2026-10-09; awaiting the user's ear.** `language.normalise_compass` / `normalise_for_translation` and the one-token-per-phrase `_PROTECTED_TERM` as claimed, plus `_translate_by_sentence` as the fallback; `voice.py` reads the letter forms in English and in the native branch. **Measured:** 276/279 tokens (98.9%) and 78/81 sentences (96%) survive translation in nine languages (was 89% and 71%). **Live, fresh backend, the user's failing cases:** Kannada "answer in kannada: nearest fishing zone near kochi" shows "15 km WSW" and is spoken "15 ಕಿಲೋಮೀಟರ್ ಪಶ್ಚಿಮ ದಕ್ಷಿಣ ಪಶ್ಚಿಮ"; Tamil "pfzs near kochi" shows "15 km WSW" and is spoken "15 கிலோமீட்டர் மேற்கு தெற்கு மேற்கு"; NNW is spoken as the language's north north-west in Kannada, Tamil, Hindi, Gujarati and Odia; 0 "retranslating by sentence" warnings in these seven answers. Tests: `test_fix_placeholder.py` (rewritten expectations, normaliser cases incl. "A-N-W" left alone, the adjacent-number failure, the per-sentence fallback), voice and language suites 590 pass. **A defect found and fixed in the process: a normaliser guard** ("A-N-W" was read as "A-NW"). **Not touched:** the translated sentence still shows Latin "NNW", "WSW", "2 Oct 2026" inside native text (as before).
- **VOICE-7 root cause found, 2026-10-09 (nothing changed):** the "latency" of Gujarati and Odia speech (and Marathi's) is Bhashini's speech service answering `504 Gateway Time-out` for those three languages on every call, for any text, either voice, with or without the preprocessor (60 s with a 90 s limit); Hindi on the same service id answers in 0.8 s; the other seven languages are 1-4 s. Our limit (3 s, then 12 s, one retry) then falls to the local MMS voice after about 25 s; measured through `/voice/speak`: gu 28.5 s, or 29.7 s, mr 76.3 s (model load), all `rung=mms_tts`, and 0.0 s on repeat (cached). The local models for mar/guj/ory are now in the HF cache. Proposal in the checklist (skip Bhashini per language for ten minutes after a failure; a visible marker for the local voice), awaiting the user.

### [2026-10-09] FIX-CONTEXT-1 — the whole chat stays in view: 30 turns kept (was 5); the last 5 carry their answers, older turns carry their question; the models are told when older turns are gone — IMPLEMENTED, waiting for the user's check

- **Why (the user's rule: "previous prompts + responses must be properly taken as context ... it is a chatbot"):** after the "answer the same in kannada" bug, an AUDIT through the real path (real model, agent account, 8 scripted multi-turn chats on port 8001). **Worked:** follow-up chain with "what was my first question" (4 turns), a place correction ("no i meant kozhikode" answered Kozhikode), small talk in the middle of a sea chat then "and tomorrow?", "why is that" / "explain that more simply" / "is that zone far", "what did you just tell me" / "what was my last question", a vessel correction ("i am in a trawler" answered for a trawler), Gujarati -> Kannada -> English -> "and the wave height there?". **Defect found:** in a seven-turn chat "what was my first question in this chat?" was answered about the SECOND question ("the wind near kochi"): `session.MAX_TURNS = 5` silently dropped the first, and nothing told the model anything was missing. **Change:** `session.MAX_TURNS` 5 -> 30 and `RECENT_TURNS = 5`; `understand._build_understand_prompt` and `reporting._describe_recent_turns` show EVERY question and the answers of the last five; a chat at the limit adds "(Only the last 30 turns of this chat are kept; anything earlier is not available.)"; the frontend's reopen-a-chat restore sends up to 30 turns (`chatStore.ts`). **Checked:** 6 new tests (`test_fix_context_window.py`), one old test rewritten to the new rule, unit suite 1811 passed with the same 3 known failures, the failing seven-turn chat now answers "the wave height near Kochi". tsc clean. **Open, found by the same audit, not changed:** (1) a language-only request ("now in english please", "answer the same in kannada") re-runs the WHOLE pipeline, 13-18 s, and the English re-run showed a different verdict banner ("CAUTION: Insufficient data") than the first answer; the P3.13 path (`_language_change_stream`) re-renders the stored answer with the same facts, but it only fires on a fixed phrase list (`language_command.py`), which is what the founding principle forbids; the fix is to let the model's reading choose the re-render path, one mechanism, not two; (2) `carry_intent` reads only the immediately previous turn. **By:** Claude. **Files:** `session.py`, `agents/understand.py`, `agents/reporting.py`, `frontend/app/ask/chatStore.ts`, tests.

### [2026-10-09] CONTEXT-2 — "answer the same in kannada" / "now in english please" translates the SAME earlier answer (7 s, was 18 s; same facts, same verdict card) — IMPLEMENTED, waiting for the user's check

- **Design (the user's principle: the model reads, code validates):** the planner's reading gained one field, `language_only` (true only when the message asks for nothing but the earlier answer in another language; prompt rule 18). `planning_node` checks it against the real history (a follow-up, a reply language, no new place, no new time, an earlier answered turn whose finished answer is held) and, only then, builds the answer from `api/language_rerender.rerender_last_answer`, which translates the stored English answer the way `language_egress` does (placeholders, product name) and keeps the verdict card, citations and what the chat remembers (place, routing rows, English query, vessel, verdict), so the next follow-up ("and the wave height there?") still has them; the graph then ends after planning (no specialist runs). **Session:** `turn_from_final` stores the whole finished frame as `frame`; only the LAST turn keeps it (`replace_turns`). `turn_from_final` also reads a re-rendered frame's verdict. **If anything is missing** (no frame, translation fails, the model says it asks more) the normal pipeline runs, so a request is never dropped. The old fixed-phrase path (P3.13, "speak to me in Telugu") uses the same builder and may still fall back to re-writing from the trace. **First attempt rejected:** re-rendering through `trace_routes.render_query` (the P3.13 mechanism) took 19-40 s and produced a different answer (a generic wave/wind summary instead of the fishing zone), so CONTEXT-2 never uses it. **Checked through the real path (agent account, port 8001, real model):** Gujarati PFZ answer -> "okay fine answer the same in kannada" (7 s, the same Mangrol zone in Kannada) -> "now in english please" (4 s, same zone) -> "and the wave height there?" (answered for Mangrol, place carried) -> "in hindi please, and what about tomorrow?" (more than a language: the full pipeline ran). 11 tests (`test_context2_language_rerender.py`), unit suite 1833 passed, the same 3 known failures. **Not run:** frontend, the 4 guards, response-guarantee, messy-prompts. **Honest limits:** a chat restored from history after a restart has no frame, so that first language request runs the full pipeline; the 7 s is the planner's model call plus translation. **By:** Claude.

### [2026-10-09] PC5.6 — verify romanized distress and the gazetteer — RUN; ONE SAFETY NOTE and ONE PLACE NOTE, each to be fixed as its own point (the plan forbids fixing a distress gap inside PC5.6)

- **Live, through /query (real model, agent account), the plan's cases.** **(a) distress, 6 phrases:** all 6 came back `distress_flag=true`, outcome DISTRESS. **BUT** the deterministic phrase check (no model) catches only 3 of the 6 (`engine nindruduchu udhavi venum`, `mera boat dub raha hai bachao`, `padagu moozhgudhu udhavi venum`); it MISSES `naav ka engine kharab ho gaya, madad chahiye`, `meri naav ka engine kharab ho gaya hai pamban ke paas madad chahiye` and `nanna boat engine halaaytu malpe hatra sahaya beku`. The live responses for those three took 4-5 s (the others 0 s), so they were raised later, by the planner's model reading (`planned_distress`, D-1), not before ingress as the plan requires, and only while a model is reachable. **NOTE-2 (SAFETY):** with no model reachable those three would be answered as ordinary prompts. The founding rule allows the phrase list to raise the alarm and forbids a list miss from ruling distress out; the model is the second line, which is what happened; but the plan says the check must raise romanized phrases before ingress. Fix as its own point (extend `_ROMANIZED_DISTRESS_PATTERNS`, today only "bachao"; the Hindi/Kannada/Tamil romanized phrases need a native reader per the module's own note). **(b) places, 16 prompts:** 11 resolved to the right place (Rameswaram x2, Kochi, Mangalore, Chennai, Vizag, Udupi, Mangaluru and the Rameswaram Tamil prompt); "aaj goa me hawa ki raftaar kitni hai" and "karwar inda goa varege ..." gave NEEDS_PLACE ("Goa is a whole coastline, which of these ports?"), which is the designed answer; 5 prompts that name no place (naalai kadalukku pogalama, ivattu samudradalli gaali vega eshtu, ee vaara samudra tumba alegalu ide ya, repu samudram ki vellochha, and the English controls "is it safe to go to sea tomorrow" / "wind speed today") were answered at the pilot default (Gulf of Mannar) WITH the disclosure "This question names no place ... not your position", the same as English; "tum kaiso ho" = OUT_OF_SCOPE (small talk). **NOTE-1 (place):** "mujhe tuticorin se pamban tak sabse surakshit raasta batao" read as two places, status `ambiguous`, yet was ANSWERED at the pilot default position (with the disclosure "derived from the pilot default position"), and the answer text says "For your route from Tuticorin to Pamban ... calm and safe"; the English "safest route from tuticorin to pamban" resolves as a passage at Tuticorin. A route answered from a position that is neither end is misleading. Not fixed here. **Not covered:** a native-script mixed case, voice. **Tests kept:** `tests/unit/test_pc5_6_romanized.py` (3 distress phrases pass, 3 are strict xfails for NOTE-2, the route case is a strict xfail for NOTE-1, the distress check precedes ingress in the graph). **By:** Claude.

### [2026-10-09] NOTE-PLACE-1 and NOTE-VOICE-1 — root causes found for the user's "kundapura" and "m/s in Kannada" reports; NOTHING CHANGED YET (waiting for the user's go)

- **Replayed through /query (agent account, real model):** "pfzs near ktaka" (asked which port), "near udupi" (answered), "oh sorry i meant kundapura" -> answered at the PILOT DEFAULT (8.8 N 78.3 E, Thoothukudi) with the Tamil Nadu sector, "wait didnt you just list it? ... kundapur" -> answered at Kundapur, "give me the SST and chlorophyll of kundapura in kannada" -> "I do not have SST data for Kundapur", the Thoothukudi values. **Root cause 1 (position has two readers that disagree):** `api/main.py` resolves the POSITION from the raw text with the word list (`resolve_or_ask`) before the graph runs; "kundapura" is not an exact gazetteer name, so it becomes status `ambiguous` (a near-miss "did you mean Kundapur?"), whose position is the pilot default. The planner's model reads the same word correctly (`places=[{raw: kundapura, normalized: Kundapur}]`) and `validate_reading` accepts "Kundapur" (resolved), so planning does NOT stop; the text's "ambiguous" is only enforced when the model returned NO places, so the agents run at the default position while the card talks about Kundapura. Nothing sets the position from the model's validated place. With the exact spelling ("kundapur") the word list resolves it, which is why the third message worked. **Root cause 2 (chat replies guess about the system's own behaviour):** "You are entirely right ... I should have recognized that", and "I didn't have a clear, confirmed location earlier" are written by the chat model, which is given the capability facts but NOT what the system actually did last turn (the place status, the disclosure, the position used), so it agrees with the user and invents a reason. **Generalisation:** it hits every place whose spelling is not the gazetteer's exact name and that the planner can still read (Kundapura, Cochin/Kochi, Mangalore/Mangaluru, Tuticorin/Thoothukudi, any typo or romanisation). **Proposed fix:** the validated model reading sets the position (one source of truth, still checked against the gazetteer: the model proposes, code checks), a disclosure says "read 'kundapura' as Kundapur", an exact-or-validated place never reaches the default, and the reply model is handed the previous turn's real place resolution. **Root cause (voice, m/s):** `speakable` turns "2 m/s" into the Kannada template "ಸೆಕೆಂಡಿಗೆ 2 ಮೀಟರ್" (per-second, 2, metres); read back through Bhashini ASR the audio says exactly "ಗಾಳಿ ಸೆಕೆಂಡಿಗೆ 2 ಮೀ.", so the words reach the voice as written; the Kannada unit template is the lexicon entry nobody native-confirmed (VOICE-9 covers the lexicon; Kannada was confirmed only for words/months/compass). The user hears it as wrong, so the template (word order) is the thing to change; I cannot judge Kannada by ear. Candidates to listen to: "2 ಮೀಟರ್ ಪ್ರತಿ ಸೆಕೆಂಡ್", "ಪ್ರತಿ ಸೆಕೆಂಡಿಗೆ 2 ಮೀಟರ್", "ಎರಡು ಮೀಟರ್ ಪ್ರತಿ ಸೆಕೆಂಡ್". **By:** Claude.

### [2026-10-09] FIX-PLACE-1 — the model's gazetteer-checked place sets the position; the word list is the fast path and the fallback; the chat model is told what really happened — IMPLEMENTED, waiting for the user's check

- **Fixes NOTE-PLACE-1 (root cause 1 and 2).** **Change:** `place_resolution.adopt_model_place` (called from `planning_node`): when the planner's reading names exactly ONE place, the words it says it read are in the message (it cannot invent a place), the gazetteer confirms it, and the position so far is not one the caller chose or the text resolved exactly (`regional_default`, `session_carried`, `home_port`, `gps_fix` are replaced), THAT place sets `user_location` and `place_resolution`, and the answer carries "Read “kundapura” as Kundapur." `resolve_confident` (used there and in `validate_reading`) accepts ONE close (>= 0.8) near-miss of a single non-region place, because the model's wording varies run to run (first replay it wrote "Kundapura", not "Kundapur", and the old exact check asked "did you mean Kundapur?"); a whole coastline (Goa, Karnataka, Gujarat), several candidates, an unknown name and an inland place still ask. **Chat replies:** a stored turn now records the place status; `_describe_recent_turns` says "(no place was resolved for it, so it was answered at the pilot default position)" for such a turn; the chat prompt says to state only what the conversation records, not to guess a reason or apologise for an unrecorded fault. **Checked through the real path (agent account, port 8001, real model):** the user's 5-message Kundapur conversation, THREE runs: "oh sorry i meant kundapura", "...kundapur right" and "SST and chlorophyll of kundapura in kannada" all resolved at Kundapur, 3/3 (before: Thoothukudi, Thoothukudi, "no data"). 12-prompt spelling sweep: Kundapura, Cochin, Tuticorine, Mangalore, Udupii, Vishakhapatnam, Pondicherry, Thoothukkudi, Rameshwaram, Karwr all resolved to the right place (the changed spellings with a "Read X as Y" note); "gujurat" and "atlantis" still ask. 36 new tests (`test_fix_place_1_model_place.py`); unit suite 1862+ passed, the same 3 known failures; ruff, mypy, pyrefly clean, the 4 CI guards green. **Not run:** frontend (untouched), response-guarantee, messy-prompts. **Honest limits:** (1) the chat reply's apology/"I didn't have a clear location" text is prompt-guided, not guaranteed; (2) the model still decides what it read, so a place it fails to extract at all is still the word list's call; (3) the 0.8 threshold is a judgement, and the "Read X as Y" line is how a wrong guess becomes visible; (4) NOTE-PLACE-2.. (the rest of the user's pasted prompts) and PC5.6 notes are next. **By:** Claude.

### [2026-10-09] NOTE-CHL-1 — "no chlorophyll data" for Kundapur: the data EXISTS, the answer path never reads it at a place — FOUND, NOT CHANGED
- **Data on disk, read through the real loaders at Kundapur (13.63 N 74.62 E):** EOS-06 OCM-3 chlorophyll 0.075 mg/m3 (25 km, 30 Sept), CMEMS chlorophyll 10.58 mg/m3 (4 km, 30 Sept, the nearest cell is coastal), INSAT-3DR SST 26.81 C (30 Sept), CMEMS SST 29.71 C (1 Oct). **So we do have chlorophyll and SST grids. Two findings:** (1) **No path gives a value at the user's place.** `ocean_analytics` only computes a Pearson correlation of SST against chlorophyll over `state["target_bbox"]`, a field nothing in the app ever sets (it is only read), and returns neither number; the reply for "SST and chlorophyll at Kundapur" therefore has no reading to quote, and the model said "not tracked in our live telemetry" (wrong: it is tracked, not read). The SST it quoted in an earlier replay (29.8 C "at the Thoothukudi offshore location") came from a different source and a different place. (2) **The sources disagree a lot** at this coastal cell: chlorophyll 0.075 vs 10.58 (a 140x gap: 25 km open-ocean product against a 4 km coastal-aware product) and SST 26.8 vs 29.7 C (3 C). Both are 9-10 days old on 9 Oct. Quoting either as "the" reading without the source, its date and the disagreement would be a confident answer from a thin basis; the reading needs the cascade, the date and a disagreement statement, like wave and wind do. **Proposed (not built):** `ocean_analytics` returns point readings at the resolved position (nearest cell, its distance, source, date, both sources when they disagree), the narrative quotes them with their age, and it says plainly when the nearest cell is more than a set distance away or the data is old. **By:** Claude.

### [2026-10-10] NOTE-CHL-2 — the "sources disagree" statement is a systematic offset between two different products, not conflicting evidence — MEASURED, NOT CHANGED (waiting for the user's decision)
- **Measured over ~45 gazetteer ports (coast cell) and 45 points ~0.8 deg offshore, today's grids:** SST INSAT-3DR minus CMEMS: coast mean -1.59 C (median -1.27, sd 1.05, only 11/45 within 1 C), offshore mean -0.83 C (median -0.53, 29/45 within 1 C). Chlorophyll CMEMS / EOS-06: coast median 27.7x (1 of 45 within 3x), offshore median 7.8x (11/45 within 3x). **So the gap is consistent in sign and size, not random:** INSAT reads cooler than CMEMS everywhere, more so at the coast (INSAT is a clear-sky satellite skin product on ~4 km pixels, CMEMS is a model analysis of the near-surface bulk temperature on a 1/12 deg grid, one day apart); the 25 km EOS-06 chlorophyll is far lower than the 4 km CMEMS ocean-colour product, most of all within 25 km of the coast (coastal turbid water makes ocean-colour chlorophyll read high; a 25 km cell averages coast and open sea). **My `point_readings` flag (SST > 1.5 C or chlorophyll > 3x apart) therefore fires at nearly every coastal place and tells the reader nothing; the answer's "the sources disagree" is wrong framing.** Options for the user's decision are in the chat. **By:** Claude.

### [2026-10-10] NOTE-CHL-2 — SST and chlorophyll: ONE headline reading per quantity; "the sources disagree" only for a gap outside the measured normal offset — IMPLEMENTED, waiting for the user's check (live prompt effect NOT verified, see limits)
- **User's decision:** go with the recommendation; never say contradiction / sources disagree unless one really exists. **Change:** `ocean_analytics.point_readings` returns, per quantity, `headline` (SST: INSAT-3DR; chlorophyll: EOS-06; the next source with a cell if the preferred one has none), `cross_checks` (CMEMS, the INCOIS model SST), and `unusual_gap` set ONLY when a cross-check is outside the normal offset: SST more than 3 C apart (normal measured: INSAT 0.8 C cooler offshore, 1.6 C at the coast, sd ~1), chlorophyll CMEMS below a third of EOS-06 or more than 200x (normal measured: 8x offshore, 28x at the coast, p90 117x). Chlorophyll also gets a `level` (below 0.2 mg/m3 low, above 1.0 high, between moderate; a judgement for Indian waters, change in `_CHL_LOW/_CHL_HIGH`) and `near_shore_indicative` (within 30 km of a gazetteer port, the gazetteer standing in for a coastline). The deterministic lines give the headline with source, date and age, the level, and "Close to the coast the satellite chlorophyll is only indicative"; they never say "disagree". The narrative prompt rule 9a now says: give the headline, do not quote a cross-check unless `unusual_gap` is set, do not say the sources disagree/conflict/contradict, never use the words "headline" or "cross-check". The critic's facts list the headline, the level and the cross-checks marked "a normal offset, not a contradiction". **Checked:** 31 tests (`test_note_chl_1_point_readings.py`: headline choice and fallback, normal offsets are NOT flagged (4 SST and 4 chlorophyll pairs including the user's Udupi values 26.5/29.38 and 0.08/1.97), unusual gaps are, level classes, near-shore flag, no "disagree/conflict/contradict" in the deterministic text, prompt wording, critic facts, completion from data); unit suite 1922 passed with the same 3 known failures; ruff, mypy, pyrefly clean, 4 CI guards green. **Live (real model, port 8001), before the last prompt tightening:** Udupi gave "26.5 C (INSAT-3DR, 30 September, 10 days ago) ... chlorophyll 0.08 mg/m3 (low level ...), noting that near the coast satellite chlorophyll is only indicative" with NO "disagree", but it still quoted the CMEMS value as a "cross-check" and used the word "cross-check". A follow-up after a real answer is fine ("pfzs near ktaka", "near udupi", "more detail please" carried Udupi). **Cause:** the earlier turn ended in a place question and recorded no place; the follow-up names none, the planner reads it as a sea question, and the "names no place" rule answers at the pilot default (a rule meant for a first, standalone question). **The right behaviour:** a follow-up that asks for more detail after a place question is about THAT question (rule 14b: chat_followup), or re-asks which place; it must not run the sea agents at a position nobody gave. **Proposed fix (not built):** record `place_status` ambiguous/unresolvable on the turn (done since FIX-PLACE-1) and, when the previous turn was a place question and this one names no place, answer conversationally and repeat the options (chat reply with the real status, no agent run); validated by code against the history, the model proposes `chat_followup`. **PC5.6-NOTE-1 cause:** `place_resolution._PASSAGE_SHAPE` is the regex `from ... to | between ... and` (English grammar). "tuticorin se pamban tak" (Hindi), "mangalore la irundhu goa varaikkum" (Tamil), "karwar inda goa varege" (Kannada) and even "tuticorin to pamban" have two places and no "from", so the word list says `ambiguous` (no position -> pilot default) while the model read both places; `adopt_model_place` (FIX-PLACE-1) handles exactly ONE place. **Proposed fix (not built):** the planner already returns the places in order and a ROUTE intent; when it returns two gazetteer-validated places and the intent is a route, treat it as a passage: origin = the first, the same disclosure as the English case ("this is a passage ... the conditions are for the origin"). The English regex stays as the fallback. **By:** Claude.

### [2026-10-10] D-15 and PC5.6-NOTE-1 — IMPLEMENTED, waiting for the user's check (checked live)
- **D-15 (`graph.planning_node`, `session.turn_from_final`):** a turn that ended in a place question records `place_question` (the fixed question text, with its options). When the NEXT message is a sea question that names no place and the position is a guess (`regional_default` or `session_carried`), the question is asked again (`_refusal("NEEDS_PLACE", ...)`, the model words it, the execution plan and routing rows are cleared so no agent runs); a place the user names is answered normally; a chosen position (explicit/coordinates/chip) is never overridden; a greeting or chat kind is left to the chat path; after an answered turn nothing is pending. **PC5.6-NOTE-1 (`place_resolution.adopt_model_passage`):** two places the model read, both in the message and both confirmed by the gazetteer, with a ROUTE intent, are a passage answered for the ORIGIN (the place named first in the message), with the same "This is a passage ... conditions are for <origin>" sentence the English regex path gives; a whole coastline, an unknown name, the same place twice, three places, a non-route intent, a chosen position or an exactly resolved text are not adopted. **Live (agent account, port 8001, real model):** "pfzs near gujarat" then "can you explain that in more detail" -> asks which port again (was: answered at the Gulf of Mannar default); then "near porbandar" -> answered at Porbandar; "mujhe tuticorin se pamban tak sabse surakshit raasta batao" -> answered at Tuticorin with the passage note (was: the default); Kannada "karwar inda udupi varege ..." -> answered at Karwar; the English control unchanged; Tamil "mangalore la irundhu goa varaikkum ..." asks which port because Goa is a whole coastline (by design). "safe near atlantis" then "tell me more" re-asks (the first turn's own wording is "This question names no place ... Gulf of Mannar", odd for an unknown name: a separate wording defect, not fixed). 24 tests (`test_d15_note1_place_followups.py`), the superseded xfail in `test_pc5_6_romanized.py` retired. **Limits:** the original "alibaug" example no longer fails (Alibag is in the gazetteer); the pending question is only the LAST turn's; with no model reachable the word list decides and a passage is not recognised (the English regex still is). **By:** Claude.

### [2026-10-10] Critic false positives (the IMBL item) — probed live; NOT reproduced; the real class identified; NOTHING CHANGED (waiting for the user)
- **Probe (agent account, port 8001, real model):** "how far is the maritime boundary from tuticorin" (48 nm), "am i close to the IMBL near rameswaram" (13 nm): the critic passed both, nothing deleted. "is it safe near pamban and how close is the boundary": the narrator wrote "Wave height is calm at 0.24 meters"; the critic flagged it against the measured 0.48 m and corrected it while KEEPING the boundary sentence (18.85 nm) — the critic doing its job. So the recorded "critic deletes the IMBL distance" does not reproduce today. **The class that does repeat:** the critic judges the narrative against `critic.build_facts_block`, a hand-written list of fields; anything the narrator is allowed to say that is not in that list reads as "not in the measured facts" and its reviser deletes it. Seen twice: the original IMBL case (the docstring records that the list once held only the boundary distance and the verdict) and 2026-10-09, when the SST and chlorophyll answers were deleted because the list lacked them (fixed for those two fields only). **Proposed fix (not built):** build the critic's facts from the same `narration_view` of the same agent results the narrator is given, so the two cannot diverge, plus a parity test (every figure the narrator's facts hold appears in the critic's); keep the hand-written extras only for derived values. **By:** Claude.

- **Found:** (11) the satellite grids were "live source, validated on fetch" though they are files; (12) chlorophyll had a decision only for the DIAGNOSTIC row; (13) ocean picked the headline by a constant. **Built:** `discovery._grid_probe` for `mosdac_open_sst`, `mosdac_open_chl`, `copernicus_cmems` (dtype from the new `ctx["data_type"]`): ok when the source has a cell within 60 km of the position, with its distance, observed date and age, stale from 3 days (RECENCY_BANDS), a missing cell is a coverage gap (`systemic=False`, no breaker); a probe may return None = "unchecked, validated on fetch" for data types it has nothing to say about; `discovery.source_report_entry` (shared by weather, now refactored onto it, and ocean). `ocean_analytics`: `grid_reading`, `_ID_BY_NAME`, readings carry `source_id`, `point_readings(lat, lon, decided)` takes the headline from Agent 3's decision (a decided source with no cell keeps the default headline), the NOAA CoastWatch last rung is read ONLY when Agent 3 decided it (a network call, never speculative), `source_report` {tide, pfz, sst, chlorophyll}: decided / used / obeyed. `graph.marine_data_discovery_run`: sst + chlorophyll are settled whenever `_asks_sea_colour`. **Live (agent account, port 8001, real model):** "give me the sst and chlorophyll values for udupi right now": Agent 3 -> `mosdac_open_sst` "INSAT-3DR SST: cell 1.5 km away, observed 2026-10-09, 1.5 d old", `mosdac_open_chl` "EOS-06 OCM-3 chlorophyll: cell 13.6 km away", PFZ "1145 advisory node(s); newest valid for 2026-10-10, 0 day(s) old, current"; ocean decided = used = obeyed for sst, chlorophyll, pfz, tide; the answer quotes INSAT 28.44 C and EOS-06 0.09 mg/m3 (low); "nearest fishing zone near malpe": PFZ decided = used. 18 tests (`test_discovery_colour_pfz_binding.py`); CI gate: ruff (backend, root), mypy, pyrefly, 4 guards, response guarantee 49, messy prompts 77 + 7 xfailed, unit suite 2082 passed, the 2 known friend failures. **Not done:** the regional SST-vs-chlorophyll correlation (`_sst_grid`/`_chl_grid`, freshest-wins) still picks its own grids (it is a regional statistic, not the answer's reading); catch_statistics, cyclone and the IMD nowcast are not in the reports; A6 next. **By:** Claude.

### [2026-10-10] AUDIT-4 A6 — decided vs used is on the trace data: per-specialist `source_report`, a run-level `source_check`, and Agent 3's `trace_line` — IMPLEMENTED, waiting for the user's check (checked live)
- **Built (`discovery.py`, `graph.py`, `api/main.py`):** `source_report_entry` takes a list for `used`; `source_check(reports)` -> `{checked, obeyed, mismatches, unknown, line}` (a difference is listed, logged at WARNING and said; no decision = `unknown`, never obeyed); `discovery_trace_line(selections, unusable)` builds one plain sentence from the real decisions; `marine_data_discovery_run` outputs `trace_line`; `geospatial_run` outputs `source_report` (boundary: used marineregions_eez + unep_wcmc_wdpa; eez, mpa, fishing_ban when decided); `final_response.source_check` aggregates weather, ocean and geospatial (`_source_check_for`). **Live (agent account, port 8001, real model):** "is it safe near kochi tomorrow morning and what is the tide": 6 of 6 obeyed, no mismatches, step line "Chose data sources: wave height and wind speed from Open-Meteo Marine API / ECMWF WAM Blend; boundaries from ...; lightning from Open-Meteo lightning_potential (CAPE-derived proxy for IMD Damini); ...; tides from Survey of India 2026 Annual Tide Tables; ..."; "tide times at mangalore please": "tides from Stormglass.io Marine API ... (fell back: no Survey of India rows for station NMP)", 6 of 6; "sst and chlorophyll at karwar": 8 of 8. 16 tests (`test_discovery_decided_vs_used_report.py`); CI gate: ruff (backend, root), mypy, pyrefly, 4 guards, response guarantee 49, messy prompts 77 + 7 xfailed, unit suite 2098 passed, the 2 known friend failures. **Honest limits:** (1) the line names every data type settled for the plan (7-9 sources for a simple question): the trace UI will need a short headline with the full line behind a click; (2) cyclone (SACHET), the IMD nowcast, catch statistics and the regional correlation are still not in any `source_report`, so `source_check` does not cover them; (3) a live primary-source failure has not been exercised end to end (weather's skip path and the cascades are unit-tested). **Audit 4 (marine_data_discovery) is complete in code (A1-A6).** **By:** Claude.

### [2026-10-10] AUDIT-4 A5 and A6 — VERIFIED by the user, ticked on the user's instruction. Audit 4 closed. Next: the parallel specialists one by one, geospatial first (audit 6).

### [2026-10-10] AGENT-AUDIT 6 (geospatial): written (defects G1-G8 identified; G1 and G2 fixed in code)
- **What it does / routing:** in `docs/ORCA_Agent_Trace_and_Audit.md` section 5b (distance to the nearest treaty line, one MPA check, the seasonal fishing ban; readers: risk_assessment, reporting, critic, api `hazard_breakdown`, the SMS/WhatsApp renderer). **Defects, each measured on the real data:** G1 the MPA check covers only the Gulf of Mannar Marine National Park (2 of 11 usable MPAs flagged when a vessel is inside each); G2 the fishing-ban 12 NM carve-out uses the distance to the nearest EEZ edge, which near the Sri Lanka line is the IMBL, not the coast (20 of 84 sample sea points 20-34 nm from land treated as inside the carve-out, so the ban goes undisclosed there, inside the ban windows); G3 two band tables disagree (label CAUTION <= 5 nm, verdict CAUTION <= 3 nm); G4 the SMS/WhatsApp renderer tests "SAFE" but geospatial emits "CLEAR" (every GO summary says "IMBL boundary clear" is the hazard; confirmed by calling it); G5 the IMBL number cites the polygon files' vintage (2026-08-30) not the treaty-lines file's (2026-09-16), and Agent 3's "boundary" decision names the MPA file; G6 ZONES_TO_AVOID gets no zone list (`spatial_query_zones` unwired) and the bearing / treaty are dropped; G7 depth / shallow hazard is not in the agent's output; G8 confidence and coverage are constants. **Needs the user:** G1 (which MPAs are hard NO_GO, which a disclosure; some are Sri Lankan, some lakes or reserve forests where fishing is permitted with a permit); G3 (which band edge); G7 (scope). **By:** Claude.
- **G1 fixed (2026-10-10):** All 11 geofence-usable MPAs detected via `point_in_polygon`. Gulf of Mannar NP = NO_GO (`mpa_violation=True`); remaining 10 MPAs = `mpa_regulatory` disclosure. 25 unit tests (`test_g1_mpa_all_parks.py`) pass.
- **G2 fixed (2026-10-10):** `distance_to_shore_nm` added in `geospatial.py` measuring geodesic distance to the Indian coast (`_district_index` / Census 2011 district polygons) and straight baselines. `fishing_ban_status` measures to Indian shore rather than nearest EEZ edge. 6 unit tests (`test_g2_fishing_ban_shore_distance.py`) pass; 52 tests green across G1, G2 and `test_geospatial.py`.
- **G3 fixed (2026-10-10):** `_PROXIMITY_BANDS` in `geospatial.py` updated from 5.0 NM to 3.0 NM for CAUTION, synchronizing the label with `risk_assessment`'s safety verdict threshold (`imbl_distance_nm <= 3.0` -> CAUTION, `> 3.0` -> GO). Eliminates contradiction at 4 NM where geospatial previously reported CAUTION while safety verdict was GO. 11 unit tests (`test_g3_boundary_band_agreement.py`) pass; 63 tests green across G1, G2, G3 and `test_geospatial.py`.
- **G4 fixed (2026-10-10):** In `orca/channels/renderers.py`, `_verdict_and_hazard()` previously tested `hazards.get("imbl_alert_level") not in (None, "SAFE")`. Because `geospatial` emits `"CLEAR"`, every `GO` summary with a clear boundary reported `hazard = "IMBL boundary clear"` instead of `"no active hazard"`. Updated condition to check `str(hazards.get("imbl_alert_level") or "").upper() not in ("", "SAFE", "CLEAR")`. Verified across all channels (SMS, IVR, WhatsApp, USSD, VHF, Harbour Board). 17 unit tests in `test_g4_channel_renderer_clear_boundary.py`; 88 unit tests green across G1-G4, `test_geospatial.py`, and `test_channel_renderers.py`.
